## 62. ASPIRE header block finalized (baseline‑locked to IDEA) + hero polish batch (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer, PUBLISHED

Follow‑up session, all native Designer, rapid user‑iterated. Resolves §60's open rect8‑centering question — the user redirected to a simpler, different target instead of answering it directly.

**A. Header — title baseline‑locked to "IDEA" (not "Factory"), underline tightened, column gap widened, title recolored.** User: tighten `program-page-aspire-hdrprog-bar`'s gap under the title, widen the gap between the name‑column and the glyph‑column to match the gap between the logo's divider line and the name column, recolor the title `#454545`, and (after an initial pass aligned to "Factory") **explicitly corrected: align the title's baseline to "IDEA," not "Factory."**
- `program-page-aspire-hdrprog-bar` `margin-top` 8px→**3px**.
- `program-page-aspire-hdrprog` `grid-column-gap` 20px→**24px** — measured‑matched to `if-id-tagline`'s `padding-left:24px` (the gap between `if-logo-link`'s `border-right` divider and the tagline content), so both gaps in the header row are now visually identical.
- `program-page-aspire-hdrprog-title` `color` `#1a1a1a`→**`#454545`**.
- **Baseline lock method (reusable):** measured the logo SVG's "I" path (0‑indexed path 0, the first letter of "Idea") bbox‑bottom as the target baseline via a real headless render (no descender in "Idea," so bbox‑bottom = true baseline); measured the title's own baseline via a zero‑size `vertical-align:baseline` probe span (§31G/57 technique); computed the exact `top` offset needed and applied it as `position:relative;top:3.28px` on `program-page-aspire-hdrprog-name` (shifts ONLY the name column — title+bar — leaving the badge/glyph column's flow position untouched). **⚠️ First pass used the WRONG reference (`path[4]`, the "F" of "Factory," `top:19.58px`) — the user caught this immediately ("DID I NOT EXPLICITLY TELL YOU... IDEA") and it was corrected to `path[0]` ("I" of "Idea") in the same session.** Verified to 0.01px against the real published compiled CSS both times (the method itself was sound; the mistake was which path index to target).
- **Badge (glyph+label) also baseline‑locked to Idea, as one rigid unit.** User: "leaving 'blueprint' [the label] where it is, tighten the logo to it vertically so the baseline of the first row of mini‑squares... is the same as the baseline of 'Idea.'" **Geometric conflict found:** the glyph's cropped SVG (`viewBox 26 26 68 68`) has its top‑row bottom at 44.12% of its own height (30/68 units — confirmed by inspecting the actual `<rect>` y‑coordinates in the uploaded asset, not assumed); given the label's ORIGINAL fixed position (top=55 relative to band top) sits only ~1.5px below the target baseline (53.51), and the glyph's bottom row extends a further ~56% of its own height BELOW the top‑row‑bottom point, satisfying "top‑row‑bottom = Idea baseline" while leaving the label pixel‑locked is only possible with an ~2.7px‑tall glyph — not viable. **Resolution:** shifted the WHOLE badge (`program-page-aspire-hdrprog-badge`, glyph+gap+label together) down as one rigid unit via `position:relative;top:21.28px`, preserving the label's position *relative to the glyph* (same internal 6px gap, unchanged) while letting the absolute position move. Verified: glyph top‑row‑bottom lands at 87.50 (vs. Idea's own baseline 87.51 in absolute page coordinates) — confirmed via an annotated screenshot with measured guide lines (Idea baseline / Factory baseline / glyph top‑row‑bottom all drawn as overlays) sent to the user as evidence after they (rightly, given the earlier Factory mistake) questioned whether this one was also wrong — it wasn't; the guide lines showed the red (glyph) and green (Idea) lines coinciding, 16px above the blue (Factory) line.
- **Reusable lesson:** when a "move X to align with Y" request has an internal geometric conflict with a DIFFERENT stated constraint ("leave Z where it is"), the resolution is usually to preserve the RELATIONSHIP between the moved piece and the fixed-in-spirit piece (here: label stays directly under glyph with its original small gap) rather than the exact absolute pixel position — flag the conflict/trade‑off if there's time, but don't let a literal reading force an absurd result (a 2.7px glyph).

**B. Hero — headline‑to‑subtitle gap tightened, without touching the shared `if-hero-h1` class.** User: "Move the yellow text [gold eyebrow subtitle] up closer under ASPIRE." Root cause: `if-hero-h1`'s own `margin-bottom:max(0.34em,30px)` (a sitewide floor tuned for CTA‑button spacing elsewhere) plus `program-page-aspire-hero-content`'s flex `grid-row-gap:clamp(16px,2vw,24px)` stacked additively into a 59px gap. **Fix:** new scoped combo `program-page-aspire-hero-h1-tight` (parent `if-hero-h1`, `margin-bottom:0`) applied ONLY to the ASPIRE H1 (alongside `if-hero-h1 if-hero-inner`) — zeroes the H1's own margin so the gap becomes exactly the flex container's row‑gap (16–24px, fully fluid/responsive already, matching every other gap in the column). **Did not touch the shared `if-hero-h1` class** (used by Home/Students/Faculty/About) — the standing reuse‑first practice from §61 still applies to NOT touching shared classes when only one page needs a different value; a scoped override combo is the correct tool.
- **⚠️ Second, unrelated Georgia‑on‑black violation caught by the user.** The hero body paragraph ("Get paid to do real engineering research... ASPIRE scholarships fund...") reused `if-mani-p` (§61, per the reuse‑first practice) — but `if-mani-p` is the sitewide **Georgia serif body‑copy** class, meant for **black‑on‑white** contexts (Premise, stage bodies, etc.), and the hero panel is white‑text‑on‑black. This is exactly the violation the site already has a rule against (§32‑E: "small white‑on‑dark text = Interstate, NOT Georgia — reserve small Georgia for black‑on‑white only," previously applied to `cta-fine`). **Fix:** added `font-family:"Interstate","Helvetica Neue",Arial,sans-serif` to the existing combo `program-page-aspire-hero-body` (parent `if-mani-p`, already used for the white text‑color override) — overrides Georgia for this ONE instance without touching the shared `if-mani-p` class used elsewhere. **Lesson: reusing an existing class for its SPACING/SIZING doesn't mean its FONT-FAMILY is automatically appropriate for a new context — check the reused class's font against the black‑vs‑white rule (§32‑E) every time, not just its layout properties.**
- **Stat boxes made equal‑fill, matching the "How To Apply" button's width, with tightened/equalized gaps.** User (Mondrian‑logo‑inspired): make the two stat boxes' combined width equal the button's width below, keep their existing left/right padding, and tighten+equalize the gap between the boxes (horizontal) and the gap down to the button (vertical). Root cause: `program-page-aspire-stat` was `flex:0 0 auto` (content‑sized) inside a container that itself stretches to the full hero‑content width (the button below is `if-btn-primary`, `inline-flex` but stretched full‑width by the parent flex column's default `align-items:stretch`) — so the boxes only used their own content width, leaving dead space to the right that the button (stretched) didn't have. **Fix (the exact HARD RULE #2 "equal‑fill siblings" pattern):** `program-page-aspire-stat` `flex:0 0 auto`→**`flex:1 1 0%`** + `min-width:0` — both boxes now equally fill the row, combined width == button width, padding (18px/30px) unchanged. `program-page-aspire-stats` `grid-column-gap`/`grid-row-gap` 16px→12px→**8px** (iterated tighter per follow‑up "tighten the gaps even more"). **A first pass missed that the horizontal box‑gap and the vertical gap‑to‑the‑button‑below are two DIFFERENT CSS properties on two different elements** (`program-page-aspire-stats`'s own gap vs. the outer `program-page-aspire-hero-content` flex column's gap) — tightening only the former left the latter at its old ~24px, visibbly mismatched; user caught this ("you've only tightened the vertical spacing — not the horizontal" [sic, meant the reverse pairing was inconsistent]). **Fix:** new scoped combo `program-page-aspire-btn-tight-gap` (parent `if-btn-primary`, `margin-top:clamp(-16px, 8px - 2vw, -8px)` — a NEGATIVE margin computed to net out the container's own fluid `clamp(16px,2vw,24px)` gap down to a flat, constant 8px, matching the box‑gap exactly at every viewport width, not just the one tested) applied to the button. Verified against the real published CSS: both gaps = 8px, box‑pair width == button width (684px at 1440).
- **Lesson (the recurring one this session): when a "gap" spans two different flex containers (an inner row's own gap vs. an outer column's gap to the NEXT sibling), tightening one does NOT touch the other — they are unrelated CSS properties on unrelated elements, even though they look like "the same kind of gap" to the eye.** Check both explicitly before declaring a spacing pass done.

**Verification method used throughout this pass:** every change was (1) applied via `update_style`, (2) published, (3) re‑curled fresh from the live compiled CSS (new hash each time), (4) re‑measured against the REAL fetched CSS in a Playwright harness (never a hand‑patched/assumed value), (5) screenshotted for a visual look before reporting done — including, when the user pushed back with a specific accusation ("aligned to Factory, not Idea"), an ANNOTATED screenshot with measured guide lines overlaid, so the user could see the actual pixel evidence rather than take a claim on faith. This is the strongest form of the project's "verify, don't assert" discipline — worth reusing whenever a measurement claim is disputed.

## 63. ASPIRE nav rebuild, hero 3‑zone regrouping, glyph‑baseline hard fix, and full‑page build (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer, PUBLISHED

**A. ASPIRE nav rebuilt as 6 flat anchor links + gold apply button, entirely inside `Main Nav — ASPIRE`.** Removed the inherited Home/Students/Faculty‑Researchers/Companies/Partners links + the News and About dropdowns (7 elements) from the independent nav component (§58); built 6 new `if-nav-link` LinkBlocks (About/Eligibility/Deadlines/For Mentors/FAQ/Contact, each `href="#slug"`) plus repurposed the existing `if-give-btn-solid` "Give" button into "How To Apply" (`href="#how-to-apply"`) — that class was already gold‑bg/black‑text, a ready‑made match. **Lesson:** a stale static `href="#"` attribute can linger on a Link element after `set_link` changes its real `link` setting — the resolved `link` setting is authoritative for what publishes, but `remove_attribute` on the leftover raw `href` keeps element state honest. All changes scoped via `scope_component_id` to the independent component — verified the shared `Main Nav`'s instance count stayed unaffected throughout.

**B. Hero content regrouped into 3 explicit zones per user's iterative correction (superseding §61's simple top/paragraph/bottom split).** Final structure: `program-page-aspire-hero-content` (flex column, `justify-content:space-between`, `flex-grow:1` so it fills `if-hero-left`'s full height) contains exactly 3 children — `program-page-aspire-hero-topgroup` (ASPIRE h1 + gold eyebrow, own tight gap), `program-page-aspire-hero-midgroup` (body paragraph + stat boxes + How‑To‑Apply button + date‑line — floats in the middle, moves as one unit), `program-page-aspire-hero-botgroup` (footer links only, bottom‑anchored). **This took 3 rounds of live correction** — user first wanted just the paragraph floating alone, then "paragraph hangs together with the buttons," then "all of this [paragraph+stats+button+date‑line] floats middle, the two lines below float bottom" — each round was a straightforward `move_element` regroup (create/reuse a wrapper, move existing children into it), no rebuild needed. **Reusable pattern:** `justify-content:space-between` + a `gap` on the parent gives a true "top‑anchored / floats‑with‑min‑gap / bottom‑anchored" 3‑zone layout for free — `gap` sets the floor, `space-between` distributes any extra room, exactly matching "float in between with minimum padding" asks.

**C. ⚠️⚠️ Glyph‑to‑logo baseline — the REAL fix, after two wrong attempts genuinely frustrated the user.** §62 aligned the glyph's TOP‑ROW‑BOTTOM to Idea's baseline (matching the user's literal original wording) — but the glyph's own BOTTOM ROW then extended down to where it landed almost exactly ON Factory's baseline (measured: glyph bottom 104.27 vs Factory baseline 103.81, sub‑1px apart), so the glyph visually read as "sitting at Factory," not Idea — exactly what the user kept reporting, correctly, across several increasingly‑emphatic messages. **Root cause: satisfying "top‑row = Idea's baseline" for a glyph this tall (30px) against a logo where Idea‑to‑Factory baseline spacing is only ~16px inevitably drags the bottom half into/past Factory — the sub‑row reference point was the wrong target for what "aligned with Idea" means to a viewer looking at the icon as a whole.** **Fix:** anchor the glyph's OWN OVERALL BOTTOM EDGE (not a sub‑row) to Idea's baseline — `program-page-aspire-hdrprog-badge` `top` recomputed from `21.28px` → **`4.51px`** (`= ideaBaseline − glyphHeight − originalFlowTop = 53.5111 − 30 − 19`). Result: the entire glyph `[23.51,53.51]` sits ABOVE Idea's baseline with 16.3px of clear air before Factory's line — unambiguous at a glance, not just on measurement. Verified via the same annotated‑guide‑line screenshot technique as §62, sent directly to the user rather than re‑asserting the number. **Standing lesson: when a target reference point is a SUB‑PART of a multi‑part glyph/icon (a "row" within a multi‑row mark), check whether anchoring that sub‑part necessarily drags the WHOLE shape somewhere unintended — anchor the shape's own outer boundary to the target instead, unless the user is demonstrably asking about internal alignment specifically AND has confirmed the resulting extent is acceptable.**

**D. Full page built end‑to‑end from a header‑to‑footer mockup screenshot** — "as accurately and carefully as possible, using cognate existing site classes where possible for consistency... using established protocols & standards." Per user's explicit instruction, the pre‑existing leftover Home‑duplicate sections (`if-mani-sec`, `if-stage-sec`, `if-sm-box`, `if-aud-sec`, `if-proof-sec` [original], `if-news-sec`, `if-events-sec` — §29's "still pending deletion" list) were **deliberately left in place, untouched, positioned where they already sat** ("keep all this at the top... we can delete later") — all NEW sections were inserted as siblings AFTER that whole leftover block and BEFORE the Footer component instance (anchoring "before" a ComponentInstance errors — anchor on the last real sibling with `creation_position:"after"` instead). Eleven new sections, in final order:
1. **At a Glance** (`program-page-aspire-glance-sec`) — 5‑column stat strip (Award/Who/When/Limit/How), each card = `if-prog-card if-prog-card-stage` (the site's established red‑top‑via‑background‑gradient card, §32‑A — reused verbatim, not reinvented) + new `-glance-body/-label/-value` classes + `if-prog-desc` for the small copy.
2. **ASPIRE by the numbers** — reused `if-proof-sec/-wrap/-grid/if-countup/if-stat-label` **completely unmodified** (same red band, count‑up numbers as Home's "The proof"); only a scoped combo `program-page-aspire-numbers-grid` (2‑col override of the shared 3‑col `if-proof-grid`) was added, since the base 3‑col class is shared with Home and must stay untouched.
3. **About ASPIRE** (`#about`) — `if-eyebrow-row` + `if-manifesto if-mani-callout` heading + `if-mani-p` body (2 paragraphs) + `if-stg-photo/if-stg-img` (the About‑page stage‑photo frame, red‑left‑accent) with asset `aspire-02-about.png`.
4. **Build with ASPIRE** — reused `if-syn-hero/-bg/-overlay/-inner/-h/-h-gold` **verbatim** (the About‑page synthesis photo‑band pattern) with asset `aspire-03-band.png`.
5. **Eligibility** (`#eligibility`) — heading + 3‑item checklist, new `-elig-list/-item/-check/-note` classes (small red square + ✓ character, reused again in Deadlines' evaluation list).
6. **How to Apply** (`#how-to-apply`) — 5 numbered steps (new `-step-list/-step/-step-num/-step-role` + `if-mani-p`) beside a submission‑card sidebar (`if-prog-card if-prog-card-stage` + new `-card-body/-term/-h/-list/-file/-meta-row/-meta-label/-meta-value/-btn` [combo of `if-btn-primary`]).
7. **Deadlines** (`#deadlines`) — a CSS‑Grid "table" (new `-dl-table/-head/-cell/-cell-current` — deliberately NOT a semantic `<table>`, to avoid custom‑tag table/tr/td nesting risk under time pressure; header row dark, current‑term row gold‑tinted) + 2 info notes (`-dl-notes-row/-dl-note`) + a reused eligibility‑style checklist for evaluation criteria.
8. **For Mentors** (`#for-mentors`) — same step‑list pattern as How to Apply (5 mentor‑facing steps) + `if-stg-photo` (asset `aspire-04-mentors.png`) + 2 funding boxes reusing the **hero's own** `program-page-aspire-stat/-stat-red/-stat-gold/-value/-label` classes (same look as the hero's $1,200/$4,000 boxes, now $300/$1,000 minimums).
9. **After the Award** — simple 4‑col grid (new `-after-grid/-after-h` + `if-prog-desc`), no card chrome.
10. **FAQ** (`#faq`) + **Contact** (`#contact`) — FAQ = 2‑col Q/A grid (new `-faq-grid/-faq-q` + `if-prog-desc`); Contact = dark band (new `-contact-sec/-cols/-name/-title/-info/-photo`) with Joseph Naft's info + asset `aspire-05-contact.png`. **Built in the wrong order first** (both anchored "after" the SAME element lands second‑inserted‑first) — caught via a full‑tree re‑query and fixed with one `move_element`.
11. **Where ASPIRE Fits** — reused the About page's "Walk the Factory" bar **verbatim** (`if-walk-sec/-row/-label/-label-h/-label-sub/-item/-glyph/-txt/-num/-name`), linking each of the 4 stage glyphs to `/about#stage-0X` (cross‑page anchors — the target page's own stage sections already carry those DOM ids per §38).
- **Recurring builder pitfalls hit (and fixed) throughout, all previously documented but worth restating since they recurred often at this volume:** `set_text` silently no‑ops on `DivBlock`/custom‑tag‑div elements (hit on the countup numbers, 7+ checkmark icons, and the submit button — each time fixed by appending a `BY_CUSTOM_TAG span` with the text instead, then re‑querying to confirm); a `Heading` built with child spans but no own `set_text` seeds a stray default `"Heading"` string (hit once, removed). **A genuinely new pitfall this session:** a completely empty, unstyled‑content stray `Section` (styled `program-page-aspire-about-sec`, zero children) turned up adjacent to the Footer with no clear origin — likely a builder artifact from a race/retry — caught by a full `get_all_elements` re‑scan before wrapping up and deleted; **worth a final full‑tree sweep after any long build session, not just spot‑checking the sections you meant to add.**
- **Verification:** every section spot‑checked via `element_snapshot_tool` directly in the Designer (Glance, About, How‑to‑Apply, Deadlines, For Mentors, Where‑ASPIRE‑Fits all confirmed visually correct; Contact's snapshot call errored twice — a tool‑level flake, not a content issue — cross‑checked via `query_elements` instead, text/asset both confirmed present); a full‑page `query_elements` pass confirmed no other empty text nodes remained. Published and confirmed live.
- **Known placeholders / not yet real:** most body copy was read off a compressed mockup screenshot at the limit of legibility — treat exact wording (deadlines dates, dollar amounts beyond the ones explicitly confirmed, mentor/coordinator contact details) as best‑effort and confirm against source copy before this goes external; card/button links are `#anchor` placeholders within the page (real destinations — e.g. actual form URLs — still needed); the Deadlines table is a CSS‑Grid approximation of a table, not a semantic `<table>`; the FAQ/After‑the‑Award copy synthesized from the mockup's visible fragments, not verified word‑for‑word.

## 64. ASPIRE nav-button current-state fix, button text-centering rule, leftover-section cleanup (2026‑07‑09) — 1 shared‑CSS fix (promoted to `main`) + 2 native Designer rules + 7 elements removed, PUBLISHED

**A. Nav "How To Apply" button was picking up the site's current‑page look (dark‑red fill) — fixed in shared CSS, scoped to not affect real nav links.** Root cause: the repurposed `if-give-btn-solid` button's link is `{mode:"url", to:"#how-to-apply"}` — a same‑page anchor, which Webflow's runtime resolves to "the current page" exactly like a real page link, so it gets marked `.w--current` right alongside the 6 plain nav links. The site's nav current‑marker CSS (§13/§13b) has three broad `.if-navmenu a.w--current` safety‑net selectors (desktop dark‑red fill, mobile transparent‑reset, mobile red bar) that match ANY current anchor in the menu — including this solid gold CTA button, which should never take the "current nav link" look. **Fix:** added `:not(.if-give-btn-solid)` to all three selectors in `idea-factory.css`, plus a short comment explaining why. Verified in an isolated offline render: a plain `.if-nav-link.w--current` still gets the dark‑red/gold‑underline marker correctly; `.if-give-btn-solid.w--current` stays its native gold, no dark override, no stray red bar, at both desktop and mobile widths.
- **⚠️ Branch‑divergence lesson (a sharper case of §48's):** this session's dev branch (`claude/keen-johnson-f9w833`) had fallen genuinely STALE relative to `main` for `idea-factory.css` — `main` had already picked up several native‑migration cleanups from earlier this session (the walk‑bar stacking breakpoint, the nav scroll‑hide max‑height/`if-nav-tucked` rule, and the `if-prog-grid` column‑collapse breakpoints all moved from shared CSS to native Designer styles, per §54/§55, and were pushed straight to `main` at the time) that had never been pulled back into this dev branch's working copy. Unlike §48 (where dev and main content were byte‑identical and a straight cherry‑pick sufficed), this time a naive push of the dev‑branch file to `main` would have SILENTLY REGRESSED those three already‑shipped fixes. **Caught it by diffing the dev branch's file against `origin/main` BEFORE pushing** (not after) — found the divergence, took `origin/main`'s content as the base, applied the SAME 3‑selector edit to that instead (via a disposable git worktree tracking `origin/main`, so the working session's own checkout was never disturbed), pushed that to `main`, then separately synced the dev branch's copy to match so it isn't stale for the rest of the session. **Standing rule, sharper than §48's: before promoting ANY shared‑file edit to `main`, diff the working copy against `origin/main` first — don't assume identical content just because a prior promotion in the same session found them identical.**

**B. Button text was left‑aligned when it wrapped to two lines — fixed as a site‑wide rule, not a one‑off.** User: "As a rule of thumb, center text in buttons for the most part." The "Submit Your Application" button (`if-btn-primary program-page-aspire-card-btn`) had `justify-content:center` on its flex container (already present, §61's defensive fix) but NO `text-align` — `justify-content` only centers a flex ITEM as a block along the row; once that item's own box is forced nearly full‑width by wrapped text (2 lines), there's no extra space left for `justify-content` to redistribute, and the wrapped lines default to the browser's left‑aligned text. **Fix:** added `text-align:center` to the base **`if-btn-primary`** class AND to **`if-give-btn-solid`** (both global, native `update_style`) — safe site‑wide since single‑line button text (Home's "Find Your Path," the real nav Give button, etc.) is already exactly as wide as its own text, so centering it changes nothing visually; it only matters once text wraps. Verified in the Designer canvas via `element_snapshot_tool`. **Standing rule going forward: any new button class should get `text-align:center` at creation, not just `justify-content:center` on its flex container** — the two are not redundant once text wraps.

**C. Seven leftover Home‑duplicate sections removed from ASPIRE‑Home.** Per user's explicit follow‑up instruction (after §63‑D's "keep at the top for now, we'll delete later"): removed `if-mani-sec`, `if-stage-sec`, `if-sm-box`, `if-aud-sec`, the ORIGINAL `if-proof-sec` (Home's "The proof"), `if-news-sec`, and `if-events-sec` — the untouched Home‑manifesto/four‑stages/synthesis‑moment/audience/proof/news/events sections that sat between the Hero and the 12 new ASPIRE sections, none of which appear in the user's layout diagram. **Did NOT touch the second, NEW `if-proof-sec`** (id `07c3a01a…`, "ASPIRE by the numbers," §63‑D item 2) — same class name reused for visual consistency (the site's established practice) but a structurally distinct element built fresh for ASPIRE, sitting later in the page, with its own ASPIRE‑specific stats. Verified via a full‑page `get_all_elements` before AND after: the page now flows Header → Hero → At a Glance → ASPIRE by the numbers → About ASPIRE → Build with ASPIRE → Eligibility → How to Apply → Deadlines → For Mentors → After the Award → FAQ → Contact → Where ASPIRE Fits → Footer, with no gaps or reordering. Published.
- **Note:** this resolves the "pending deletion" leftovers specifically on **ASPIRE‑Home**. The ABOUT page's own still‑undeleted leftover sections (§29 — a different page, different element ids that coincidentally share the same class‑name pattern since About was also originally a raw Home duplicate) remain untouched and separate; do not conflate the two when either is revisited.

## 65. ASPIRE — Deadlines crosshatch fill, About‑ASPIRE full‑width eyebrow row, a genuinely‑flaky CDN asset, and the mirrored gold accent bar (2026‑07‑09) — 1 native class swap + 1 native element move + 2 fresh asset uploads + 1 new native combo + element, PUBLISHED

**A. Deadlines "current term" row — swapped the pale‑yellow fill for the archived gold crosshatch pattern.** User: "I don't like this pale yellow fill... remember that yellow crosshatch fill we'd developed before that we'd used in the dropdown hovers? Find that and put that in there." The pattern no longer exists in the live shared CSS (removed when the mobile‑nav hover was simplified to grow‑only, §13c) — recovered it from **git history** (`git log -S"hatch" -- idea-factory.css`, commits `d5b7938`/`f74d7fb`): `background-color:rgba(255,210,0,0.7)` + `background-image:repeating-linear-gradient(170deg,rgba(255,255,255,.55) 0,rgba(255,255,255,.55) .5px,transparent .5px,transparent 2.5px)`. Applied to `program-page-aspire-dl-cell-current` (native `update_style`, replacing its `#fff6d9` background-color) — no `!important` needed here (unlike the original nav-hover use, which needed to out-specify a competing `!important` rule; this combo has no such conflict). **Heads-up flagged to the user:** the 0.5px hairline stripe is nearly imperceptible at this scale/resolution — it reads mostly as a bold solid gold rather than a visible crosshatch texture. Not yet revisited: no follow-up complaint since, currently at rest with this value.

**B. About‑ASPIRE "ABOUT ASPIRE ─── SINCE 1998" eyebrow row now spans the full section width.** User: "should extend across the entire width of the page, as in other instances of a similar device used across the site." Root cause: `if-eyebrow-row` (label + `if-eyebrow-rule` divider + meta text) was nested INSIDE the narrow left TEXT column of the section's 2‑column grid (`program-page-aspire-about-cols`), so its divider could only stretch as far as that column — everywhere ELSE on the site (the About page's own stage headers, Deadlines, Eligibility, etc.) the eyebrow row is a standalone full‑width child sitting ABOVE any internal column split, never squeezed inside one. **Fix:** `move_element` the eyebrow row (id `94148457…`) out of the text column to become a direct child of `if-stage-wrap`, positioned immediately BEFORE `program-page-aspire-about-cols` — zero style changes needed, purely a structural move. Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`: rule now runs the full 1240px content width with "SINCE 1998" flush at the right margin.

**C. ⚠️⚠️ The About‑ASPIRE photo's intermittent broken‑image icon was a REAL, reproducible Webflow CDN failure — not a Designer‑cache artifact, not user error.** The user reported this **three times** (screenshots each time), with mounting (justified) frustration since earlier reassurances that "it should show now, try a refresh" turned out to be wrong on repeated checks. **Root cause, found by direct `curl` against the CDN (not guessed):** the asset's canonical/base filename URL (`cdn.prod.website-files.com/<site>/<assetId>_<filename>.png`) returned **HTTP 403** — persistently, across repeated checks minutes apart — while a KNOWN‑GOOD, long‑established site asset (Home's hero photo) returned 200 at the equivalent URL, ruling out a permanent site‑wide config problem. Several OTHER freshly‑uploaded ASPIRE photos from the same upload batch showed the SAME 403‑at‑base pattern (some self‑resolved over the course of the session, some didn't) — consistent with a genuine, asset‑specific CDN propagation/permission fault (not something under Designer or MCP control), not the general "Designer canvas doesn't load the shared file" caveat this project usually reaches for. **Fix (no waiting‑it‑out, no re‑assurances — direct action):** downloaded a working SIZED VARIANT of the broken asset (its `-p-1080` derivative, which DID resolve, confirming the pixel content itself was fine) via `curl`, computed its md5, uploaded it as a **brand‑new** Webflow asset (`data_assets_tool create_asset` → presigned S3 POST, with the established policy‑validation guard, §30/§31), confirmed the NEW asset's canonical URL resolves (200) BEFORE touching anything, then `set_image_asset` the About‑ASPIRE `if-stg-img` element to the new asset id and published. **Verified stable since** (no further broken reports on this specific image). **Standing lesson: when a user reports the SAME visual failure multiple times and an explanation didn't fix it, stop reasoning and go get hard evidence (`curl -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}"` against the actual serving domain) — a plausible‑sounding theory ("Designer cache," "just refresh") is worthless if it's wrong, and repeating it after being wrong once is what actually damages trust here, not the underlying bug.** Also recovered the original full‑page layout mockup image from the session transcript (same technique as §28a: extract base64 image blocks from genuine (non‑tool‑result) user messages in the `.jsonl` transcript) to confirm exact visual intent for item D below — reusable whenever a past‑uploaded design reference is needed but has scrolled out of context.

**D. Gold accent bar added to the About‑ASPIRE photo — mirrored (right‑anchored) vs. the About page's left‑anchored original, confirmed against the recovered layout mockup.** The photo already had the established `if-stg-photo` red‑left `border-left`; it was missing the matching `if-stg-goldbar` bottom accent entirely. Zoomed into the recovered mockup (`about_photo_zoom3.png` in scratchpad) and confirmed: red bar stays LEFT (unchanged), but the gold bar sits at the bottom‑**RIGHT** (not bottom‑left, and with a visible gap from the red corner — not touching it), the mirror image of the About page's stage‑photo pattern (§28/§34, gold bottom‑left, touching the red corner). **Built as a new combo, not a shared‑class edit:** `if-goldbar-right` (parent `if-stg-goldbar`) sets `margin-left:auto;margin-right:0` (flips the block from flush‑left to flush‑right within `if-stg-photo`'s normal flow — no `position:absolute`, avoiding the empty‑absolute‑div Designer‑canvas‑inflation bug, §31E/§34) plus a mirrored `clip-path:polygon(1.75px 0,100% 0,100% 100%,0 100%)` (cuts the top‑LEFT corner instead of top‑right, so the angled "flag" edge sits on the growing/leading side, same as the original's cut sits on ITS growing side). Appended as a `DivBlock` (`if-stg-goldbar if-goldbar-right`) directly after the `if-stg-img` inside `if-stg-photo`. **The scroll‑"grow" animation needed ZERO JS changes:** the shared module `about-goldbars` (idea-factory.js) already selects generically by class (`.if-stg-goldbar, .if-syn-goldbar`) and just writes an inline `width:X%` as the element scrolls into view (30%→90%) — since `margin-left:auto` keeps the bar's RIGHT edge pinned while its LEFT edge is what moves as width changes, the exact same JS logic that visually reads as "grows in from the left" for every OTHER instance reads as "grows in from the right" for this one, automatically, purely from the CSS anchor direction. Verified via `element_snapshot_tool` (red bar full‑height left, gold bar spanning the right ~40% of the bottom edge with a clear gap from the corner) and confirmed the shared JS selector match by grep. Published.
- **Scope note:** this was applied ONLY to the one photo the user was actively discussing (About‑ASPIRE). The "For Mentors" section's photo also uses `if-stg-photo`/`if-stg-img` and its bottom edge wasn't fully visible in the (very low‑resolution, 258px‑wide) recovered mockup — **not yet confirmed whether it needs the same treatment; revisit if asked.**

**E. ⚠️ "At a Glance" rebuilt from a program‑card grid into a single connected progression strip — the wrong‑component lesson.** User (angry): "You've completely messed up the 'at a glance' feature... You put them in equivalent boxes. That's NOT what this feature is... We're showing a PROGRESSION here: NOT AN ARRAY OF PROGRAMS or NEWS ITEMS." **Root cause:** the original build (§63‑D item 1) used `if-prog-card if-prog-card-stage` — the site's established clickable PROGRAM‑LISTING card component (`Link` elements, hover‑scale, individual borders, `grid-column-gap`, and the card's signature 3px red‑top accent line) — for all 5 segments. That component is semantically "a browsable array of independent items," which is exactly wrong for a left‑to‑right fact strip (Award → Who → When → Limit → How) meant to read as one continuous idea. Re‑examined the recovered layout mockup (§65‑C's extraction) at `glance_zoom2.png`: it shows ONE bordered rectangle (single 2px outer border, thin 1px internal dividers, NO gaps, NO rounded corners, NO per‑segment red line) with only the first segment solid‑filled red.
- **Fix:** rebuilt as plain `DivBlock`s (not `Link`s — there's no click destination for a fact segment), removing all card chrome: `program-page-aspire-glance-grid` `grid-column-gap`/`grid-row-gap` → `0`, added `border:2px solid #1a1a1a` on the GRID itself (the single outer frame). New base class `program-page-aspire-glance-item` (`display:flex;flex-direction:column;background:#fff;color:#1a1a1a;border-right:1px solid #e6e6e6`) with a native `pseudo:"last-child"` override (`border-right:none`) so the 5th segment doesn't double up with the outer border — no JS, no `:nth-child` selector hack, just the Designer's native last‑child pseudo state. New combo `program-page-aspire-glance-award` (parent `-glance-item`, `background-color:#e21833`) on the first segment only. **Content migration, not a rebuild‑from‑text:** moved the EXISTING `program-page-aspire-glance-body` blocks (label/value/desc paragraphs, already correct) out of their old `Link` parents into the 5 new plain divs via `move_element`, then deleted the 5 now‑empty old card links — preserved all copy without retyping anything.
- **White‑on‑red text:** the label/value/desc paragraphs' shared classes default to gray/dark (correct for the 4 white segments) but would be invisible on the red Award segment — added 3 new scoped combos (`program-page-aspire-glance-label-onred`, `-value-onred`, and `-glance-desc-onred` parented under the shared `if-prog-desc`) each just setting `color:#fff`/`rgba(255,255,255,.85)`, applied only to the Award segment's 3 text instances via `set_style` — same "reuse the base class, override color only" pattern as the hero's `stat-red`/`stat-gold` combos (§61).
- **Trophy icon:** user uploaded `trophy.svg` (full gold‑tone icon, `#fbc700`/`#ffd200`/`#ffa812` — already brand‑appropriate against red, no recoloring needed) to Webflow assets; inserted as an `Image` (new class `program-page-aspire-glance-trophy`, 40×40px, `margin-bottom:6px`) prepended before the "AWARD" label inside the red segment.
- **Verified** via `element_snapshot_tool`: one continuous black‑bordered strip, thin gray dividers between segments (none on the last), zero gaps, zero rounded corners, zero per‑segment red top line, gold trophy above white "AWARD"/"$1,200…"/"Paid directly…" text on solid red, other 4 segments unchanged white/gray‑label/dark‑value. Grid's default `align-items:stretch` naturally equalizes all 5 segments to the tallest (Award, now with the trophy) — confirmed the 4 shorter segments just carry extra bottom whitespace rather than being visually shorter. Published.
- **Standing lesson (the core one here):** reusing an established component for its LOOK can silently import its SEMANTICS too — `if-prog-card` isn't just "a bordered box with padding," it's specifically "this is one clickable item in a browsable list," and that meaning shows up as real, visible differences (hover‑scale, gaps between items, individual borders, a top accent line) that read as "array of independent cards" even when the content itself is a linear progression. Before reusing a card/component class for a NEW section, ask what the class semantically MEANS on the rest of the site, not just what properties it happens to carry — a "look-alike" reuse in the wrong semantic context is exactly the kind of thing that reads as "completely messed up" even though every individual CSS property might be defensible in isolation. **Not yet audited: whether any OTHER section on this page made the same card‑reuse mistake** — the user said "it looks like many others as well," which may mean this one section had several symptoms (plural) rather than multiple sections; revisit if flagged again.
- **⭐ Follow‑up (same session): outer edge softened, and a new STANDING RULE set.** User: "get rid of that black outline around the whole thing — I don't like that, don't use those on things I bring over. Make the edge more subtle using another precedent from the site. You can apply subtle shadow to the whole thing as well." The `2px solid #1a1a1a` outer border (my own invention, not in the mockup — the mockup's border color was never actually color‑sampled, I'd just defaulted to near‑black) was swapped for `1px solid #e6e6e6` (matches `if-prog-card`'s own established border color, and now matches the strip's internal 1px dividers exactly — one consistent subtle gray throughout) **plus** `box-shadow:0 4px 8px -2px rgba(0,0,0,0.06)` (the site's standing "soft lift" shadow, reused verbatim from the header bars §23, the Walk‑the‑Factory bar §41, and the gold Premise section §43 — a real, established, repeated precedent, not a new invention). **⭐ STANDING RULE going forward, stated explicitly by the user: when rebuilding a section from a reference/mockup the user "brings over," do NOT default to a bold black outline for a box/strip edge — reach for the site's existing subtle‑edge vocabulary first (`#e6e6e6` 1px borders, and/or the `0 4px 8px -2px rgba(0,0,0,0.06)` soft shadow), the same way HARD RULE #1 already requires reusing native look‑and‑feel rather than inventing new visual treatments.**
- **Section background lightened to `#faf9f8` to pop against the page.** User: "make the background on that one a light grey color to make the whole thing pop more." `program-page-aspire-glance-sec` `background-color` `#ffffff` → `#faf9f8` — reused the EXACT hex already established sitewide as "the" light‑gray section tone (`program-page-aspire-dl-sec`/Deadlines, and the synthesis diagram panel §43/§46), not a newly‑picked gray. The white strip (unchanged) now separates cleanly from the page.
- **Content centered in every segment + trophy enlarged 50%.** `program-page-aspire-glance-body` gained `align-items:center` (centers the flex column's children — icon/label/value/desc — horizontally) + `text-align:center` (centers each paragraph's own wrapped lines); both properties needed since `align-items` only positions each child as a block, it doesn't center multi‑line text within that block. `program-page-aspire-glance-trophy` `40px→60px` (both dimensions, exactly 1.5×). Published.
- **Category labels ("WHO"/"WHEN"/"LIMIT"/"HOW"/"AWARD") made big + heavy — the real headline of each segment.** User: "The labels/question words in this feature being tiny print is stupid. This is what people WANT TO KNOW. Make those big and a heavy weight." `program-page-aspire-glance-label` `font-size:12px→clamp(20px,2.2vw,26px)` (fluid, per HARD RULE #2 — never a flat px for a sizing change) + `font-weight:700→800` (heaviest available) + `margin-bottom:4px→8px` (breathing room at the new size). Left color (`#7f7f7f`)/uppercase/letter-spacing untouched — reads clearly as the dominant line in each segment now, ahead of the `-value` line beneath it. Published.
- **Award dollar figures ("$1,200"/"$4,000") enlarged relative to their surrounding qualifier text.** User: "Make those figures BIGGER" (just the numbers, not the whole value line — "per semester"/"for the summer" stay at the base 18px). The value paragraph's text was already split across 3 lines by native `<br>`s with "$1,200 " as its own isolated String but "$4,000" embedded mid-string inside "per semester / $4,000 " — **deleted both old String nodes and rebuilt the run as 4 inline `BY_CUSTOM_TAG span`s** in the same order (`$1,200 ` → `<br>` → `per semester / ` + `$4,000 ` → `<br>` → `for the summer`), giving ONLY the two dollar-figure spans the new global class **`program-page-aspire-glance-figure`** (`font-size:clamp(28px,3.2vw,38px)`; weight/color inherit from the parent paragraph, so no separate override needed). Verified via `query_elements` (exact child order + text preserved) and `element_snapshot_tool`. Published.
- **⭐ Rebuilt as a Mondrian‑style 3‑column/2‑row CSS Grid — a genuine 2‑D placement case, the one scenario §56 says Grid (not flexbox) is the right tool.** User: "The five‑cell row is bad layout... mimicking the 'Mondrian look' put them in THREE COLUMNS: Award (double height) / Who‑over‑Limit / When‑over‑How." `program-page-aspire-glance-grid` → `grid-template-columns:repeat(3,1fr)` + **added** `grid-template-rows:repeat(2,1fr)` (ensures the two stacked cells in cols 2‑3 together equal Award's spanned height). `program-page-aspire-glance-award` (existing combo) gained `grid-column:1;grid-row:1 / 3` (spans both rows). Four NEW uniquely‑named combos (§29 Lesson A — never share one combo name across different placements) on the shared `program-page-aspire-glance-item` base: `-cell-who` (`grid-column:2;grid-row:1`), `-cell-limit` (`grid-column:2;grid-row:2`), `-cell-when` (`grid-column:3;grid-row:1`), `-cell-how` (`grid-column:3;grid-row:2`).
  - **Divider borders recomputed for the NEW visual grid, not the old 5‑across order.** The base item class's `border-right:1px #e6e6e6` (all non‑last DOM child) + `pseudo:last-child{border-right:none}` was tuned for a flat 5‑item row and no longer matches 2‑D position — DOM order is still Award→Who→When→Limit→How (unchanged; still a sensible reading order for the new grid too), so "How" (last DOM child) still correctly gets no border‑right (it's the rightmost column either way) and "Limit" still correctly keeps its border‑right (divides col2 from col3) — but **"When" (3rd in DOM order, NOT last‑child) would incorrectly inherit a border‑right along what is now the grid's own right edge**, so `-cell-when` explicitly overrides it to `none`. New `border-bottom:1px solid #e6e6e6` added to `-cell-who` and `-cell-when` only (the two TOP‑row cells in cols 2‑3, dividing them from Limit/How beneath) — Award needs no internal bottom border (one continuous red block) and Limit/How need none (bottom of their column already meets the outer grid border).
  - **Why Grid here, not flexbox, despite §56's standing preference:** true 2‑D placement (one cell spanning 2 rows while its neighbor column splits into 2) has no clean flexbox equivalent — this is the exact "genuinely needs 2‑D placement" exception §56 itself carves out. None of §56's actual failure triggers are present (no `display:contents`, no negative‑offset positioning, no dissolved wrapper) — this is a plain, standard `grid-column`/`grid-row` placement on direct grid children, the least risky shape of Grid usage.
  - Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`: Award spans the full 2‑row height on the left; Who/Limit stack in col 2 with a divider between them; When/How stack in col 3 with a divider between them; one continuous vertical line between col1/col2 and col2/col3; no stray border under Award. Published.
- **The four question‑word labels ("WHO"/"WHEN"/"LIMIT"/"HOW") turned brand red.** User: "Make those four question words the palate red color." Simple base‑class edit: `program-page-aspire-glance-label` `color:#7f7f7f→#e21833`. **"AWARD" was unaffected on purpose** — its `-onred` white‑text combo (§65‑E) has equal‑or‑higher specificity as a combo (`.base.combo{}` beats `.base{}`) and is declared to override color regardless, so the shared base‑class edit only reached the 4 white‑background labels as intended; verified in the same snapshot. Published.
- **Real GAPS between the Mondrian cells (replacing the hairline‑border dividers), then tuned twice more.** User: "Try adding a GAP between the cells of an equal width (horizontal and vertical gaps of equal width)." Set `program-page-aspire-glance-grid` `grid-column-gap`/`grid-row-gap` from `0` to a real `16px`, and **removed the now‑redundant per‑cell hairline borders** (`remove_properties` on `program-page-aspire-glance-item`'s base `border-right` + its `last-child` pseudo override, and on the `-cell-who`/`-cell-when` combos' `border-bottom`/`border-right`) — a real gap plus leftover 1px borders would have doubled the dividers and looked busy. Since the grid container itself has no background, the gap area shows the section's light‑gray `#faf9f8` through the gutters between the white cells.
  - **Award cell content re‑centered vertically** (separate ask, same turn): "Center the content within the 'award' cell vertically." Now that Award spans 2 grid rows (§65's Mondrian rebuild) its body no longer fills a single‑row height, so its content (trophy/label/value/desc) sat at the top with empty space below. New scoped combo **`program-page-aspire-glance-body-center`** (parent `program-page-aspire-glance-body`, `justify-content:center`) applied ONLY to the Award instance's body element — the other 4 cells' bodies are unaffected (their own single‑row height already has little slack). **⚠️ Caught + fixed a copy‑paste mistake mid‑turn:** the first `set_style` call for this accidentally carried over `program-page-aspire-glance-value`/`-value-onred` (the VALUE PARAGRAPH's classes, not the body container's) onto the Award body element, since `set_style` REPLACES an element's full class list and the wrong list was pasted in — caught immediately via a `query_elements` re‑check (a class list that doesn't match what was just requested is a signal to re‑verify, not assume success from a green `status:"success"` response) and corrected with a second `set_style` call to the intended `["program-page-aspire-glance-body","program-page-aspire-glance-body-center"]`.
  - **Gaps tightened + contrast increased** (third ask, same turn): "make the gaps smaller and make the contrast with the background greater." `grid-column-gap`/`grid-row-gap` `16px→8px`; grid container `background-color` set to the site's established mid‑gray `#7f7f7f` (already used elsewhere, e.g. nav‑sublink/meta text — reused rather than inventing a new gray, consistent with the standing reuse‑precedent practice) so the gutters between cells now read as a clearly visible, deliberate grid line rather than a barely‑there tint of the section background.
  - **⚠️ Corrected immediately (user, same turn): "NO! You put a FILL behind the whole thing. TAKE THAT OUT: Make the entire BACKGROUND a bit darker."** The `#7f7f7f` grid‑container background read as a solid gray FILL sitting behind/around the cells (since it fills the whole grid box, not just the thin gutters) — not what "contrast" meant. **Fix:** removed `background-color` from `program-page-aspire-glance-grid` entirely (`remove_properties`, back to transparent — gaps once again show whatever sits behind the grid) and instead darkened the SURROUNDING SECTION's own background — `program-page-aspire-glance-sec` `background-color` `#faf9f8→#f0efed` (one small, deliberate step down, not a big jump — "a BIT darker"). Net effect: the whole section (page background behind/around the strip) is a touch darker, and the gaps between cells inherit that same slightly‑darker tone naturally (no separate fill layer) rather than a distinct, heavier gray box. **Lesson: "increase contrast at the gaps" and "add a background fill to the container" are NOT the same fix** — a fill colors the whole box (including the thin lines that should read as gaps‑in‑a‑white‑surface), while darkening the page‑level background behind everything preserves the gaps as true negative space, just against a darker backdrop. Verified via `element_snapshot_tool` after each of the 4 sub‑changes in this batch; published once at the end.
- **All 3 text tiers scaled up together (labels, value, figures) to keep their relative hierarchy intact.** User: "Make the Question Words and the word 'Award' BIGGER. Make the bold text also BIGGER." `program-page-aspire-glance-label` `clamp(20,2.2vw,26)→clamp(26,2.8vw,32)`; `program-page-aspire-glance-value` (the bold answer line — "the bold text") flat `18px→clamp(22px,2.4vw,28px)` (fluid now too, per HARD RULE #2); `program-page-aspire-glance-figure` (the dollar amounts, already‑bold‑and‑bigger per §65) `clamp(28,3.2vw,38)→clamp(34,3.8vw,44)` — scaled up IN THE SAME PASS as value so the figures stay clearly bigger than the surrounding value text rather than the gap between them shrinking. Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`: all 3 tiers bigger, no wrapping/overflow issues in the half‑height Who/Limit/When/How cells (checked specifically since the Mondrian rebuild, §65, made those cells shorter than before). Published.
- **Labels switched from ALL‑CAPS display to title case.** User: "Make Question Words and the word 'Award' title case." The underlying text was ALREADY authored as `"Award"/"Who"/"When"/"Limit"/"How"` (proper title case) — the all‑caps LOOK was purely a `text-transform:uppercase` display transform on `program-page-aspire-glance-label`. **Fix = one property:** `text-transform:uppercase→none` — no text content changed, letter‑spacing (`0.08em`, originally tuned for all‑caps tracking) left as‑is since the user didn't ask for it and it still reads fine on mixed‑case at this weight/size. Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`. Published.
- **⚠️ Reverted both the size bump AND the title‑case change on the labels only.** User: "Nah I dont like it. Revert twice on Question Words and the word 'Award'." Restored `program-page-aspire-glance-label` to its state from 2 changes prior: `font-size:clamp(26,2.8vw,32)→clamp(20px,2.2vw,26px)` (undoes the "make bigger" bump) + `text-transform:none→uppercase` (undoes the title‑case change) — both properties reset in ONE `update_style` call. **Scoped to the label class only** — the value/figure size increases from the same "bigger" request are a DIFFERENT class (`program-page-aspire-glance-value`/`-figure`) and were intentionally left untouched, since the user said "Question Words and the word 'Award'" specifically, not the value lines beneath them. `element_snapshot_tool` errored twice in a row during verification (a tool‑level outage, not a content problem — same generic failure pattern noted elsewhere this project, e.g. §56) — proceeded on the strength of the `update_style` response itself, which echoed back the exact reverted property values, and the fact that these exact values were ALREADY visually verified via snapshot earlier in this same session (before the two changes being undone were ever applied). Published; re‑confirm visually next time the snapshot tool is healthy if any doubt remains.
- **Label font-weight — confirmed already at the type family's ceiling, not changed.** User: "can you make Question Words and the word 'Award' heavier weight?" `program-page-aspire-glance-label` is already `font-weight:800`, the heaviest real weight the Adobe Interstate kit serves (400/500/700/800 only, no 900, per §22). **Did not apply a faux‑bold `text-stroke` workaround** — the user explicitly rejected that exact technique once already for a different element (§20: "only renders on published, not a real face") — so this was surfaced as a known limitation and a direct question back to the user, rather than silently faking it or silently doing nothing. No change made pending the user's answer.
- **Letter‑spacing on the labels — identified via direct query, then removed per the user's follow‑up.** User asked "Is the letter spacing wider on these?" — confirmed via `query_styles` that `program-page-aspire-glance-label` carries an explicit `letter-spacing:0.08em` while the value/desc text below it has none (normal), so yes, measurably wider. That tracking dated back to when the label was all‑caps (extra tracking aids all‑caps readability) and was left in place through the title‑case experiment and its later revert. User: "remove that wide spacing there" → `letter-spacing:0.08em→normal`. Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`; reads cleanly at the current all‑caps/800‑weight/`clamp(20,2.2vw,26)` combination. Published.
- **Line-height tightened on the bold value text ONLY, not the labels.** User: "the bold text (that is NOT Question Words and the word 'Award'): Decrease line spacing on that." — a precise scope callout distinguishing `program-page-aspire-glance-value` (the multi‑line wrapped answer text — "Clark School undergraduates," "Fall, Spring, or Summer," the Award dollar‑figure lines, etc.) from `program-page-aspire-glance-label` (left untouched). `line-height:1.25→1.1`. The `program-page-aspire-glance-figure` dollar‑figure spans inherit line‑height from the same parent paragraph, so they tightened too without a separate edit. Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`: wrapped lines (e.g. "Find a mentor, define a project, apply," the 3‑line Award value) visibly closer together, no overlap/clipping. Published.
- **⚠️⚠️ The "$1,200"/"$4,000" figure spans from earlier in §65 had SILENTLY REVERTED to plain, unstyled text — root cause never fully identified, but caught and re‑fixed immediately.** User: "I told you and you never did it: Make $1,200 and $4,000 BIGGER. Also make them gold." A `query_styles` check on `program-page-aspire-glance-figure` showed the size increase from earlier in this same session (`clamp(34,3.8vw,38)`… → `clamp(42,4.6vw,54)`) WAS still correctly defined on the class — but a `query_elements` check on the actual value paragraph's children showed **"$1,200 " and "$4,000 " had reverted to bare String nodes, no longer wrapped in the `BY_CUSTOM_TAG span`s** built earlier — i.e. the class definition survived but its APPLICATION to these two spans was lost at some point between then and now (`"per semester /"`/`"for the summer"`'s plain‑string siblings were untouched, and the `<br>` elements were untouched — only the two figure‑wrapping spans vanished, replaced by plain text carrying the exact same content). **No single action in the intervening turns (Mondrian rebuild, Award‑body centering + its classlist‑fix, gap/contrast changes, label size/weight/case/spacing edits) obviously touches this specific paragraph's children** — flagging this honestly as an unexplained one‑off rather than claiming false certainty about the cause. **Fix:** re‑applied the exact same technique as the original build (§65) — insert a new `BY_CUSTOM_TAG span` styled `program-page-aspire-glance-figure` immediately before each plain "$…" string, then `remove_element` the old plain string — verified via `query_elements` that both spans were correctly back in place (with the class) before trusting a snapshot. Also added `color:#ffd200` (brand gold) to `program-page-aspire-glance-figure` per the user's second ask, and bumped the size further (`clamp(42,4.6vw,54)→ up, then per follow‑up feedback "too big, a little smaller" → clamp(36px,4vw,46px)` final). **Standing lesson: a style CLASS being correctly defined does not guarantee it's still APPLIED to the elements it was applied to earlier — when a user reports something "was never done" that you're confident you did, check the actual element/child structure via `query_elements`, not just the style definition via `query_styles` — the two can silently diverge.** Verified via `element_snapshot_tool` at each size step; published.
- **Small extra vertical gap added between "per semester" and "$4,000" ONLY (not the other two line breaks in the same value block).** User: "Put a small vertical gap between 'per semester' and '\$4,000'." All 3 line breaks in the Award value (`$1,200` / `per semester` / `$4,000` / `for the summer`) are plain `<br>` tags with no per‑break styling hook, and the two figure spans share one class — so a plain `margin-top` on `program-page-aspire-glance-figure` would have also pushed "$1,200" away from "AWARD" above it, which wasn't asked for. **Fix:** new scoped combo `program-page-aspire-glance-figure-gap` (parent `program-page-aspire-glance-figure`, `display:inline-block;margin-top:8px` — the `inline-block` is required since a plain inline span ignores vertical margin in normal CSS flow) applied to ONLY the `$4,000` span, leaving `$1,200`'s spacing untouched. Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`: extra breathing room visible between "per semester" and "$4,000" specifically, other line gaps unchanged. Published.
- **⭐ Caught + fixed a real §32‑E Georgia‑on‑dark violation, found via direct user audit question ("Do you see any problem in this section vis‑a‑vis Georgia font usage?").** The Award segment's caption ("Paid directly to the student each term.") uses `if-prog-desc` (base `font-family:Georgia,"Times New Roman",serif`) + the `program-page-aspire-glance-desc-onred` combo — but that combo ONLY overrode `color` (to white 85%), never `font-family`, so it was rendering white Georgia serif text on the red background: the exact same class of bug as `cta-fine` (§32‑E) and the ASPIRE hero body paragraph (§62). **Fix:** added `font-family:"Interstate","Helvetica Neue",Arial,sans-serif` to `program-page-aspire-glance-desc-onred` — scoped to just this ONE onred combo, so the OTHER 4 segments' captions (Who/When/Limit/How, plain `if-prog-desc` on white, correctly Georgia) are untouched. Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`. Published. **This is now the THIRD time this exact rule has been violated on this site (cta-fine, ASPIRE hero body, this) — worth treating "any new `-onred`/`-ondark`/white‑text combo built on a Georgia‑family base class" as an automatic check‑the‑font‑family moment going forward, not just a reactive fix when caught.**
- **⭐ STANDING RULE captured: colored‑background text gets ONE weight step heavier than its white‑background equivalent — then the whole section's remaining Georgia converted to Interstate.** Two related asks, same turn. First, following the Georgia‑to‑Interstate fix above: "can [you] push the weight of that bit you just converted from Georgia up to the next weight?" — bumped `program-page-aspire-glance-desc-onred` `font-weight` (previously unset/inherited 400) to **500** (Medium — the next real step up in the Interstate kit's 400/500/700/800 ladder, §22). Mid‑fix the user volunteered the reasoning as a **general practice going forward**, verbatim: *"It is my general style refinement practice to make fonts on colored backgrounds boosted a bit heavier than the equivalent against a white background."* **Recorded here as a standing rule: any new `-onred`/`-ondark`/colored‑background text combo should default one weight step heavier than its white‑background sibling, not just match its color** — apply this proactively on future colored‑background text, don't wait to be asked per‑instance. Then, same breath, the user widened scope: *"Actually on this whole piece try converting all those [G]eorgia fonts (even those on [W]hite) to Interstate."* Audited the full "At a Glance" section via `query_elements` scoped to the grid container (`element_filter:{style:"if-prog-desc"}`) and found exactly 5 matches: the already‑fixed Award caption (`-onred`, now Interstate 500) plus **4 white‑background captions** (Who/When/Limit/How) still genuinely on Georgia. **Fix:** created a sibling combo **`program-page-aspire-glance-desc-onwhite`** (parent `if-prog-desc`, `font-family:"Interstate","Helvetica Neue",Arial,sans-serif"`, no weight override — stays at the inherited 400, since 400 is the correct baseline per the new rule above, the heavier 500 being reserved for the colored/red instance) and applied it alongside `if-prog-desc` to all 4 white‑bg caption paragraphs. Verified via `query_elements` that the class list persisted on all 5 captions (1 `-onred` + 4 `-onwhite`) BEFORE trusting a snapshot (§65's own "class defined ≠ class applied" lesson) — confirmed via `element_snapshot_tool`: all 5 captions now render in clean Interstate, matching the section's labels/values/figures, zero Georgia remaining anywhere in "At a Glance." Published.
- **Cell vertical padding increased.** User: "Add more margin to the top and bottom of these cells." The actual mechanism is `padding-top`/`padding-bottom` on **`program-page-aspire-glance-body`** (the flex‑column content wrapper inside every cell, including Award) — bumped `20px→32px` on both. Applies uniformly to all 5 segments (shared base class, no per‑cell override needed). Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`: visibly more breathing room above/below each cell's content block. Published.
- **"per semester" / "for the summer" qualifier text — tried smaller, user reverted, landed on a lighter weight instead.** User: "Make 'per semester' and 'for the summer' smaller." These two phrases were plain, unstyled `String` nodes sitting directly in the Award value paragraph (siblings of the `<br>`s and the two `program-page-aspire-glance-figure` dollar spans) — inheriting the parent paragraph's own `clamp(22px,2.4vw,28px)`/800‑weight/white styling with no independent style hook. **Fix (structural, kept):** new combo **`program-page-aspire-glance-qualifier`** (parent `program-page-aspire-glance-value`). Wrapped each phrase in a new `BY_CUSTOM_TAG span` (inserted immediately before the old plain string via `creation_position:"before"`, then the old string removed) styled `program-page-aspire-glance-value program-page-aspire-glance-qualifier`. **Caught immediately via a `query_elements` re‑check, before publishing:** the new spans initially rendered in the base value class's own dark `#1a1a1a` instead of the parent's white — since the bare strings previously had no class and simply INHERITED color from the parent paragraph, while the new wrapping span's own `program-page-aspire-glance-value` class carries an explicit `color:#1a1a1a` that overrides inheritance. **Fixed by also adding `program-page-aspire-glance-value-onred`** (the existing white‑text combo) to both new spans, matching the parent's own class stack. **Lesson (a new instance of the §65 "class defined ≠ class applied" family): wrapping a previously‑bare, color‑inheriting text node in a new span with an explicit‑color class can silently override inherited color — always re‑check color/context after such a wrap, not just the property being changed.**
  - First size attempt was `font-size:clamp(14px,1.6vw,18px)` (~0.65× the value line) — user: "No, that's too small, revert." **Reverted** by removing the `font-size` property from `program-page-aspire-glance-qualifier` entirely (back to inheriting the base value class's own `clamp(22px,2.4vw,28px)` — visually identical to the pre‑edit state, span wrapper kept in place).
  - User's actual ask, restated: "instead, make those the next lighter weight." Added `font-weight:700` to `program-page-aspire-glance-qualifier` — the next real step down from the inherited 800 in the Interstate kit's 400/500/700/800 ladder (§22), same family as the colored‑background weight‑boost rule just established, applied here in reverse (one step LIGHTER for a secondary/qualifier line under a heavier headline figure). Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`: "per semester"/"for the summer" now visibly lighter than the dollar figures and "AWARD" label, same size as originally, still white. Published.
- **User then asked directly: "Is 'Per summer' the same weight as the black bold text in the four white cells?"** Queried both styles rather than answering from memory: `program-page-aspire-glance-value` (the white‑cell bold answer lines) was `font-weight:800`; the qualifier combo above was `700` — genuinely different, confirmed and reported as such.
- **"OK, make those 700 too"** (the four white‑cell bold values). Since ALL 5 cells' answer text — the 4 white ones AND the Award cell's own value paragraph (parent of the `$1,200`/`$4,000` dollar‑figure spans) — share the SAME base class `program-page-aspire-glance-value`, changing its weight directly would have also lightened the dollar figures (they inherit weight since `program-page-aspire-glance-figure` had no explicit `font-weight` of its own). **Fix, in order:** (1) added an explicit `font-weight:800` directly to `program-page-aspire-glance-figure` FIRST — this locks the dollar figures at their current heavy weight via CSS's "explicit‑on‑element beats inherited‑from‑parent" rule, regardless of what the parent's weight later becomes; (2) THEN changed the base `program-page-aspire-glance-value` `font-weight` `800→700`. Net result: the 4 white‑cell values (Clark School undergraduates / Fall, Spring, or Summer / One ASPIRE project per term / Find a mentor…) dropped to 700 as asked, while `$1,200`/`$4,000` stayed 800 (untouched, since they now carry their own explicit weight) and the qualifier spans stayed 700 (already had their own explicit combo). Verified via `element_snapshot_tool` before publishing. **Reusable technique: to change a shared base class's weight/property without affecting a specific descendant that currently relies on inheritance, give that descendant its OWN explicit value FIRST (locking it), then change the base — order matters, doing it in the other order would show a flash of the wrong weight only in the Designer's undo history, not a real risk, but locking first is the cleaner sequence.**

**⭐ Award cell restructured into a TOP / MIDDLE / BOTTOM floating layout (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer.** User sent 3 cropped screenshots labeled "Float TOP" (trophy+AWARD), "Float MIDDLE" ($1,200.../$4,000...), "Float BOTTOM" (the caption) — specifying which content belongs in which zone of the cell. Previously all 4 elements (trophy, label, value, caption) were flat siblings in `program-page-aspire-glance-body`, vertically centered as ONE block via the Award‑only combo `program-page-aspire-glance-body-center` (`justify-content:center`, §65).
- **Fix (first pass):** grouped the trophy image + "AWARD" label into a NEW wrapper — global class **`program-page-aspire-glance-topgroup`** (`display:flex;flex-direction:column;align-items:center`), built via `data_element_builder` as a sibling before the trophy, then `move_element`‑ed both trophy and label inside it (append, in order). Changed `program-page-aspire-glance-body-center`'s `justify-content` `center→space-between` so the 3 now‑top‑level children (topgroup / value paragraph / caption paragraph) distribute with topgroup pinned top, caption pinned bottom, value floating between — same pattern as the ASPIRE hero's established 3‑zone layout (§63‑B). Verified via `element_snapshot_tool` + published.
- **Follow‑up (same session): "Smaller gap between 'Award' and '\$1,200'."** `space-between`'s two gaps (topgroup→value, value→caption) are always EQUAL by definition — no way to shrink just one while keeping the other via that property alone. **Fix:** switched `program-page-aspire-glance-body-center`'s `justify-content` `space-between→flex-start` (items resume natural top‑anchored flow; the topgroup→value gap is now just the body's own `grid-row-gap:8px` + the label's own `margin-bottom:8px` ≈ 16px, visibly tight) and added **`margin-top:auto`** to `program-page-aspire-glance-desc-onred` (the Award caption's OWN combo, already scoped to this one element — not the shared `if-prog-desc` base) so the caption alone still gets pushed all the way to the bottom, consuming whatever free space remains. **Reusable pattern: when a 3‑zone `space-between` layout needs ASYMMETRIC gaps (one tight, one loose), drop `space-between` for `flex-start` + a small shared `gap`, and push only the specific edge‑anchored element with its OWN scoped `margin-top:auto`/`margin-bottom:auto` combo — never touch the shared base class for a single‑instance margin.**
  - **User: "NO. Too much. Split the difference."** — the flex‑start fix above over‑corrected (16px total read as too cramped against the earlier, much looser `space-between` gap). **Fix:** added `margin-top:32px` to **`program-page-aspire-glance-value-onred`** (the Award value paragraph's OWN already‑scoped combo — not the shared `program-page-aspire-glance-value` base, so the 4 white‑cell values are untouched) on top of the existing ~16px base gap, landing at a visibly intermediate spacing — noticeably tighter than the original loose layout but with real breathing room above the tight 16px attempt. Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`, published. **Dial:** that `32px` value on `program-page-aspire-glance-value-onred` is the one knob for this specific gap — nudge up/down if revisited again.

## 66. ASPIRE "At a Glance" responsive columns bug + new intermediate breakpoint; "ASPIRE by the numbers" restored + resized; numbers grid stacks at vertical-phone (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer, PUBLISHED

**A. ⚠️⚠️ Root‑caused the "columns receive different widths" bug the user flagged with a screenshot — a genuine, pre‑existing responsive defect, not something the trophy/gap edits caused.** At narrower breakpoints the grid's own column COUNT shrinks (`program-page-aspire-glance-grid`: `main`/`medium`=3 cols, `small`=2 cols, `tiny`=1 col — all pre‑existing) but the 5 cells' placement combos (`-cell-who/-when/-limit/-how`, `-award`) were hardcoded to `grid-column:2`/`3` with **zero breakpoint overrides**. At `small` (2 explicit columns), anything placed at `grid-column:3` falls into an **implicit, auto‑sized column** Grid invents on the fly (`grid-auto-columns:auto`, content‑based, NOT constrained by `1fr`) — confirmed by direct measurement (offline harness): column 2 = 107px, the implicit column 3 = 420px, a 4× disparity, exactly reproducing the screenshot. **This is why the earlier same‑day `min-width:0` fix (§ this session, "Did you just push the trophy into 'Award'?" thread) didn't fully solve it** — that fix addressed a DIFFERENT, narrower content‑based‑minimum issue at the base breakpoint; the implicit‑column issue only manifests once the grid's own template shrinks. **Fix:** gave `-glance-grid`/`-award`/`-cell-who/when/limit/how` explicit `small` AND `tiny` breakpoint overrides collapsing to a single equal‑width stacked column (Award→Who→When→Limit→How, one per row). Verified via a real‑browser offline harness across 12 widths (320–1440px): equal widths, zero overflow, zero overlap at every width, in all 3 modes (3‑col desktop / stacked small+tiny at the time). Published.
- **Lesson: `element_snapshot_tool` renders at some fixed internal width that (for this page) lands inside the `small`/`tiny` range** — several snapshots this session that looked like "the fix didn't work" were actually correctly showing the ONE breakpoint not yet fixed, not a failure of the fix itself. When a snapshot disagrees with an offline test that passes cleanly, suspect a breakpoint mismatch before doubting the CSS.
- **Lesson: a duplicate‑style‑object check (§11) is worth ruling out early when a saved CSS change doesn't visibly take effect**, but in this case `get_styles query:"all"` grepped for the class name confirmed there was only ONE object each for the classes involved — the real cause was the breakpoint/implicit‑column issue above, not a duplicate.

**B. New INTERMEDIATE layout at the "small" (≤767, landscape/tablet‑phone) breakpoint, per explicit user spec — supersedes the single‑column stack from (A) at this ONE breakpoint.** User: "I want this to be an INTERMEDIATE state where the RED PIECE is full width at the TOP, and the remaining four pieces stack just as they are on Desktop (but they each get ~50% width... since the red piece is no longer a column competing for space)." Changed `small` breakpoint only (kept `tiny`'s single‑column full stack from (A) unchanged, per "vertical phone" being handled separately — see D below):
  - `-glance-grid` @small: `grid-template-columns:repeat(2,1fr)`, `grid-template-rows:repeat(3,auto)`.
  - `-award` @small: `grid-column:1/3` (spans both columns, full width), `grid-row:1`.
  - `-cell-who` @small: `grid-column:1;grid-row:2`; `-cell-when`: `grid-column:2;grid-row:2`; `-cell-limit`: `grid-column:1;grid-row:3`; `-cell-how`: `grid-column:2;grid-row:3` — i.e. the SAME relative 2×2 (Who|When over Limit|How) as desktop's column‑2/column‑3 × row‑1/row‑2, just now both columns are 50% of the FULL row instead of ⅓ each.
  - One update (`-cell-who` @small) hit a transient `MPS... [Conflict]` server error twice on retry — but `query_styles` showed the value had actually saved correctly anyway (a repeat of the "trust the query, not the error/success message" lesson, this time in the opposite direction: an ERROR response that was actually a false negative).
  - Verified via offline harness across 12 widths, asserting per‑mode geometry (desktop: 5 equal cols; small: award≈2×cell‑width + who/when same row same width + limit/how same row same width; tiny: 5 equal full‑width stacked rows) — all 12 pass. Published.

**C. "AT A GLANCE" missing its horizontal rule divider — user caught this against the original mockup.** The heading was a bare `if-eyebrow` Paragraph, never wrapped in the established `if-eyebrow-row` (label + `if-eyebrow-rule` divider + optional `if-eyebrow-meta`) pattern used 5× elsewhere on this same page (e.g. "About ASPIRE ⸻ Since 1998", §65‑B). **Fix:** built a new `if-eyebrow-row` div, moved the existing heading into it, appended an `if-eyebrow-rule` div after it (no meta needed — the mockup shows none). **⚠️ Caught my own mistake before it shipped:** the FIRST attempt passed the class as a bare `style_names` key directly on `element_schema` in `data_element_builder` — the correct shape is nested under `set_style:{style_names:[...]}`; the malformed call silently created unstyled, class‑less divs (an "unrecognized field" is dropped, not rejected). Caught via the now‑standard `query_elements` re‑check (styleNames came back empty) BEFORE trusting a snapshot, and fixed with a follow‑up `set_style` call. **Lesson: `data_element_builder`'s per‑node styling key is `set_style:{style_names:[...]}`, NOT a bare `style_names` array on the schema node — double‑check this exact shape every time, since a wrong shape fails silently (no error) rather than throwing.** Verified via snapshot: rule now runs the full remaining width. Published.

**D. "ASPIRE by the numbers" heading was missing entirely from the stat band — restored.** `if-proof-wrap` contained ONLY `if-proof-grid` (the stat grid); no heading element existed at all (structurally absent, not just hidden/empty — confirmed via a full section‑tree query). **Fix:** checked the Home page's own (untouched, original) `if-proof-sec` for the established pattern — `if-proof-wrap` › `if-proof-head` (flex row, `margin-bottom:clamp(36px,5vw,64px)`) › [`if-proof-h2` Heading, optional CTA button] › sibling `if-proof-grid`. Built a bare `if-proof-h2` Heading with text "ASPIRE by the numbers" (no CTA button needed, per the ASPIRE mockup) directly before the grid — **first attempt overlapped "900" underneath it**, because ALL the vertical spacing in the established pattern lives on the `if-proof-head` WRAPPER's `margin-bottom`, not on `if-proof-h2` itself (which has `margin:0` on every side). **Fix:** built the missing `if-proof-head` wrapper div, moved the heading inside it. Also corrected the heading's default level from `h1` (data_element_builder's default) to `h2` (`set_heading_level`), matching Home's pattern and avoiding a second `h1` on the page. **Lesson: when reusing an established multi‑element pattern, the spacing is often on the WRAPPER, not the content element — check the reference's full structure (not just the one element you're rebuilding) before assuming a bare rebuild will look right.** Verified via snapshot at each step; published.

**E. Impact numbers ("900"/"\$1.4M") and their descriptions enlarged, scoped to ASPIRE only.** User: "Make the impact numbers and the descriptions BIGGER here. Compare to the layout diagram." `if-countup` (`clamp(40px,5.6vw,96px)`) and `if-stat-label` (`14px`) are SHARED with Home's own "The proof" section — rather than editing those base classes (which would also resize Home's), created two new scoped combos: **`program-page-aspire-numbers-countup`** (parent `if-countup`, `font-size:clamp(52px,7.5vw,128px)`) and **`program-page-aspire-numbers-label`** (parent `if-stat-label`, `font-size:18px`) — applied to both of the 2 stat instances. Since ASPIRE's grid is 2‑column (vs. Home's 3‑column), there's meaningfully more per‑cell width available, so a larger size was safe. Verified via `query_elements` (class lists correct on all 4 elements) before snapshotting; published.

**F. Numbers grid (2‑col) now stacks to 1‑col at the vertical‑phone breakpoint — same root cause as (A), same fix shape.** `program-page-aspire-numbers-grid` (combo overriding the shared `if-proof-grid`'s 3‑col to 2‑col for ASPIRE) had properties only at `main` — meaning its un‑mediaqueried, higher‑specificity rule permanently overrode `if-proof-grid`'s OWN `small`‑breakpoint stacking behavior (`1fr`, inherited from Home) at every width, since a combo's unconditional (no‑media‑query) declaration beats a lower‑specificity base‑class rule even when that base rule sits inside an active `@media` block — specificity wins over media‑query truthiness when both simultaneously match. **Fix:** added `program-page-aspire-numbers-grid` @`tiny` (≤479, vertical/portrait phone): `grid-template-columns:1fr`. Left `small` (≤767, landscape) at the existing 2‑column layout — the user asked specifically for "vertical phone." Verified via offline harness across 8 widths (991→320): 2‑column holds down through 480px, stacks cleanly at 479px and below. Published.
- **Standing lesson (a sharper restatement of A's, worth calling out on its own): when a scoped ASPIRE combo overrides a SHARED class's property, check whether the shared class has ITS OWN breakpoint‑specific behavior the combo might be silently suppressing at every width — a combo with only a `main` value doesn't "leave the base breakpoints alone," it OUTRANKS them everywhere via specificity, even inside their own media queries.
