Any time a combo touches a property the base class ALSO sets per‑breakpoint, mirror the override at each breakpoint the base class uses, or the combo's un‑mediaqueried value wins unconditionally.**

## 67. "Build with ASPIRE" photo — flat dark overlay replaced with a strategic radial-gradient vignette (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer, scoped combo

User: "compare what you put there with what was in the layout guide material. It was applied STRATEGICALLY to HIGHLIGHT the girl in the middle and hopefully the two other people flanking her to a lesser degree. You didn't do that." The section reuses the About page's synthesis‑photo‑band pattern verbatim (`if-syn-hero/-bg/-overlay/-inner`, §63‑D item 4) — including `if-syn-overlay`'s flat `background-color:rgba(0,0,0,0.5)`, a UNIFORM darken with no spatial variation, correct for About's building/sunset photo (no figure to highlight) but wrong for ASPIRE's photo (3 people at a table, center figure in a VR headset meant to be the visual focus).

**Fix — scoped combo, not a shared-class edit.** `if-syn-overlay` is shared with the About page's differently-composed photo, so the fix couldn't touch the base class. New combo **`program-page-aspire-build-overlay`** (parent `if-syn-overlay`): `background-color:transparent` + `background-image:radial-gradient(circle at 50% 33%, rgba(0,0,0,0.05) 0%, rgba(0,0,0,0.25) 35%, rgba(0,0,0,0.55) 65%, rgba(0,0,0,0.75) 100%)`. Applied only to this one overlay element.
- **Focal point placed per user spec:** "50% 33%" = horizontal center, vertical 33% from the top — i.e. the "67% up from the bottom" the user asked for, landing on the central figure's face/headset.
- **Shape:** a true vignette — near-transparent at the focal point (5% black, the center figure reads almost unfiltered) → moderate at the 35–65% radius band (the two flanking people, "highlighted... to a lesser degree" as asked) → darkest at the outer edge (75% black, frames the photo and keeps the white/gold headline legible on the left).
- Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`: the center figure (VR headset) is visibly brighter than the flanking two, who fade toward the corners; headline text still reads clearly over the darker left edge. Saved in the Designer, not published.

**Follow-up (same session): headline moved from top-left to bottom-left — it was covering the central figure's face.** `if-syn-hero` is `display:flex;flex-direction:column;justify-content:space-between` with the text wrapper (`if-syn-inner`) as its only flex child, so the item defaulted to the top under `padding-top` alone (no `padding-bottom` existed). Both `if-syn-hero` and `if-syn-inner` are shared with the About page's synthesis section, so the fix again used scoped combos rather than touching the base classes: **`program-page-aspire-build-inner`** (parent `if-syn-inner`, `margin-top:auto` — pushes the single flex item to the bottom, same technique as the Award cell's bottom-anchored caption) and **`program-page-aspire-build-hero`** (parent `if-syn-hero`, `padding-bottom:clamp(48px,7vw,96px)` — mirrors the existing `padding-top` so the now-bottom-anchored text gets the same breathing room from the edge it had at the top). Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`: headline sits bottom-left, clear of the woman's face. Saved in the Designer, not published.

## 68. ASPIRE "How to Apply" — full REDO (steps + submission card) + a genuine CSS Grid vertical-gap bug found and fixed (2026‑07‑09/10) — native Designer, PUBLISHED

User rejected the first-pass "How to Apply" build outright ("you completely messed up the 'how to apply' section. Get rid of that and REDO it") and separately flagged the step numbers as "pink, not red." Rebuilt against the recovered layout-guide mockup (`project_page` full recovery, §65's `L2926_1.webp` technique — the submission-card region was re-cropped/upscaled at 8× via a fresh Python/Pillow pass since the earlier zoom crops were too blurry to read the card's internal structure).

**A. Step-number color — flat fix.** `program-page-aspire-step-num` was `rgba(226,24,51,0.35)` (35%-opacity red = reads pink). Changed to solid `#e21833`. One property.

**B. Submission card — rebuilt from a 2-line stub into the full mockup structure.** The card previously carried `if-prog-card if-prog-card-stage` (the site's clickable program-listing card, complete with its red top accent) and held only a flat term label + 3 plain-text file lines. Per the mockup's actual structure:
- **Removed `if-prog-card-stage`** (this card isn't a "browsable program listing," and the mockup shows no red top line here — solid black header instead).
- **New black header bar** (`program-page-aspire-card-header`, first child, `background:#1a1a1a`, flex row space-between) containing **"Submission Card"** (`program-page-aspire-card-header-title`, white/bold/uppercase) and the existing **`program-page-aspire-card-term`** ("Fall 2026") moved in and recolored gold (`#ffd200`) — confirmed single-use via `query_elements` before editing the shared-name class directly.
- **File list restructured** from 3 flat paragraphs into 3 **`program-page-aspire-card-file-row`** blocks (flex row: red bold number → `program-page-aspire-card-file-info` [bold title + gray subline] → optional `program-page-aspire-card-file-badge` "PDF" outline chip), each with a bottom divider except the last (`program-page-aspire-card-file-row-last` combo removes it — same native last-child-style pattern as `if-walk-item`, §38). **Reordered + renamed to match the mockup exactly:** 1 "Student Application Form" / "Completed by the student" + PDF badge; 2 "Project Description Form" / "Completed by the mentor" + PDF badge; 3 "Your resume" / "Including honors, awards, and publications" (no badge) — supersedes the old "Bracket Application Form" / wrong order noted in earlier session notes.
- **New "Name your files with your last name first" section** (was entirely missing): reused `program-page-aspire-card-meta-label` verbatim for the label (+ combo `-namefiles-label` for a touch of extra margin-bottom) and added 3 new **`program-page-aspire-card-filename-chip`** (light-gray rounded rectangles) reading `Smith-Application.pdf` / `Smith-ProjectDescription.pdf` / `Smith-Resume.pdf`.
- **Send To / Deadline restructured from a single-line value into multi-line runs**, matching the mockup (name above email; time above date above term):
  - Send To value: plain `<span>` "Joseph Naft" + `<br>` + `<span class="program-page-aspire-card-meta-value-red">` "aspire@umd.edu".
  - Deadline value: `<span class="…-red">` "4:00 p.m." + `<br>` + `<span class="…-red">` "September 14, 2026" + `<br>` + `<span class="program-page-aspire-card-meta-note">` "(Fall 2026)" (small gray).
  - New combo **`program-page-aspire-card-meta-value-red`** (parent `program-page-aspire-card-meta-value`, `color:#e21833`) and new class **`program-page-aspire-card-meta-note`** (gray, 11px).
  - **Both meta-rows moved into a new side-by-side wrapper** `program-page-aspire-card-meta-cols` (flex row, `justify-content:space-between`, `grid-column-gap:16px`) — the mockup shows Send-To/Deadline as two stacked-label columns sitting side by side, not two full-width rows. `program-page-aspire-card-meta-row` itself was changed from a horizontal label/value row (`justify-content:space-between`) to a vertical stack (`flex-direction:column;align-items:flex-start;grid-row-gap:4px`) plus **`flex:1 1 0%;min-width:0`** so the two columns share width evenly and the long email/date text wraps within its own column instead of overflowing into its neighbor (first attempt without the flex-basis/min-width fix showed the email butting directly into "September 14" — classic missing `min-width:0` on a flex-shrink child, same family as the HARD RULE #2 narrow-flex-child note).
- **⚠️ Builder mistake caught mid-build:** an attempt to insert "Joseph Naft" as plain text used a nested `BY_CUSTOM_TAG span` wrapping ANOTHER empty span with no `set_text` at all — produced two empty spans. Caught via `query_elements` (no String child under either span) before publishing; removed and rebuilt as one span with `set_text` directly. Same standing lesson as elsewhere in this file: re-query after every builder call, don't trust `status:"success"` alone when a JSON literal was hand-assembled for a nested structure.

**C. ⚠️⚠️ NEW LESSON — a plain `display:grid; grid-template-columns:48px 1fr` (2 columns, 2 children, no declared rows) produced ~200px phantom vertical gaps between sibling grid instances; switching to `display:flex` fixed it INSTANTLY, with zero other changes.** Each step (`program-page-aspire-step`) was a small 2-column grid (number | body) — nothing exotic, no negative offsets, no `display:contents` (i.e. NOT the specific §56 trigger combo). Symptom: gaps between steps ran ~200–235px regardless of how much text a given step held (a step with just a 1-line title left the SAME huge gap as a step with 2 full paragraphs) — a dead giveaway that the row-gap value itself wasn't the driver, confirmed by testing: dropping `program-page-aspire-step-list`'s `grid-row-gap` from `clamp(20px,2.6vw,32px)` all the way down to `8px` barely changed the visible gap at all. Converting `program-page-aspire-step` from `display:grid` (removing `grid-template-columns`) to `display:flex; align-items:flex-start` (keeping the same `grid-column-gap:18px` between number and body, which Webflow aliases as flex `column-gap` too) **eliminated the gap completely, instantly, first try** — confirmed via `element_snapshot_tool` before/after on the identical element. Finished the conversion properly: gave `program-page-aspire-step-num` an explicit `width:48px;flex-shrink:0` (replacing the grid column's implicit sizing) and created a new **`program-page-aspire-step-body`** class (`flex:1 1 0%;min-width:0`) applied to the previously-unstyled wrapper div in **all 9** step instances across BOTH step-lists on the page (How to Apply's 4 + For Mentors' 5 — same shared `program-page-aspire-step`/`-step-list` classes, so the fix and the bug both applied to both sections at once). This is also consistent with the user's own standing, explicitly-stated preference recorded in §56 ("I DO NOT LIKE WEBFLOW GRID, I prefer flexbox when at all possible") — this bug is one more concrete data point in favor of that default.
- **Root cause is NOT fully understood** (unlike §56's grid+`display:contents`+negative-offset combo, which had a specific identified trigger) — flagged honestly rather than over-claiming. What's established empirically: (1) it reproduced identically across multiple sibling grid instances in a flex-column list; (2) the row-gap property was ruled out by direct testing; (3) `display:flex` with otherwise-equivalent properties fixed it immediately; (4) no DOM-level cause (hidden elements, stray margins) was found via full-depth `query_elements` on multiple affected steps. **Standing rule going forward: if a CSS Grid layout — even a simple one, not just the `display:contents`/negative-offset combo already flagged in §56 — produces spacing that looks disproportionate to its content, don't assume the visible gap/row-gap property is the cause; test a flex conversion empirically before spending more time reading properties, since Grid itself may again be the culprit even in "boring" 2-column cases.**
- Verified end-to-end: both step-lists (How to Apply 4 steps, For Mentors 5 steps) render with clean, tight, proportional spacing; step numbers solid red; submission card matches the mockup (header bar, 3 file rows with badges, filename chips, 2-column Send-To/Deadline, submit button). Published (`publish_site`, after 2 transient 429/stream-closed retries — consistent with the documented "just retry" pattern, §2).
- New classes this session: `program-page-aspire-card-header`, `-card-header-title`, `-card-file-row` (+ `-row-last` combo), `-card-file-num`, `-card-file-info`, `-card-file-sub`, `-card-file-badge`, `-card-filenames`, `-card-filename-chip`, `-card-meta-value-red` (combo), `-card-meta-note`, `-card-meta-cols`, `-card-namefiles-label` (combo), `-step-body`. No shared-file (`idea-factory.{css,js}`) change → `stable` unaffected.
- **Follow-up (user): column ratio changed to 3/5 left (steps) : 2/5 right (submission card).** `program-page-aspire-apply-cols` `grid-template-columns` `1.4fr .6fr` (70/30) → **`3fr 2fr`** (exactly 60/40 — fr units are pure ratios, so `3fr 2fr` = 3/5 : 2/5 with no rounding). Confirmed `if-prog-card` has no width/max-width of its own, so it stretches to fill the wider track under Grid's default `justify-items:stretch`. Verified + published. **Re-litigated ("HOW DID YOU JUST SET THOSE WIDTHS. YOU ARE FUCKING AROUND AGAIN?????") before the user reloaded their Designer tab and confirmed it was correct** — same recurring family as §31H: an API-side style edit doesn't repaint an already-open Designer canvas until reloaded. Re-verified no duplicate style object existed under the name (single match, `query_styles`) before answering, per the standing "verify, don't just re-assert" discipline.
- **Follow-up (user): submission card should NOT behave like a clickable/selectable program card that grows on hover** ("That card shouldn't act like a selectable item that grows when you hover over it. Take that hover behavior off of it."). The card had kept the shared **`if-prog-card`** class after §68's `if-prog-card-stage` removal — `if-prog-card` is the sitewide clickable-program-card class whose scale/shadow hover lives in the SHARED `idea-factory.css` (§3: "Sibling established hovers… `.if-prog-card:hover{transform:scale(1.03)}`"), so simply having that class name on the element was enough to inherit the hover-grow even though the card is a static info panel, not a browsable listing. **Fix:** confirmed via `query_elements` that `if-prog-card` was applied to only this ONE element on the whole page (safe to fully decouple, not just override), created a new plain native class **`program-page-aspire-submission-card`** carrying the identical rest-state look (`display:flex;flex-direction:column;overflow:hidden;border:1px solid #e6e6e6;background-color:#fff;color:#1a1a1a;text-decoration:none`) with NO shared-CSS-matched name, and swapped the card's class list to just that one class — the shared hover rule can no longer match it at all, at the CSS-selector level, not just visually cancelled. Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`: identical rest-state appearance, confirmed class list is now `["program-page-aspire-submission-card"]` only. Published.
- **⚠️ Missing step 02, found via a clearer reference screenshot the user provided directly.** User asked "What happened to Item #2???" — the built steps read 01, 03, 04, 05 with no 02. The earlier low-res (258px-wide) recovered mockup genuinely APPEARED to skip 02 entirely (confirmed no room for a hidden item in the vertical gap between 01 and 03) — but the user then supplied a much higher-resolution crop of the SAME section showing step 02 clearly: **"02 [STUDENT pill] [MENTOR pill] Agree on a project." / "Define the project together. Mentors: this is also when you'll plan your matching-funds commitment; see For Mentors."** — this content had been WRONGLY MERGED into step 01 as its second paragraph during the original build (§68), with the "03" label never renumbered to fill the gap. **Fix:** built a new step 02 block (native `program-page-aspire-step`/`-step-body`/`-step-head`, TWO role pills side-by-side — base `program-page-aspire-step-role` "Student" + combo `-role-mentor` "Mentor" — + title "Agree on a project."), then `move_element`'d the existing misplaced paragraph (with its "For Mentors" `if-inline-link` intact) out of step 01's body into the new step 02's body, and corrected its leading sentence to match the clearer reference exactly ("This is also where you'll plan your matching funds commitment" → "Mentors: this is also when you'll plan your matching-funds commitment"). Step 01 now correctly ends after its own single paragraph. Sequence is now a complete, gap-free 01→02→03→04→05. Verified via `element_snapshot_tool` (both pills render side-by-side, text matches); published. **Lesson: when a low-resolution recovered reference and a live build agree with each other, that agreement can still be systematically wrong if the recovery itself lost real content — a clearer, freshly-provided screenshot of the same source is stronger evidence than my own earlier low-res extraction, and is worth re-checking against even after "confirming" via the blurrier version.**

## 69. ASPIRE "How to Apply" steps — pills restyled outline/white + title-first restructure + a NEW Designer-only empty-element bug found and fixed (2026‑07‑10) — native Designer, PUBLISHED

User: "I don't like those student and mentor 'pills' ... Keep the student red and the mentor black but only show them as OUTLINE and TEXT colored, and make the fill of both white. Make them smaller as well. And the placement is obnoxious: Instead give them more structure. Put the TITLE of each numbered item first. Underneath that a thin ... dark line the full width of the column. Then directly beneath that put the 'Pill(s)' as appropriate. Then a small gap. THEN the Georgia descriptive text."

**A. Pills restyled (shared classes — affects "For Mentors" too, correctly, since they're the same classes).** `program-page-aspire-step-role` (base/Student): `background-color:rgba(226,24,51,.12)`→**`#ffffff`**; added `border:1px solid #e21833`; `font-size:12px→10px`; `padding:3px 10px→2px 8px`. `program-page-aspire-step-role-mentor` (combo/Mentor): removed its own `background-color:#1a1a1a` (base's white now shows through untouched — combos only need to declare what they're OVERRIDING); added `border-color:#1a1a1a` + `color:#1a1a1a`. Net: small white-fill pills, red outline+text (Student) or black outline+text (Mentor).

**B. Structural reorder — title→divider→pills→gap→description — applied ONLY to "How to Apply"'s 5 steps, NOT "For Mentors."** Reason: "For Mentors" steps have NO separate title element at all (their pill sits directly beside ONE flowing `if-mani-p` paragraph, no bold headline) — confirmed via `query_elements` on all 5 — so the requested reorder isn't structurally possible there without inventing title text the user never provided; scope was correctly limited to the section actually being discussed. For each of the 5 How‑to‑Apply steps: `move_element`'d the title paragraph OUT of `program-page-aspire-step-head` to become the FIRST child of `program-page-aspire-step-body` (title now stands alone, no longer inline with the pill); inserted a new divider element between title and the (now pill‑only) `step-head` row. `program-page-aspire-step-title` gained explicit `margin-bottom:8px` (all 4 sides set individually, replacing the old `margin:0` shorthand, to avoid ambiguity). `program-page-aspire-step-head` (now purely the pill row) `grid-column-gap:10px→6px`, `margin-bottom:8px→12px` (the "small gap" before the description).

**C. ⚠️⚠️ NEW LESSON — a genuinely EMPTY div (zero children, zero text) gets a gray PLACEHOLDER BOX in the Webflow Designer canvas regardless of its own CSS height/border — a new trigger in the "empty-div inflation" family (§28/§31E/§34/§56), this time NOT fixed by "give it an explicit height" (§28a's own stated fix, which did NOT work here).** Built the divider as a plain `DivBlock` (`program-page-aspire-step-divider`, `height:1px;background-color:#1a1a1a;width:100%`) — `element_snapshot_tool` showed a large gray box (~90px tall) in its place instead of a thin line. **Tried, in order, all FAILED except the last:** (1) bump height 1px→2px — still inflated; (2) switch to `height:0;border-top:1px solid` (zero-height bordered line, the literal `<hr>` technique) — the border line itself now rendered correctly-positioned and correctly-thin, but a SEPARATE gray box still appeared where the div's box was; (3) add an empty `<span>` child to "de-empty" it — fixed THAT div's gray box, but the empty span itself immediately got its OWN gray-box placeholder a few lines down (just relocated the bug, didn't fix it). **ROOT CAUSE (empirically confirmed, not fully explained): the Designer's placeholder heuristic triggers on the presence of ANY zero-content element — even an invisible one — not on the element's own height/border styling.** **REAL FIX: give the element genuine TEXT content, not just a child.** Deleted the `DivBlock` entirely and rebuilt the divider as a **`Paragraph`** with `set_text:"."`, then styled the text itself invisible (`color:transparent;font-size:0px;line-height:0;overflow:hidden`) while `border-top:1px solid` supplies the actual visible line. **This fully eliminated the gray box** — confirmed via `element_snapshot_tool` (clean thin line, no placeholder) — because the element now has a genuine text node, even though that text is visually suppressed. **Verification method used throughout (per §56/§68's own standing lesson): before touching the Designer fix, independently confirmed the ORIGINAL `height:1px`+`background-color` version was ALREADY byte-correct and rendered as a perfect 1px/700px-wide line in a real headless-Chromium harness built from the site's live compiled CSS** (`curl`'d the published stylesheet, confirmed `.program-page-aspire-step-divider{border-top:...}` matched exactly, rendered with real Interstate font) — proving the Designer-canvas gray box was ALWAYS a tool/canvas-only artifact, never a real published-site defect, before spending further effort chasing a Designer-only cosmetic fix. **Standing rule, sharpened: "give an empty div an explicit height" (§28a) is NOT a universal fix for Designer placeholder-box inflation — if a normal-flow, explicitly-sized, non-absolute div STILL shows a gray box, the next thing to try is making the element a text-bearing type (Paragraph/Heading) with invisible text, not further tweaking height/border on a still-childless div.**
- All 5 dividers rebuilt as invisible-text Paragraphs; all 9 step-list steps (4 remaining "How to Apply" + all "For Mentors") automatically got the pill restyle since it's shared; only "How to Apply" got the structural reorder.
- **Follow-up (user): divider color lightened.** `border-top` color `#1a1a1a`→**`#999999`** (medium gray — still clearly visible as an intentional rule, no longer near-black).
- **Follow-up (user): step number and title now share one text baseline.** `program-page-aspire-step` (the flex row pairing the big number with the step body) `align-items:flex-start→baseline`. Since the title is now the body's FIRST child (per the §69‑B reorder), flexbox's native baseline alignment naturally locks the big red number's baseline to the title's baseline — no manual offset math needed, unlike the About page's stage‑header baseline‑lock system (§31H/§45/§53) which needed one because THAT layout couldn't use native flex/grid baseline alignment (cross‑element glyph alignment, not two flex siblings).
- Verified end-to-end via `element_snapshot_tool` after each change; published after every step. No shared-file (`idea-factory.{css,js}`) change → `stable` unaffected.

## 70. ASPIRE — inline-link wrap fix (site-wide) + gold vertical bar on "ASPIRE by the numbers" (2026‑07‑10) — native Designer, PUBLISHED

**A. "Clark School department websites" link jumped as a whole unit instead of wrapping naturally — root cause: Webflow's default `display:inline-block` on "Link" elements.** User: "This link doesn't line break so it cause the word immediately before it to be the last word on a line that could easily accept more words, creating a strange break... allow the link to wrap, so it can begin on the same line as the word before it." Confirmed via `element_snapshot_tool` on the live element: "Browse faculty research interests on the" ended line 1 with a large empty gap, then the ENTIRE 4-word link "Clark School department websites" jumped to line 2 as one block. Checked `if-inline-link`'s Designer properties (color/font-weight/letter-spacing/text-decoration only, no `white-space`/`display`) and the actual text content (verified byte-for-byte via Python — plain `U+0020` spaces, no hidden non-breaking spaces) — neither explained it. **Root cause: Webflow's "Link" element type defaults to `display:inline-block` unless a class overrides it** — an inline-block with no explicit width sizes to fit its own content on one internal line, and then behaves as ONE ATOMIC BOX when the surrounding paragraph's text flows around it (this is standard CSS: an inline-block is opaque to the surrounding line-breaking algorithm). **Fix:** added `display:inline; white-space:normal` to `if-inline-link` (both are otherwise-default values, but explicit here to override the element-type default) — a `<a>` with real `display:inline` is NOT an atomic box, so the browser can break between any of the link's own words at normal word boundaries, same as plain text. **Verified via the live published compiled CSS + `element_snapshot_tool` after publish:** "Browse faculty research interests on the Clark School department" now correctly fills line 1, with only "websites" (the last word) wrapping to line 2 — exactly the requested behavior. `if-inline-link` is used in multiple places sitewide (§20/§25/etc.) — this fix is universally safe since both properties are otherwise CSS-default values for a plain `<a>`, so it can only correct the inline-block default, never regress a genuinely-intended inline-block usage (there is none for this class). Published.
- **Standing lesson: when a multi-word Webflow "Link" element refuses to wrap internally and jumps as a whole unit, suspect the ELEMENT TYPE's default `display` (inline-block for "Link"), not just the applied class's own properties — check by forcing `display:inline` explicitly, which is always a safe no-op-or-fix change for a plain hyperlink use case.**

**B. Gold vertical bar added to "ASPIRE by the numbers," reusing the Home "The proof" section's exact pattern.** User: "Add the gold vertical bar to the right side of this section as in 'The proof' section in the Homepage." Found the reference structure on Home (`if-bar-wrap`[aria-hidden] › `if-bar-inner` › `if-proof-goldline` — the same shared global classes already reused on the About page's stage headers, §35‑B) and inserted an identical instance as the first child of the ASPIRE `if-proof-sec` (id `07c3a01a…`). Zero new classes — pure reuse of an already-proven pattern, so no risk of the "empty div inflation" issue that hit the brand-new step-divider (§69‑C): `if-proof-goldline` has been live in multiple places already and never flagged. Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`: gold bar appears flush at the section's right edge, matching Home's look exactly. Published.
- No shared-file (`idea-factory.{css,js}`) change for either fix → `stable` unaffected.

## 71. ASPIRE submission card — PDF badges get a down-arrow + hover color-flip (2026‑07‑10) — native Designer, PUBLISHED

User sent a screenshot of the "PDF" file-type badge with a down arrow inside it and said: "Those PDF buttons are supposed to have a down arrow in them... And they flip colors on hover (red fill, white text)."

- **Arrow:** both badges' text changed from plain `"PDF"` to `"PDF ↓"` (`set_text` on the two String children of `program-page-aspire-card-file-badge`, ids `04c04c18…` and `2bcc8cdb…`) — a literal Unicode down-arrow character appended with a space, matching the screenshot's spacing.
- **Hover flip:** added a `hover` pseudo-state to the shared `program-page-aspire-card-file-badge` style: `background-color:#e21833; border-color:#e21833; color:#ffffff` — flips the rest-state look (white fill, red outline + red text) to a solid red fill with white text, leaving the rest-state properties (padding, font, border-radius, etc.) untouched.
- Both badges share this one class, so the fix applies to both PDF badges on the submission card in one edit — no per-instance work needed.
- **Verified:** re-queried both String children before trusting the `set_text` success response (per the standing "class defined ≠ applied, always re-query" discipline) — both confirmed `"PDF ↓"`; `element_snapshot_tool` on the submission card shows both badges rendering "PDF ↓" cleanly inside their red-outline boxes. The hover pseudo-state can't be captured in a static Designer snapshot (snapshots don't render `:hover`), so it was confirmed via the `update_style` response itself, which echoed back the exact `hover` properties saved to the style object.
- No shared-file (`idea-factory.{css,js}`) change → `stable` unaffected. Published.

## 72. ASPIRE "How to Apply" two-column grid — later stacking threshold + equal-width tablet split (2026‑07‑10) — native Designer, PUBLISHED

User: "On the How to apply section I don't [want] the two columns responsively stacking until the horizontal phone size, on the tablet size keep them two column but make each column 50% there."

- **Root cause:** `program-page-aspire-apply-cols` (the shared grid container behind both the "How to Apply" step-list/submission-card split and the "For Mentors" step-list/sidebar split — confirmed via `query_elements`, both sections use this exact class) had only a `main` value (`3fr 2fr`) and a single `medium` (≤991) override (`1fr`) — so it collapsed to one column starting at tablet width, with no intermediate two-column tablet state.
- **Fix:** `medium` (≤991, tablet) breakpoint changed `1fr` → **`1fr 1fr`** (equal 50/50 split, two columns retained); added a new **`small`** (≤767, phone-landscape) breakpoint override with `1fr` (stacks to one column starting there instead). The `main`-breakpoint `3fr 2fr` ratio (§68) is untouched.
- **Shared-class side effect, checked deliberately:** since "For Mentors" uses the identical class, this also moved its stacking threshold from medium→small. Verified via an offline real-browser harness (built from the live compiled CSS + the actual queried DOM for both sections) at 1100/991/900/767/700/400px — at every width both sections' `grid-template-columns` and child geometry matched exactly (e.g. 900px: both split 408px/408px with no overflow; 767px and below: both stack to a full-width single column). The "For Mentors" photo + two funding-stat boxes hold up cleanly at the 50%-width tablet column, so the shared behavior was left as one class rather than forking a duplicate — flagged to the user rather than silently deciding.
- **Verified:** real-browser geometry check (`docWidth === viewportWidth` at every tested width, confirming zero page-level horizontal overflow) plus `element_snapshot_tool` on the Designer canvas at the default/main breakpoint (unaffected, still 3fr/2fr) per HARD RULE #0. Published.
- No shared-file (`idea-factory.{css,js}`) change → `stable` unaffected.

## 73. Site-wide DEFAULT link style (auto-apply to brand-new links) — investigated, requires ONE manual Project Settings step (2026‑07‑10) — hand-off, no site change shipped

User asked for a true zero-click default: whenever anyone selects a word in body text and turns it into a link (Webflow's default gives blue + underline), it should automatically come out **Interstate, weight 700 (the "heavy weight" already chosen in §20), letter-spacing −0.4px (the sizing already tuned in §20 to blend with Georgia), color `#e21833` (brand red), no underline** — without applying any class by hand.

- **This EXACT look already exists as a class** — `if-inline-link` (style id `b19bcee7-daa1-abbf-3314-78d964d4c195`): `font-family:Interstate,"Helvetica Neue",Arial,sans-serif; font-weight:700; letter-spacing:-0.4px; color:#e21833; text-decoration:none`. Nothing new needed there.
- **Tested whether the Style Manager (Designer classes, MCP-editable) can make this auto-apply to a brand-new unclassed link.** Webflow's class system has no "default class for freshly-created elements of type X" mechanism — a class only ever styles elements it's explicitly applied to. Confirmed empirically: created a test style literally named `Link` (the same name as the element type) via `create_style`, published, and checked the live compiled CSS — it emitted an ordinary `.link{...}` class selector, NOT a bare tag selector (`a{...}`). So naming a class after the tag does **not** make it a tag-level default; it's just another class nobody would ever apply automatically. Deleted the test style immediately and republished clean (confirmed `.link{}` gone from the compiled CSS) — no lasting change from this experiment.
- **The genuine native mechanism for "every new unclassed link gets this look with zero clicks" is Webflow's Site Settings → Fonts tab → the sitewide typography defaults (Heading 1–6 / Paragraph / Body / Links).** This is a real, fully-native, Designer-visible feature — but it lives in **Project Settings**, the same surface as Custom Code and the Adobe Fonts kit, which MCP cannot edit (§2: "MCP cannot edit Project Settings... Only embed elements, Designer styles, and pages are MCP-editable"). None of the available tools (`data_style_tool`, `data_fonts_tool` [[custom font uploads only]], `designer_tool` [[canvas navigation only]], `data_sites_tool` [[site meta/publish only]]) expose it.
- **Why this is still the RIGHT fix, not a workaround:** a tag-level default set here compiles to a plain, low-specificity `a{...}` rule. Every existing classed link on the site (`if-nav-link`, `if-foot-link`, `if-inline-link`, buttons, etc.) has its own class-based rule, which is higher-specificity and keeps winning — so this change is additive and safe: it only affects links that currently have **no class at all**, i.e. exactly the brand-new links the user is asking about, and nothing already built breaks.

**⭐ WHAT'S NEEDED FROM THE USER (one-time, ~1 minute, in the Webflow Designer):**
Open **Site Settings → Fonts tab** → find the typography-defaults section (lists Heading 1–6, Paragraph, and Links) → under **Links**, set:
- **Font:** Interstate
- **Weight:** 700 / Bold
- **Letter spacing:** −0.4px (if that field isn't offered for links specifically, skip it — color + weight + no-underline is the bulk of the effect)
- **Color:** `#E21833`
- **Underline:** off / none

Once set and published, any newly-inserted plain link (in any body text, Georgia or otherwise — this is a sitewide tag default, not scoped to just the serif classes) will render this way automatically, with no class required. Tell me once it's done and I'll re-fetch the compiled CSS to confirm the exact `a{}` rule landed correctly and spot-check it renders right.

## 74. ASPIRE Deadlines page — real alert boxes (bell icon + crosshatch fill) + evaluation-criteria checklist fixed (white boxes/checkmarks → plain red bullets, real Mentor pill, proper title) (2026‑07‑10) — native Designer + 1 new SVG asset, PUBLISHED

**A. Alert boxes ("Financial aid" / "Summer commitments") built, then corrected twice after real mistakes — final state below.** User sent a reference screenshot: pale-yellow crosshatch boxes, a black icon square with a bell glyph, bold lead-in text. The two boxes (`program-page-aspire-dl-note`, in `program-page-aspire-dl-notes-row`) previously had only a plain paragraph — no icon, white bg, thin gray border.
- **⚠️ MISTAKE 1 (user, furious, rightly): used a self-designed/uploaded bell icon instead of the one the user had already placed in Webflow Assets.** User's own message said "add the alert (bell) icon in Webflow assets" — meaning "use the one I put there," not "go make one." Never checked the Assets panel first; hand-drew a gold bell SVG and uploaded it as a brand-new asset instead. **Fix:** `list_assets` found the user's own `alert.svg` (id `6a5081e6db9d02d23eec4ba6`, uploaded ~6 minutes before my own upload — confirming it was already sitting there, unchecked) — a proper red (`#e21833`) bell-with-motion-lines glyph. Swapped both note-box images to this asset via `set_image_asset`, then deleted the wrongly-created asset (`delete_asset`) since it was my own error, unused, and pure clutter. **Standing lesson: when a user says an asset is "placed in Webflow assets," that is a direct instruction to check the Assets panel BEFORE creating anything — never assume a needed asset doesn't exist without listing/searching first.**
- **⚠️ MISTAKE 2: invented an unrequested black square container around the icon.** This was my own unprompted inference from the reference screenshot (which showed a black square holding a "!" character) generalized into "build a black badge, then swap in whatever icon" — never confirmed with the user, and wrong once a real (already-colored, already-legible) bell asset was in play. **Fix:** removed `background-color` from `program-page-aspire-alert-icon` entirely; the wrapper now just sizes/positions the icon (32×32, no fill) so the red bell sits directly on the crosshatch background. Directly acknowledged to the user, when asked "who told you to do that," that no one did — it was an unconfirmed design decision of my own.
- **⚠️ MISTAKE 3: border didn't match the sibling element using the same fill.** First cut used `border:2px solid #1a1a1a` (heavy black), which the user explicitly said they don't want (a recurring, previously-stated preference — see §65's "no black outlines" standing rule). **Fix:** queried `program-page-aspire-dl-cell` (the Deadlines table cell that also uses this exact crosshatch fill, via its `-current` combo) — its border is `1px solid #e6e6e6`, the site's standard subtle-edge tone. Applied that same `1px solid #e6e6e6` to `program-page-aspire-dl-note` so the two crosshatch-filled elements match.
- **⚠️ MISTAKE 4 (the same §32-E class of bug, now a 4th documented occurrence): left the alert-box body text on the inherited Georgia from `if-prog-desc` without checking whether it was appropriate for this new context.** User asked directly "What FONT are you using there and why" — the honest answer was Georgia, inherited, never actively reconsidered. On reflection this is wrong for the same reason §32-E exists: an alert/warning notice is functional system UI (like labels, pills, badges — all Interstate sitewide), not narrative/editorial body copy (what Georgia is reserved for). **User then pointed out, correctly, that this exact checking discipline has been told to me at least 3 times before** (§32-E itself, re-flagged in §61, re-flagged again in §65-E, each time as "check font-family whenever a class is reused in a new context — don't wait to be caught") — this is the 4th instance of the same failure, not a new lesson. **Fix:** new scoped combo `program-page-aspire-alert-text` (parent `if-prog-desc`, `font-family:"Interstate","Helvetica Neue",Arial,sans-serif`) applied to both note paragraphs — leaves the shared `if-prog-desc` class (used in many genuinely-Georgia contexts elsewhere) untouched. **This is not a new rule to record — it's a record that the existing rule (§32-E) is not being applied proactively and needs to be run as an explicit checklist item on every text-element touch, not treated as remembered.**
- **New classes (final):** `program-page-aspire-alert-icon` (32×32, flex-centered, `flex-shrink:0`, no background), `program-page-aspire-alert-icon-img` (32×32), `program-page-aspire-alert-text` (combo, Interstate). Inserted as the first child of each note box via `data_element_builder` (prepend).
- **`program-page-aspire-dl-note` final style:** `background-color:rgba(255,210,0,0.7)` + `background-image:repeating-linear-gradient(170deg,rgba(255,255,255,.55) 0,rgba(255,255,255,.55) .5px,transparent .5px,transparent 2.5px)` (the established §65-A crosshatch recipe, reused verbatim) + `border:1px solid #e6e6e6`.
- **Bold lead-in:** each note's lead phrase ("Financial aid:" / "Summer commitments:") split off into its own `<strong>` (existing text trimmed via `set_text`, then the strong prepended via `data_element_builder`) — `if-prog-desc` is already in the §31-A bold-Georgia→weight-matched-Interstate list, so no new CSS was needed for the bold treatment to render correctly.
- **Verification note (from the since-superseded first pass, still a valid lesson):** `element_snapshot_tool` initially showed the (wrong, self-made) icon square as solid black with NO visible bell — the same known Designer-canvas asset-loading quirk documented in §31 ("`element_snapshot` does NOT render a freshly-swapped image asset"), not a real defect in that asset. The user's own pre-placed `alert.svg` rendered correctly in the snapshot immediately (likely already cached from when they uploaded it), which is what surfaced the black-box and border problems clearly enough for them to react to.

**B. ⚠️ User (furious) flagged the "How applications are evaluated" checklist as broken — investigated honestly before fixing.** Screenshot showed 4 items each in a white bordered box with a black-square-white-checkmark icon, plus "The amount of matching funds offered (mentor)" as plain parenthetical text instead of a real pill. User accused reused/misapplied styling.
- **Checked first, answered honestly:** `program-page-aspire-elig-item`/`program-page-aspire-elig-check` were **not** touched anywhere in this session — they're pre-existing classes from the original Eligibility-section build (§63), and their reuse for the Deadlines evaluation-criteria list was a **deliberate, documented decision from that same original build** (§63-D: "a reused eligibility-style checklist for evaluation criteria"), not something introduced by the alert-box work just done. Said so plainly rather than fabricating an admission.
- **But DID find a real instance of the underlying complaint:** the "How applications are evaluated" heading was styled `program-page-aspire-card-h` — the **submission card's own small internal label class** (20px, meant for "What to send: 3 files" inside a card) — reused for what should be a standalone page-level section title. This is exactly the "same style applied to other parts inappropriately" pattern the user was angry about, just not in the place first assumed.
- **Fix — both shared classes updated (affects the ORIGINAL Eligibility section too, since it uses the same classes; checked this was correct rather than assuming, see below):**
  - `program-page-aspire-elig-item`: removed `background-color`/`border` entirely, `padding` → `0` (rows now separated only by the list's own `grid-row-gap:18px`, no box/chrome at all).
  - `program-page-aspire-elig-check`: resized `24×24`→`10×10`, recolored `#1a1a1a`→`#e21833` (brand red), removed the now-unused `color`/`font-size`/`font-weight` (no glyph rendered inside anymore), `margin-top:7px` to sit near the first line's cap-height.
  - **⚠️ Checkmark glyphs hidden, NOT deleted — avoids the empty-div Designer-canvas inflation bug (§28/§31E/§69-C).** Each check square's inner span still holds its original "✓" text (removing it would leave a genuinely empty div, the exact trigger for that recurring bug); instead a new class **`program-page-aspire-elig-checkmark`** (`font-size:0;line-height:0;color:transparent;overflow:hidden`) was applied to all 9 inner spans (5 in Eligibility + 4 in the reused list) — same "keep real text content, hide it visually" technique §69-C established for the stage-header divider.
  - New class **`program-page-aspire-eval-title`** (Interstate 800, `font-size:clamp(22px,2.6vw,28px)`, `color:#1a1a1a`, `margin-top:clamp(32px,4vw,48px)` for the requested spacing from the alert boxes above, `margin-bottom:clamp(16px,2vw,24px)`) replaces `program-page-aspire-card-h` on the "How applications are evaluated" heading.
  - **"(mentor)" → real pill:** trimmed the trailing text to "The amount of matching funds offered " and appended a `<span class="program-page-aspire-step-role program-page-aspire-step-role-mentor">Mentor</span>` — the exact same pill component used in "How to Apply"/"For Mentors" (§69). `program-page-aspire-step-role` is `display:inline-block`, so it drops into running paragraph text cleanly (verified: sits inline at the end of the line, not on its own row).
- **Verified the shared-class fix on BOTH reuses** (not assumed): `element_snapshot_tool` on the Eligibility section confirms its 5 items also now show clean red square bullets with no boxes — the fix is a genuine improvement to both, not a scope mistake.
- New classes this entry: `program-page-aspire-alert-icon`, `program-page-aspire-alert-icon-img`, `program-page-aspire-elig-checkmark`, `program-page-aspire-eval-title`. No shared-file (`idea-factory.{css,js}`) change → `stable` unaffected. Published throughout, re-verified against the live compiled CSS after each batch.

**NEXT SESSION: keep maintaining this file per the OPERATING PROTOCOL, and pass that instruction on.** §31H's "LOCKED" vertical‑spacing note is superseded by §48‑B/§49's native clamp formula (§48‑B's JS module is now a refinement layer on top of it, not the fix) — if margins ever look off again, **check the Designer canvas first, not just the published/JS‑enabled site**, and fix the native formula before reaching for JS. **§49 is the standing lesson for this whole project: a JS-only fix for a natively-expressible property is not acceptable even temporarily — verify in the Designer via `element_snapshot_tool` before calling anything done, every time, no exceptions.** §51 is a second standing lesson: **when several related pieces share a responsive transition, check they share ONE window (HARD RULE #2) EVERY time any one of them is resized — a scale-up pass (§45) that only touches MAX values can silently leave MIN values disproportionate at the narrow end, invisible until someone actually looks at a phone-width Designer view.** §52 is a third: **fixing an "too much space" complaint by shrinking ONE side of a padding box, without checking it against the OTHER sides/gaps it will be compared to, trades one visible flaw for another — check the whole balance, not just the one flagged number.** §57 is a fourth: **"shrink the low end" is not automatically "one continuous clamp across the whole range" — when the user scopes a fix to "phone‑sized views only," that means a native breakpoint‑specific override (e.g. `small` ≤767) with its ceiling solved to match the untouched wider‑range formula at the boundary, not a single clamp stretched from 320 all the way to 1440.** §61 is a fifth: **before inventing a new Designer class, check whether an existing sitewide `if-` class already provides the needed look/spacing/token (even just its padding/underline‑treatment values) and reuse it via a minimal color‑only combo — this is now a standing practice, not a one‑off for the ASPIRE hero.** §62 is a sixth and seventh: **(a) when reusing an existing class for its spacing/sizing, separately check its FONT-FAMILY against the black‑vs‑white Georgia rule (§32‑E) — reuse doesn't imply the font is automatically right for the new context; (b) a "gap" between two elements can be governed by TWO different CSS properties on two different containers (an inner row's own gap + an outer column's gap-to-next-sibling) — tightening one is not tightening "the gap," check both explicitly.** §66 adds an eighth and ninth: **(a) a scoped combo overriding a shared class's property doesn't just leave the shared class's OTHER breakpoints alone — its un‑mediaqueried rule outranks them via specificity everywhere, so mirror the override at every breakpoint the base class itself uses; (b) `data_element_builder`'s style key is `set_style:{style_names:[...]}`, not a bare `style_names` field — a wrong shape is silently dropped, not rejected, so always re‑check via `query_elements` after building, not just after `set_style`.** §68 adds a tenth: **CSS Grid can silently produce disproportionate vertical gaps between sibling instances even in a "boring" 2-column case with no `display:contents`/negative-offset involved — if a Grid layout's spacing looks wrong, empirically test a flex conversion before trusting a row-gap/property-level read, and prefer flexbox by default per the user's standing preference (§56).** §70 adds an eleventh: **when a multi-word Webflow "Link" element won't wrap internally and jumps as one atomic unit, suspect the ELEMENT TYPE's default `display:inline-block` (Webflow's default for "Link"), not just the applied class — forcing `display:inline` explicitly is a safe, idiomatic fix for any plain in-text hyperlink.** And a standing process lesson: **when a user disputes a specific pixel/alignment claim, don't just re-assert the number — render an ANNOTATED screenshot with the actual measured reference lines overlaid and send it, so they can see the evidence directly instead of taking your word for it.** **§74 adds a twelfth, the most important one to internalize because it is a REPEAT, not a new lesson: the §32-E font-family-on-reuse check (also cited in §62, §65-E) has now failed a 4th time — checking it "when it comes to mind" is not working. Treat font-family verification as a MANDATORY, explicit step every single time an existing class is applied to a new element or a new text treatment is built, run BEFORE publishing, not after the user asks "what font is that and why." §74 also has three more concrete, avoidable mistakes worth re-reading in full before any icon/asset work: (a) when a user says an asset "is placed in Webflow assets," that is an instruction to run `list_assets`/search FIRST — never design or upload a replacement without checking; (b) never invent a container/background/chrome element (e.g. a colored badge behind an icon) from your own reading of a reference image without it being separately asked for — a reference screenshot shows one possible treatment, not a spec for every element that superficially resembles it; (c) when told to match a border/fill to "the same as" another element, go query that other element's actual CSS value — don't estimate or reuse a value from a different, unrelated part of the site.**

## 76. ⚠️⚠️ SITEWIDE `clamp()` → FLAT PER-BREAKPOINT PX VALUES (2026‑07‑10) — the user's stated reason, the duplicate-style-object crisis it uncovered, HARD RULE #3, and the ASPIRE discrepancy cleanup — native Designer, PUBLISHED

**Why this happened (user's own words, stated forcefully):** Webflow's native Designer "Size" field cannot display or edit a `clamp()`-based value — it just shows "0". The user's explicit reasoning: **"THIS IS NOT STAYING IN YOUR CONTROL. This is a SETUP project… THIS SITE WILL BE MANUALLY MANAGED."** Every fluid `clamp()` sitewide (and on the ASPIRE page) had to become a flat, static px number **per breakpoint** (`main`/`medium`/`small`/`tiny`) so a human can directly edit it in the Designer UI going forward, with **zero** loss of the responsive stepping the clamps used to provide continuously.

**Method (repeatable if this is ever needed again elsewhere):** (1) curl the live compiled CSS; (2) parse it into `main`/`medium`/`small`/`tiny` chunks by locating the real `@media` blocks (Webflow's own, not guessed numbers); (3) expand `padding`/`margin` shorthand into `-top/-right/-bottom/-left` longhand BEFORE filtering, so partially-fluid box properties aren't misapplied uniformly; (4) for every remaining `clamp(min, Nvw, max)`, evaluate it at each breakpoint's canonical width (`main`=1440, `medium`=991, `small`=767, `tiny`=479) via a small recursive-descent `calc()`/`clamp()`/`min()`/`max()` evaluator; (5) walk the CSS cascade correctly — a narrower breakpoint may have its own genuine non-fluid override that must be preserved, not clobbered by the naive flat conversion. **One real trap hit:** `.if-move-pill`'s `width` used a **percentage** (`clamp(110px, 28%, 200px)`) — `%` resolves against the actual container's pixel width, not the viewport, so it can't be hand-computed; had to measure it in a real Playwright harness against the real DOM structure (main=158.8px, medium/small=200px, **tiny=100% — a genuine pre-existing non-fluid override, correctly left untouched**).

**⚠️⚠️ THE CRISIS — a rule that ALREADY EXISTED (verify before claiming done) was bypassed at scale, which is why HARD RULE #3 above now exists.** After applying ~107 sitewide selector fixes across 22 batches and reporting the task complete, a later audit found **8 selectors still serving `clamp()` on the live, published site** despite every `update_style` call having returned "success." Root cause: Webflow allows **multiple style objects to share one class name** (§11's old "harmless duplicate" finding was WRONG for these — only ONE of the two/three objects actually compiles into the live CSS), and `update_style`/`rename_style`/`remove_style` are **BY-NAME ONLY** with no style-ID targeting — so the fix call kept landing on a dead orphan while the real, live-compiling object sat untouched with its original `clamp()`. **Worse: the "8 known duplicates" list itself was incomplete** — a full mechanical sweep (re-fetch fresh compiled CSS, check literally every one of the ~800 selector/property/breakpoint combinations against it, not a sample) turned up a **9th** (`if-event-feat-title`) that had been missed entirely, plus **3 selectors** (`if-hero-left`, `if-syn-loop-icon`, `if-stage-head-sec.if-stage-02`) where only the `main` breakpoint had actually been sent in the original batch run — the others silently never fired. **The lesson, stated as its own hard rule (see the banner at the top of this file, HARD RULE #3): spot-checking a handful after a bulk operation is not verification — it is exactly the gap that let a 100+-selector "done" task ship with 12 real defects hiding in it.**

**The safe fix for a duplicate-name collision (repeatable procedure):** `rename_style` the object that bare-name resolution currently targets → **re-query by both the old and new name** to confirm which object is the real (still-`clamp()`-bearing) one and which is the isolated orphan → apply the actual fix to the now-uniquely-addressable real object → re-query again to confirm the value actually changed. **If a third (or further) same-named object exists, repeat the rename step — don't assume two is the ceiling** (`if-events-wrap` needed THREE rename passes before the real object was uniquely addressable: one dead orphan + one dead empty combo were both in the way). Confirmed a `false→false→false` string of identical `rename_style`/bare-name resolutions is deterministic (whichever object gets hit first, gets hit first every time), which is what makes this isolate-by-rename procedure reliable.

**Verification actually run (not asserted) before calling this done:** fresh compiled-CSS fetch → wrote a script that checks EVERY ONE of the ~800 (selector, property, breakpoint) combinations from the original clamp-extraction against the live text (comma-separated multi-selector Webflow rules had to be split properly, or the script produces its own false positives — hit this once, `.if-hero-left, .if-hero-left.if-hero-inner{padding:28px}` legitimately shares one rule since both values happened to coincide at that breakpoint) → **result: 0 remaining `clamp()`/`vw`, 0 missing declarations, 0 value mismatches, sitewide AND on the ASPIRE page.** Also ran an 8-width (320–1440px) horizontal-overflow + JS-error check across Home/Students/About/Faculty post-conversion: **0 overflow, 0 errors, every page, every width** — confirms the flattening didn't silently break responsive layout anywhere.

**Cleanup note (honest, not swept under the rug):** 10 orphan style objects were isolated by rename (`*-zzz-orphan` suffix) so they can never again collide with a live class name. 3 were successfully deleted via `remove_style`; the other **7 consistently fail with a genuine, non-transient "internal error"** (retried twice, identical failure both times) — they are confirmed harmless (uniquely named now, zero elements, zero effect on any compiled CSS), but could not be fully purged from the Designer's style list this session. If a future session has a working `remove_style` for these, the orphan names are: `if-aud-wrap-zzz-orphan`, `if-news-wrap-zzz-orphan` — wait, some of these were already deleted; check current state via `query_styles` before assuming any specific one still needs removal.

**ASPIRE layout-guide discrepancy pass (same session, after the clamp work):** recovered the ASPIRE layout-guide reference images from `scratchpad/layoutcrops/slice_0.png`–`slice_5.png` (saved from an earlier extraction) and fixed 7 confirmed gaps: contact-card italic caption + real underlined `mailto:` link (had to explicitly set `color` on the new link class — Webflow's browser-default link blue otherwise wins, same family as the old `.if-nav-sublink.w--current` blue-neutralization bug from §13); "Where ASPIRE Fits" — marked the Blueprint item `is-current` (native combo, not JS-toggled, since this page's anchors point to `/about#stage-0X`, a different page, so the About page's own scroll-spy JS never runs here) + added the "ASPIRE is a **Blueprint**-stage program…" caption; For Mentors — added the missing italic funding-caption, the "download the form" link in step 02, the "contact Joseph Naft" closing line, and fixed the funding stat boxes from a mismatched red+gold pairing to the guide's correct both-gold (by swapping which existing combo is applied on that one instance — never edited the shared `-stat-red`/`-stat-gold` classes themselves, since those are also used, correctly, on the hero); After the Award — added a red top-rule to all 4 columns + linkified "Undergraduate Research Day". **One item NOT done:** a "5th eligibility criterion" flagged as missing earlier in the session could not be verified — the recovered layout-guide slices only cover Deadlines-onward, not the Eligibility section, so there's no source material for its exact wording. Left as 4 criteria rather than fabricate a program requirement.
- **⚠️ A real bug was caught and fixed mid-verification, not just claimed fixed:** the closing-line paragraph was first appended as a THIRD sibling into `program-page-aspire-apply-cols` (a 2-column CSS grid, `3fr 2fr`, no explicit row template) — with 3 children instead of 2, the browser's default grid auto-flow would have placed it into row-1/col-2 and pushed the real photo+funding sidebar down into row-2/col-1, silently breaking the layout. Caught by reading the actual grid CSS before trusting the text-presence check, fixed by moving the paragraph to be the LAST CHILD of the step-list (a plain `flex-direction:column` stack) instead of a sibling of it — same section, correct column, no grid disruption.
- **⚠️ A Designer-canvas-vs-published divergence was hit again** (same family as §17/§18/§56): `element_snapshot_tool` rendered the "01 BLUEPRINT" current-marker box as a solid red fill, which looked wrong against the documented spec (white bg + red left bar, §44). Did NOT trust either surface blindly — checked the actual compiled CSS text (correct: `background-color:#fff`), then rendered the REAL published HTML+CSS in a real Chromium instance and read the actual computed styles AND took a screenshot: both confirmed the published site is exactly right (white bg, red inset bar). The published output is what matters; the snapshot tool's divergence here was its own rendering quirk, not a live defect — but it was verified, not assumed, either way.
- All new ASPIRE classes this pass: `program-page-aspire-after-col`, `program-page-aspire-contact-link`, `program-page-aspire-contact-caption`, `program-page-aspire-fund-caption`, `program-page-aspire-closing-line`. No shared-file (`idea-factory.{css,js}`) change from the ASPIRE-discrepancy pass itself.
- **Task #32 (sitewide 16px-minimum font-size audit) is untouched by this session** — checked whether the clamp-flattening introduced any NEW sub-16px values (it did not; the ~19 sub-16px font-sizes found, e.g. `if-move-pill`, `cta-fine`, the nav eyebrows, are all small-label/fine-print classes whose `clamp()` floor was already below 16px before this session touched anything) — still a separate, pending, lower-priority design decision for the user, not a regression to fix here.

**NEXT SESSION: HARD RULE #3 (top of this file) is now the standing, procedural, checklist-form version of "verify before claiming done" — run it in FULL after every task, especially any bulk/multi-selector style change. Don't spot-check. Don't trust a tool's "success" response for style writes without a `query_styles` re-check. If Webflow ever again shows more than one style object sharing a name, isolate by rename BEFORE editing, and check for a third/fourth collision before assuming two is the ceiling.**

Found via directly searching the raw session transcript (`.jsonl`) for the instruction after the user asked "did you forget the rebuild?" — confirmed rather than denied, since the transcript doesn't lie. **Lesson: when a user disputes a specific past instruction, grep the actual transcript file before answering — don't rely on a compacted summary's framing, which had reduced this to a small pending-items list rather than the wholesale-rebuild demand it actually was.**

This is what caught the black-vs-red Contact bug (invisible from the guide-comparison alone, only visible once my own change was actually rendered and looked at) — reinforces HARD RULE #3 item 5's "if the primary visual-check tool is down, say so and use a verified fallback, don't skip the check."  Also re-ran the 8-width overflow/JS-error check on the full ASPIRE page post-rebuild: **0 overflow, 0 errors at every width, 320–1440px.**
