## 94. ⭐ SPINOFF HEADER LOGO CONVENTION (user‑stated design rule, 2026‑07‑24) — logo → parent brand hub; program‑name text → that spinoff's own home. Applied to MIPS/ASPIRE/Minor. Plus: MIPS nav wired for 3 new pages, `data_pages_tool` confirmed fully blocked this session.

**The rule (user's own words, generalized):** Idea Factory is the **parent/carrier entity** for the shared branding (the "Idea Factory" logo mark). Every spinoff/program page (MIPS, ASPIRE, Minor, and any future one) is conceptually a child of that umbrella, and — critically — once a spinoff is actually **separated** (Duplicate Site into its own standalone site, §4), the shared Idea Factory logo showing in its header is no longer "this site's own home button." So the split is:
- **Master Idea Factory pages** (Home/Students/About/Faculty) — logo → **that site's own homepage**. Unchanged; this was already correct (§12).
- **Every spinoff/program page** (MIPS, ASPIRE, Minor, future ones) — logo → **external `https://mtech.umd.edu/`** (new tab, `target=_blank rel=noopener`, matching the sitewide "external entity link" convention, §15). The **program‑name text block beside the logo** (title + gold bar underneath — the `if-hdrprog`/`program-page-<name>-hdrprog*` header block built in §59‑62) is what actually functions as **that spinoff's own "go home" link**, since the spinoff has no distinct graphical logo of its own — the program name IS its local identity mark, and it's what the "local" nav menu revolves around once separated.

**What was actually found (not just a MIPS mistake — a gap on all three):** queried all 3 independent nav components (`Main Nav — MIPS` `9779561a‑0d8e‑01e2‑0c30‑9ce78455d782`, `Main Nav — ASPIRE` `c2a30d5f‑30b2‑2b97‑a18d‑49f791856b25`, `Main Nav — Minor` `f21fb1f7‑0f86‑19fa‑34eb‑231436b095fe`) before touching anything. None matched the rule: MIPS's logo had just been (wrongly) set to `/mips-home`; ASPIRE's and Minor's logos were still unlinked placeholders (`href="#"`, `linkType:"none"`) — i.e. never wired to `mtech.umd.edu` at all. All three `hdrprog` title blocks (MIPS "Maryland Industrial Partnerships (MIPS)", ASPIRE "ASPIRE Program", Minor "Minor in Technology Entrepreneurship & Corporate Innovation") already existed with the gold bar, but as **plain Paragraphs inside a plain Block — not clickable**.

**Fix technique (a plain Paragraph/Block can't take a link — `set_link` only works on button/text‑link/link‑block types):** used `data_element_builder`'s nested‑children support to build, in ONE atomic call per component, a **new `LinkBlock`** carrying the SAME classes as the old wrapper (`program-page-<name>-hdrprog-name if-hdrprog-name`), with a fresh `Paragraph` child (`program-page-<name>-hdrprog-title if-hdrprog-title`, exact original text incl. its literal embedded newlines) and a fresh empty `DivBlock` child (`program-page-<name>-hdrprog-bar`, the gold bar) — inserted `before` the old plain Block as anchor, then the old Block removed (`remove_element`, cascades its old children away too). Link set atomically at creation (`set_link: {link_type:"url", link:"/mips-home"|"/aspire-home"|"/minor-home"}`). Logo links retargeted via `set_link` → `https://mtech.umd.edu/` + `set_attributes` → `target=_blank`/`rel=noopener`; stale leftover `href="#"` attributes cleaned up on all three (the well‑known "resolved `link` setting is authoritative, but the stale static attribute lingers" quirk, §63).
- New element IDs: MIPS hdrprog‑name Link `2203f3d2‑128c‑dbdd‑1c17‑163e30128a43`; ASPIRE `7b407578‑c74e‑ef04‑18ce‑d80f0b821603`; Minor `d917f8a8‑6865‑09d7‑74b2‑3c4ab5dd08c0`. Old removed Blocks: MIPS `e2b98b18‑81c6‑f24c‑f64b‑1a24e2f1256b`, ASPIRE `4a545b0b‑62a1‑67a1‑cfa6‑6ceea7d41742`, Minor `a7a0b0a3‑5799‑52f3‑3b61‑65e3b496fb45`.
- **Verified independently for all 3** (fresh `query_elements` re‑fetch, not the "success" response alone, per HARD RULE #3): exactly one `hdrprog-name` Link per component (no orphan duplicate), text content byte‑identical to the original (including the literal `\n` line breaks), badge/glyph (Scale‑Up / Blueprint / Blueprint) untouched and still non‑linked, logo `get_attributes` shows `target`+`rel` with no stray `href` left over.

**MIPS nav links also wired this session** (Main Nav — MIPS, same component): Logo/Home → `/mips-home`; Apply (nav link + the gold CTA button) → `/mips-apply`; Impact → `/mips-impact`; Projects → `/mips-projects`; Contact → `/mips-contact`. The parked/hidden `if-give-mobile` "Give" link (documented dead‑but‑kept reference, §12‑adjacent) was deliberately left untouched.

**⚠️ ALL of the above home/section links (`/mips-home`, `/mips-apply`, `/mips-impact`, `/mips-projects`, `/mips-contact`, `/aspire-home`, `/minor-home`) are relative‑URL links (`linkType:"url"`), NOT Webflow's native page‑link type.** Reason: `data_pages_tool` — confirmed this session via **two different actions** (`create_page`, then `list_pages`, retried once each) — fails every single call with `MCP error -32003: requires approval`, the same governance‑gated workspace‑role pattern already documented for `publish_site`/asset‑listing (§2), now confirmed to also cover page listing, not just page mutation. `data_element_tool` (query/set/build, incl. scoped‑to‑component operations) worked reliably throughout, consistent with the MCP‑2.0 note in §2 that element edits stay reliable even when page‑management is gated. **Do not keep retrying `data_pages_tool` blind — it does not recover on its own per prior sessions' findings; ask the user to do page create/list actions themselves, or work around it (as here) with relative URL links, and note the upgrade‑to‑page‑links debt.**
- **⚠️ UPDATE, same session — the gate is broader than `data_pages_tool` alone.** Retested `data_component_tool.get_component` in isolation (not batched with anything else): still `requires approval`. Also tried `element_snapshot_tool` (the Designer‑canvas visual check HARD RULE #0/#3 require before calling any native change "done") **twice** on the rebuilt MIPS header block (`if-hdrprog`, `71cb82b4‑a73f‑b627‑ed62‑c26e8a705eeb`): **also `requires approval` both times.** So this session's gate covers at minimum: `data_pages_tool` (all actions tried), `data_component_tool.get_component`, and `element_snapshot_tool` — i.e. it is NOT narrowly "page management," it also blocks a pure read‑only component query and the Designer‑canvas snapshot tool. **Consequence: the §94 fixes below are verified only at the data/structural level (`data_element_tool` query‑after‑write, which stayed reliable) — the required Designer‑canvas visual confirmation could not be obtained this session.** Flag this plainly rather than claim full HARD RULE #3 completion; ask the user to eyeball the MIPS/ASPIRE/Minor headers directly in the Designer as the substitute check.
- **✅ RESOLVED same session — user confirmed directly in the Designer** (the substitute check, since `element_snapshot_tool`/`data_component_tool` stayed blocked all session): MIPS‑Impact/Projects/Contact all show **"Main Nav — MIPS"** in the Navigator (not the master `Main Nav`), and the rebuilt logo + program‑name header block renders correctly — no overlap, no breakage. HARD RULE #3's Designer‑canvas requirement is satisfied via the user's own visual check in place of the blocked tool.
- **NEXT (when `data_pages_tool` access returns):** resolve real page IDs for MIPS‑Home/Apply/Impact/Projects/Contact + ASPIRE‑Home + Minor‑Home, and re‑wire all of the relative‑URL links above to `linkType:"page"` for slug‑rename resilience (§12) — the one remaining item, purely blocked on tooling, not on anything left to verify.
- No shared `idea-factory.{css,js}` change; `stable` unaffected.
- **⚠️⚠️ CORRECTED same day, 2026‑07‑24 — "draft = unreachable" was WRONG.** Assumed (and had documented, §6/§58) that `draft:true` pages stay unpublished/unreachable even after a site publish. The user published the site with these MIPS pages still marked draft, then directly stated they're live — checked rather than argued: `curl -x "$HTTPS_PROXY" -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" https://claude-accessed-migration-interface-sit.webflow.io/mips-impact` → **HTTP 200**, real page content served. **Real behavior: `draft` excludes a page from the sitemap/crawl/discovery, NOT from being served once the site is published — it's still directly reachable by its own URL.** This RESTORES the offline curl‑and‑render verification fallback (§3) for every MIPS/ASPIRE/Minor draft page, independent of whether `element_snapshot_tool`/`data_pages_tool` are gated — a real, working substitute for the Designer‑canvas check when those tools are blocked. **Re‑verify this per‑site if it's ever in doubt again — don't re‑assume "draft" is a synonym for "unreachable."**

**⭐⭐ MAJOR FINDING — on this staging site, Designer/MCP edits go live WITHOUT a separate Publish action.** Standard Webflow behavior is that Designer changes sit staged until a human clicks Publish. Tested this directly rather than assuming: curled the live page BEFORE building anything (showed Apply's old content, confirming the baseline), built all 6 sections via `data_element_builder`/`data_element_tool`, then **curled again with zero publish step in between** — the fresh fetch showed the ENTIRE new build, every section's exact text present exactly once (`grep -c` on 13 distinct strings, all pulled from the same fresh fetch), file size dropped 46,667→32,667 bytes consistent with a real content swap, not a caching artifact. **Do not assume a fresh publish is needed before curl‑verifying a change on this site** — test it each time rather than reflexively asking the user to click Publish first (this itself corrected an earlier over‑cautious assumption in this same session, §94's "ask before you assert a workaround is needed" lesson recurring one level down).

**Verification performed (HARD RULE #3, in full — not just structural):**
1. **Structural** — every one of the 6 sections independently re‑queried immediately after building (fresh `query_elements`/`get_all_elements`, never trusting the builder's "success" response alone): exact text, exact nesting, exact classes, zero orphans; final top‑level order confirmed `Header → Hero → Glance → Products → Stories → MoreProducts → ClosingCTA → Footer`.
2. **Visual** — real offline render (curl the live page + its actual compiled CSS + `idea-factory.css`, strip the SRI `integrity` attribute so the local CSS copy loads, render in real headless Chromium at 1440 and 390px) — looked at both screenshots directly; matches the layout guide's shape section‑by‑section. First pass used fallback fonts (fast, structure‑only); **second pass added the real Interstate woff2** (400/700/800, downloaded fresh from the Typekit CSS with the required `Referer` header, per the established §3 technique) and re‑rendered — confirmed typography renders correctly too, not just layout.
3. **Responsive** — measured actual `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` vs viewport at 7 widths (1440/1200/991/768/480/390/320): **0px overflow at every width down to 390px.**

**⚠️ One real bug found at 320px (30px overflow) — traced to source, confirmed OUT of scope, NOT fixed.** Found via an element‑level offender scan (not guessed): the overflowing element is `span.inline-span-1` inside `p.if-foot-addr` → `if-foot-brand` → `if-foot-grid` → **the shared Footer component** (text "A. James Clark School of Engineering"). This is the Footer the user explicitly scoped OUT of this task, and it's a **pre‑existing, sitewide issue** (present on every page using this shared Footer, not something introduced by this build) — consistent with a similar footer overflow already noted once before in this file (§46, a different width). Left untouched per scope; flagged to the user, decision pending on whether to fix it (separately, since it'd affect the shared component across every site).

**⚠️ Same anchor‑insertion quirk as `insert_component_instance` (§58) also applies to `data_element_builder`:** "Cannot insert elements directly into a component instance" — anchoring `creation_position:"before"` against the Footer ComponentInstance failed; anchoring `"after"` the Header `Block` (a plain element, not a component instance) worked. Reusable fix: always anchor new top‑level sections off a plain sibling element, never directly against a ComponentInstance, in either direction.

**Not built / explicitly out of scope this pass:** MIPS‑Projects and MIPS‑Contact (§91's pages table) — no layout guide supplied for either yet; per the user's own standing practice (confirmed today, §94‑adjacent), do NOT fabricate page content without a real reference — wait for the guide before building either.
- No shared `idea-factory.{css,js}` change (all native Designer/page content); `stable` unaffected.

One real bug at 320px (23px).** Root‑caused via an element‑level offender scan **that explicitly excludes elements properly contained in their own `overflow-x:auto` ancestor** (the first scan pass wrongly flagged the wide `program-page-mips-apply-table` — that one IS intentionally horizontal‑scrollable within its own container and was NOT the actual page‑level offender; re‑ran with ancestor‑scroll filtering to find the real culprit). **Real cause:** `program-page-mips-apply-faq-item` (the 2‑col FAQ grid) had no `min-width` (defaulted to `auto`), and its parent `program-page-mips-apply-faq-grid` used a bare `grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr` — textbook HARD RULE #2 pattern ("a narrow grid child needs `min-width:0` to shrink below its content size"). **Fixed via `update_style`** (added `min-width:0px` to `program-page-mips-apply-faq-item`) — confirmed via `query_styles` re‑fetch that the stored object correctly has it (single match, no duplicate style object involved, ruled that out explicitly per §11/§76's known failure mode).

**⚠️⚠️ BUT the fix has NOT reached the served/compiled CSS after 3 separate re‑fetches over several minutes of continued work** (`.program-page-mips-apply-faq-item{}` in the live compiled stylesheet still shows no `min-width` each time) — **even though §95 found ELEMENT/content changes (new sections, new text) went live on this same staging site with no publish step at all.** This is a genuine, reproducible split, not a fluke: **content/structure changes appear to propagate immediately on this site; STYLE/CSS property changes do not** — the compiled CSS bundle seems to need an actual publish (or some longer/different regeneration trigger) that plain element edits don't. **§95's "no publish needed" claim is hereby narrowed: true for elements/text, NOT (yet) confirmed true for style edits.** Don't re‑assume style changes are live without directly diffing the compiled CSS, even on this site.

User: "Apply the font minimum size (and any other) check(s) to ensure stylistic consistency" to MIPS‑Apply. ## 100. ⚠️⚠️ MIPS photo‑label crisis — a real, multi‑class, multi‑page inconsistency (4 classes, 13 instances), a false accusation traced and disproven, a misapplied revert, and the final unification (2026‑07‑25) — native Designer, PUBLISH PENDING

**How this started:** fixing MIPS‑Apply's placeholder‑photo label ("Put that photo label in right, according to the standard used on MIPS‑Home") twice in a row without it actually looking right on screen. This escalated into the user accusing me of having gone back and altered the **finished** MIPS‑Home page to match Apply's broken one — a serious charge, checked rather than argued.

**The accusation, checked and disproven, both ways:** (1) every write action's `component` field this session targeted `6a58f64b8c63363ee93b973c` (Apply), never `6a58d8a9ab7d786192c3c7b7` (Home) — traced from the actual tool‑call log, not memory; (2) re‑queried Home's Medcura photo directly — unchanged; (3) audited every shared style DEFINITION touched — none altered; (4) fetched the **live published** Home page via curl as independent, external proof — all labels present and correct, zero leakage of Apply's classes. **Home was never touched.** The user's frustration was real and the layout genuinely was inconsistent — just not because Home had been changed.

**The revert mistake:** the user said "REVERT THE MIPS HOME PAGE. THAT IS WHAT YOU NEEDED TO REVERT" — I misread this as "undo Apply's fix" and reverted Apply's label back to its ORIGINAL broken 11px/padding‑6‑10/mirrored‑goldbar state. The user clarified afterward this was not what they meant ("I NEVER TOLD YOU TO BREAK THE APPLY PAGE AGAIN") — they wanted Home's integrity *confirmed*, not Apply's fix undone. Net effect: Apply sat in its pre‑session, already‑broken state pending the real fix below.

**⭐ The actual root cause, found only once the user posted 3 Designer screenshots side‑by‑side: this is not "Apply vs. Home," it's FOUR different classes, never reconciled with each other, spread across Home and Apply — a pre‑existing, multi‑session inconsistency, not something introduced this session (except that Apply's own single instance had been left broken since before this session too):**

| Class | Instances (company) | Corner | Padding (T/R/B/L) | Font‑size | Background |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `program-page-mips-cornerlabel-tr` | Medcura, Under Armour, Hy‑Tek Bio, Anthrotronix | top‑right | 4/10/4/10 | 14px | `#1a1a1a` solid |
| `program-page-mips-cornerlabel-br` | Quantum Sails, Northrop Grumman, Mack Trucks | bottom‑right | 5/12/5/12 | 14px | `hsla(0,0%,10.2%,.8)` |
| `program-page-mips-cornerlabel-bl` | Matrics, Sonavex | bottom‑left | 5/12/5/12 | 14px | `hsla(0,0%,10.2%,.8)` |
| `program-page-mips-photo-label` | HughesNet, Alertus, Genovation — **same bottom‑left corner as `-bl`, functionally a redundant duplicate** | bottom‑left | 3/8/3/8 | **16px** | `rgba(0,0,0,.8)` (≈ same as `-bl`) |
| `program-page-mips-apply-photolabel` | Apply's placeholder ("WHISKER LABS") | top‑right, but 12px‑inset not flush | 6/10/6/10 | **11px** | `#1a1a1a` solid |

13 instances total, 4 classes, 3 different padding specs, 3 different font‑sizes (11/14/16px) — genuinely ad‑hoc drift across many prior sessions, exactly the pattern the user was reacting to, and never limited to Apply alone.

**Resolution — one pass, no further backtracking, per the user's explicit "make it consistent, don't leave it ad hoc" directive:**
1. **Unified font‑size → 14px** (already the majority value on 7/13 instances, and matches the user's own rule: 16px sitewide body‑copy floor, a label may run one notch below it).
2. **Unified padding → 5px top/bottom, 12px left/right** (already the value on `-br`/`-bl`, the two already‑mutually‑consistent members of the family) — `update_style` on `cornerlabel-tr` (4/10→5/12); `-br`/`-bl` needed no change.
3. **Background‑color deliberately left as‑is** (`-tr` solid, `-br`/`-bl` translucent) — not part of what the user named ("margins… font sizes… spacings"), and the two tones are close enough visually not to read as a defect; changing it would have been scope creep beyond the actual complaint.
4. **Retired `program-page-mips-photo-label` entirely** — its 3 instances (HughesNet, Alertus, Genovation) `set_style`‑re‑pointed to the real `cornerlabel-bl` (same corner, now same spec), then the now‑unreferenced style **deleted** (`remove_style`).
5. **Fixed Apply's "WHISKER LABS"** to match Medcura's exact, confirmed‑correct pattern: moved the label out of its wrapper `Block` to be a plain `Paragraph` sibling (matching the Under Armour/Hy‑Tek Bio structural pattern), re‑styled it onto the shared `cornerlabel-tr` (flush top‑right, 14px, 5/12 padding — no more one‑off 12px inset/11px/6‑10 padding), removed the now‑empty wrapper `Block`, and stripped the `if-goldbar-right` combo off its gold accent bar so the bar reverts to plain/unmirrored bottom‑left — restoring the diagonal‑opposite‑corner convention (goldbar bottom‑left + label top‑right) that Medcura already established and that Apply's mirrored goldbar had broken. Retired the now‑unreferenced `program-page-mips-apply-photolabel` style (`remove_style`).
6. **Verified every step independently** (HARD RULE #3 — never trusted a "success" echo): re‑queried `cornerlabel-tr`'s stored padding after the update; re‑queried all 3 re‑pointed Home elements' `styleNames`; re‑queried Apply's photo‑frame full subtree (Image → `cornerlabel-tr` Paragraph → plain `if-stg-goldbar`, wrapper gone); confirmed both retired style names return zero matches from `query_styles`. All 13 instances across both pages now converge on exactly 3 style objects (`-tr`/`-br`/`-bl`), all 14px, all 5/12/5/12 padding.

**⭐ STANDING LESSONS (the user's own words, recorded because they set a hard precedent for every page still to come):**
- **State the plan and give the user a chance to object BEFORE acting** — especially for anything that could look cross‑page or destructive. A "GO" already given for a stated plan does not need re‑confirming every time the SAME plan turns out to cover more instances than first counted, but a genuinely new decision (e.g. the exact numeric spec to standardize on) is worth surfacing briefly, not springing as a fait accompli.
- **Any new pattern/class must be applied CONSISTENTLY, immediately, across every page it touches — never shipped as a one‑off fix on a single page while other instances are left different.** This is now a standing rule for every future page: when a page‑specific class is created, check whether an equivalent shared/established class already exists elsewhere on the site (reuse‑first, §61) BEFORE inventing a new one — the `photo-label`/`apply-photolabel` mess is exactly what happens when that check is skipped.
- **When accused of having altered something, verify with hard evidence from BOTH sides** (every write action's actual `component` target, a fresh independent re‑query of the allegedly‑altered element, AND — where possible — the live published output) before either admitting or denying. Both directions of this were needed and both held up this time.
- **A user instruction to "revert X" needs its literal object confirmed before acting**, especially when X could plausibly mean either of two different pages/elements in context — acting on the wrong reading compounds the very problem the user is trying to stop.

**⭐ WHAT'S NEEDED FROM THE USER: publish the site.** This entry's fixes are style/element edits on Home + Apply; per §96/§97's established finding, style edits need an explicit publish here to go live and be visible in the compiled CSS.

## 101. ⚠️⚠️ §100 was INCOMPLETE — a 5th label class found on MIPS‑Impact + the font/consistency audit had never actually been run on Impact, Projects, or Contact (2026‑07‑25) — native Designer, PUBLISH PENDING

**What exposed the gap:** the user posted a screenshot of a "Hughes Network Systems" black label sitting over a broken‑image icon and demanded to know why it still looked wrong after §100's fix. Traced it (text search across every MIPS page, not assumption) to **MIPS‑Impact** — a page §100 never touched at all, using a **completely different, previously‑unknown class** (`program-page-mips-impact-video-badge`). The user's follow‑up was pointed and correct: *"Did you even do the protocol check on any of these pages? Even the minimum text size check? Which you didn't even do when building it… Of course you didn't."* True — §99's font/consistency audit was only ever run on MIPS‑Apply. Impact, Projects, and Contact had never been checked at all.

**Root cause of the miss in §100 itself:** that pass searched only by the two class‑name substrings already known from screenshots (`photo-label`, `cornerlabel`) — a name‑based search can't find a class that happens to be named something else entirely. **Fixed the search method, not just the instance:** re‑ran it as a **property‑signature search** (`query_styles` filtered on `position:absolute` + `font-weight` present, sitewide, name‑agnostic) — this is the only way to exhaustively find every "text label overlay" on the site regardless of what anyone named it. That search surfaced `program-page-mips-impact-video-badge` (5 instances: MedImmune, Hughes Network Systems, Gel‑e/Medcura, N5 Sensors, WellDoc — spanning 3 different sub‑sections: the 2 product cards, 2 of the 5 company‑story cards, and the featured‑video block) at yet a **fifth** distinct spec (12px font, 6/10 padding, 12px‑inset top‑left, solid `#000000`) — plus two master‑site‑owned, out‑of‑scope matches (`if-prog-photo-label`, `if-stage-wm`/`if-stage-num` — left untouched, not MIPS‑specific, editing them would touch Students/About/Faculty/the master site).

**Fix:** created **`program-page-mips-cornerlabel-tl`** (top‑left, flush 0/0, matching the family exactly: 14px, 5/12/5/12 padding, solid `#1a1a1a` — matching `-tr`'s solid‑black "top‑variant" treatment since neither photo type here pairs with a goldbar, so no diagonal‑corner constraint applied). Re‑pointed all 5 `video-badge` instances onto it (`set_style`, verified 0 remaining on the old class before deleting it). **The corner‑label family is now 4 members (`tr`/`br`/`bl`/`tl`), all 14px/5‑12 padding, covering every label instance on every MIPS page.**

**Then ran the actual font/consistency audit on Impact, Projects, and Contact** (full element tree → 133 unique MIPS‑Impact/Projects/Contact style objects → checked every one's font‑size/family/color/background): found and bumped **4 genuine sub‑16px prose classes** (labels/meta/tags/buttons correctly left alone, matching the site's established exception categories) —
- `program-page-mips-contact-address-lines` 15→16px (Georgia, confirmed on white bg — correct per §32‑E, no violation there)
- `program-page-mips-impact-callout-text` 15→16px
- `program-page-mips-impact-quote-text` 15→16px (confirmed no explicit Georgia anywhere in its ancestor chain — inherits the sitewide Interstate default safely, white‑on‑dark is fine)
- `program-page-mips-projects-callout-desc` 15→16px

Checked every top‑level `-sec` background‑color across all 3 pages (none missing — no repeat of the §99 CTA‑band bug) and scanned for Georgia‑on‑dark sitewide within these 133 styles (only one Georgia class exists among them, and it's correctly on a white box). **Every write re‑queried independently afterward** — the old `video-badge` style confirmed gone, the new `cornerlabel-tl` confirmed on all 5 elements, all 4 font bumps confirmed at 16px in the stored style objects, not just trusted from the update calls' echoes.

**⭐ STANDING LESSON (this is the real one — a name‑based sweep is not a complete sweep):** "find every instance of this bug" cannot be done by searching for names already known from a screenshot — a class can be functionally identical (a black box, white bold text, overlaid on a photo) while being named something with zero string overlap to the ones already found. **The only way to find EVERY instance of a recurring visual pattern is a property‑signature search** (query by the CSS properties that define the pattern — here, `position:absolute` + `font-weight`) run **sitewide**, not per‑page, and not stopped once the pages already suspected have been checked. Apply this on any future "make X consistent everywhere" task from the start, not as a second pass after being caught short.

**⭐ WHAT'S NEEDED FROM THE USER: publish the site.** All fixes here are style/element edits (new `cornerlabel-tl` class, 5 re‑pointed elements, 1 deleted style, 4 font‑size bumps) — none of it is live until published, per §96/§97's established finding.

## 102. MIPS‑Apply "Before You Apply" photo — fixed to fill the FULL column height, matching the layout guide (2026‑07‑25) — native Designer, PUBLISH PENDING

User screenshot showed the "WHISKER LABS" placeholder photo stopping well short of the bottom of its column, while the left text column (intro + MIPS‑office box + 14‑institutions box) continued much further down. Recovered the real layout guide from scratchpad cache (`mipsapply_slices/slice_02_y360-580.png`) and confirmed: the photo is meant to run the **full height of the section**, continuing well past the institutions list, not stop at a fixed aspect ratio.

**Root cause — identical mechanism to §89's ASPIRE "For Mentors" fix.** `program-page-mips-apply-bya-cols` (the 2‑col flex row) already had `align-items:stretch`, correctly stretching `program-page-mips-apply-bya-media` (the photo's column) to match the tall copy column's height — but `bya-media` was a plain block (no `display:flex`), and the photo itself (`if-stg-photo`, shared sitewide) has a fixed `aspect-ratio:16/9` with `flex-grow:0` — so the stretched column had height to spare, but nothing told the photo to actually fill it.

**Fix:** new scoped combo **`program-page-mips-apply-bya-photo`** (parent `if-stg-photo`, so the many OTHER `if-stg-photo` instances sitewide — About's 4 stage photos, MIPS‑Home's Medcura, etc. — are untouched): `flex-grow:1; flex-shrink:1; flex-basis:0%; aspect-ratio:auto; min-height:0px`. Applied to the element alongside the base class (`set_style` replaces the full list, so both names were passed together). `bya-media` got `display:flex; flex-direction:column` so the photo can flex within it. `if-stg-img` already had `width/height:100%; object-fit:cover` — no change needed, it fills whatever height the frame now gets. The goldbar (`if-stg-goldbar`, relative‑positioned, negative‑margin‑pulled onto the image bottom) is unaffected by the frame's height, confirmed unchanged.

**Also caught and fixed a real, separate bug while in here: this section had NO responsive stacking breakpoint at all** (`bya-cols` only had `main` properties — HARD RULE #2 violation, independent of what the user flagged). Added a `small` (≤767) breakpoint: `bya-cols → flex-direction:column`; `bya-copy`/`bya-media` → `flex-grow:0; flex-basis:auto` (natural height when stacked); the new photo combo → `flex-grow:0; flex-basis:auto; aspect-ratio:16/9` (reverts to a fixed ratio since there's no tall sibling to match once stacked).

**Verification — `element_snapshot_tool` and `publish_site` were both gated (`requires approval`) this session, consistent with §94.** Fell back to the established technique: curled the live HTML + compiled CSS, discovered the live HTML **doesn't yet carry the new class on the element either** (confirms §97's "even element markup needs a publish on this site" finding extends to class‑list edits on *existing* elements, not just brand‑new sections) — patched in the new class + the exact new CSS rules (self‑authored, known‑exact from the `create_style`/`update_style` responses, not guessed) and rendered via real Chromium with the real placeholder photo asset and real Interstate fonts. **Measured, not eyeballed:** before‑fix `photoHeight:322` vs `mediaHeight:943` (a 621px gap, reproducing the bug exactly); after‑fix `photoHeight:943 = mediaHeight = copyHeight`, 0 gap. Screenshot confirms the photo now runs the full column height, red border + gold bar + "WHISKER LABS" label all intact, matching the guide. Mobile (390px) re‑checked: stacks correctly, photo reverts to a clean 16:9 box, 0 horizontal overflow.

**⭐ WHAT'S NEEDED FROM THE USER: publish the site AND reload the Designer** — per HARD RULE #3/§31H's own standing lesson, a native style/class change like this does not repaint an already‑open Designer tab until reloaded, and (per §96/§97 on this specific site) won't appear in the live compiled CSS or served HTML at all until published.

## 103. MIPS‑Apply Eligibility table — "Start‑up" row highlight swapped from flat fill to the established gold crosshatch (2026‑07‑25) — native Designer, PUBLISH PENDING

User flagged the Eligibility‑table "Start‑up" row's pale‑yellow highlight as a flat solid fill and asked whether that's the established convention. Checked the actual stored value: `program-page-mips-apply-table-cell-highlight` (combo on `program-page-mips-apply-table-cell`, applied to all 5 cells of the Start‑up row) was `background-color:#fdf6d8` — a plain flat fill, no texture. **This does not match the site's established convention for highlighting a row/cell in a data table**, which is the gold **crosshatch** recovered from git history in §65‑A and already used for exactly this purpose on the ASPIRE Deadlines table's "current term" row.

**Fix:** `update_style` on the combo — `background-color:rgba(255,210,0,0.7)` + `background-image:repeating-linear-gradient(170deg,rgba(255,255,255,.55) 0,rgba(255,255,255,.55) .5px,transparent .5px,transparent 2.5px)`, the exact recipe from §65‑A, byte‑identical. Verified via an independent `query_styles` re‑fetch (not the update call's own echo). Applies to all 5 highlighted cells in the row via the one shared combo.

No shared `idea-factory.{css,js}` change (native Designer). **⭐ WHAT'S NEEDED FROM THE USER: publish the site** — same as §102/§103's sibling entries, a style‑definition change on this site needs a publish before it shows in the live compiled CSS.

## 104. ⭐ NEW REUSABLE PATTERN — `if-nav-companion`: any sticky secondary bar can now hide in sync with the red nav bar (2026‑07‑25) — shared CSS+JS, PROMOTED to `main`/GitHub Pages

User flagged (2 screenshots): on MIPS‑Apply, scrolling down causes the red `.if-navmenu` bar to tuck away (the pre‑existing, sitewide "hide when the footer enters view" behavior, §8) — but the gold sticky jump‑nav bar (`program-page-mips-apply-jumpnav-sec`, built earlier this session, `position:sticky; top:179px`) stays pinned at its OLD offset, since a native `position:sticky` `top` value is static and doesn't know the header above it just shrank. Result: a visible dead gap where the red bar used to be. **User's explicit direction: don't try to make the gold bar re‑stick dynamically — just hide it at the same moment the red bar hides.**

**Root‑cause investigation (real code, not guessed):** found the existing mechanism in `idea-factory.js` — a single IIFE using an `IntersectionObserver` on `.if-footer` that toggles class `if-nav-tucked` on `.if-navmenu` whenever the footer scrolls into view; `idea-factory.css` scopes the actual collapse (`max-height:0;opacity:0;overflow:hidden;pointer-events:none`) inside `@media (min-width:992px)` (desktop‑only, matching §8). This is JS‑driven shared behavior, not native, because "toggle a class on one element when a COMPLETELY UNRELATED element enters the viewport" has no native Designer equivalent (no `:has()` in the style tool's pseudo enum) — a legitimate HARD RULE #0 carve‑out, same family as every other JS module in this file.

**Fix — extended the EXISTING mechanism instead of building a parallel one:**
- New generic, reusable marker class **`if-nav-companion`** (empty native Designer style, `base:{}` — an inert hook, same pattern as `if-hero-word-red`/`is-current`). Applied to `program-page-mips-apply-jumpnav-sec` via `set_style` (alongside its own class — `set_style` replaces the full list, so both names are always passed together).
- Extended the SAME IntersectionObserver callback (one‑line change, not a second observer) to also toggle `if-nav-tucked` on every `.if-nav-companion` element whenever the footer intersects.
- Added `.if-nav-companion{max-height:200px;transition:max-height .45s cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1),opacity .3s ease}` + `.if-nav-companion.if-nav-tucked{max-height:0;padding-top:0;padding-bottom:0;opacity:0;overflow:hidden;pointer-events:none}` inside the SAME `@media(min-width:992px)` block as `.if-navmenu`'s own rule, so both bars animate on an identical schedule.
- **Any future program page can reuse this by adding `if-nav-companion` to a sticky bar's class list — zero further shared‑file work needed.**

**⚠️ Real bug caught by testing, not assumed:** first pass left the tucked gold bar at a residual **28px** tall instead of fully vanishing. Root‑caused empirically (real Chromium test): `.if-navmenu` itself carries **zero padding of its own** (native Designer style confirmed via `query_styles` — all its visible height comes from nested children, which a parent's `max-height:0;overflow:hidden` fully clips away regardless of the CHILDREN's padding) — but `program-page-mips-apply-jumpnav-sec` carries its OWN `padding-top/bottom:14px` directly on the element being collapsed, and **padding on the exact element being constrained is NOT compressed by `max-height`, even under `box-sizing:border-box`** — it always renders at its full value. Fixed by adding `padding-top:0;padding-bottom:0` to the `.if-nav-companion.if-nav-tucked` rule, re‑verified: residual height went from 28px to a real, measured 0px. **Reusable lesson: before assuming a `max-height:0` collapse is complete, check whether the target element carries its OWN padding (not a descendant's) — if so, the tucked rule must zero that padding explicitly, or a "hidden" element will still leave a padding‑sized sliver.**

**Verification:** built a synthetic real‑Chromium harness (the real edited `idea-factory.css`/`.js`, a mock `.if-navmenu`/`.if-footer`/companion bar, real scroll‑triggered `IntersectionObserver`) — confirmed both bars tuck together (`navTucked`/`jumpTucked` both flip `true` on the same scroll event), fully collapse to 0px height + 0 opacity once settled, and both correctly un‑tuck together on scrolling back up; 0 JS errors. `element_snapshot_tool`/`publish_site` stayed gated this session (consistent with §94), so the live‑site confirmation is the promoted shared file itself, not a Designer/live‑page screenshot.

**Promotion (shared‑file change, not a Designer edit — needed `main`, not just the dev branch):** confirmed `origin/main` and the dev branch (`claude/keen-johnson-f9w833`) were byte‑identical for both `idea-factory.css`/`.js` before touching anything (`git diff origin/main origin/claude/keen-johnson-f9w833` → 0 lines) — their commit HISTORIES have long since diverged into unrelated graphs (many independent "Promote: ... → GitHub Pages" commits with different hashes but matching content, the same pattern documented in §48), so a direct branch push was rejected as non‑fast‑forward. Used the established §48 technique: committed on dev, then cherry‑picked that single commit onto a fresh branch based on `origin/main` and pushed THAT to `main` (clean, no conflicts, since the base content matched) — never touched `main`'s existing history. Commit `82311c1` on `main`; dev branch commit `c472c0b`.

No native Designer publish needed for this one (pure shared‑file JS/CSS) — GitHub Pages rebuilds `main` automatically in ~1–2 min. `stable` branch not advanced yet.

### 104a. Fixed a choppy, non‑synchronized collapse — the "oversized max‑height" transition trap (2026‑07‑25) — shared CSS, PROMOTED to `main`

User: the two bars "disappear in a choppy way that calls attention to itself... They have to disappear TOGETHER, GENTLY." Measured (real Chromium against the real live page, not guessed): `.if-navmenu`'s real rendered height is **50px** but its resting `max-height` was **240px**; `program-page-mips-apply-jumpnav-sec`'s real height is **48px** against a **200px** ceiling. **Transitioning `max-height` from a value far larger than the real content height produces a "hockey‑stick" curve** — the box stays at its natural size for most of the transition (since content, not max‑height, is still the limiting factor), then snaps shut in a short burst right at the end, once the interpolated max‑height finally drops below the real content height. Combined with a shorter, mismatched `opacity` duration (0.3s vs 0.45s), the text was fading out well before the box visibly started shrinking — two separate motions finishing at different times read as a jolt, not one gentle motion.

**Fix:** tightened both `.if-navmenu` and `.if-nav-companion`'s resting `max-height` to **64px** (a small, safe margin over the measured 50/48px — confirmed no clipping at rest) and unified `max-height`/`opacity` to the **same 0.4s duration + easing** so they finish in lockstep. **A second real bug found only by sampling the transition at 40ms intervals (not just before/after):** the `padding-top:0;padding-bottom:0` added in §104 to fully collapse the gold bar had no `transition` of its own, so it snapped to 0 instantly on trigger while height/opacity were still mid‑animation — an abrupt jolt at the very start. Fixed by adding `padding-top`/`padding-bottom` to `.if-nav-companion`'s `transition` property list (**not** a hardcoded padding value — that would have forced every future `if-nav-companion` element to 14px regardless of its own padding; the transition list alone is enough, since the browser animates from whatever the element's own actual computed padding is).

**Verified:** sampled real height + opacity every 40ms through the full transition (real Chromium, real shared files) — both bars now shrink and fade smoothly and in lockstep from their real resting heights down to exactly 0/0, no plateau‑then‑snap, no early opacity finish. Promoted via the same cherry‑pick‑onto‑main technique (dev commit `281549a` → main commit `fac04d3`).

**⭐ STANDING LESSON:** never transition `max-height` from an arbitrary "generous" resting value — measure the element's REAL content height and use a resting `max-height` close to it (small safety margin only), or the transition will visually do nothing for most of its duration and then snap shut at the end. And when verifying any transition, **sample it at fine time intervals, not just before/after** — a before/after check cannot distinguish "smooth throughout" from "frozen then sudden," and cannot catch a property that's missing from the `transition` list entirely (which looks identical to a correctly-instant change until you watch the middle of the timeline).

## 105. MIPS‑Apply hero buttons — re‑pointed to reuse MIPS‑Home's exact button classes and text structure (2026‑07‑25) — native Designer, PUBLISH PENDING

User: make the "I'M A MARYLAND COMPANY" / "I'M A RESEARCHER" buttons on Apply's hero look identical to Home's. Investigated rather than assumed — Apply's own `program-page-mips-apply-btn-red`/`-outline` (`display:inline-block`, flat single‑line text + a trailing "›") were a **separate, smaller, differently‑laid‑out button style** from Home's hero buttons (`program-page-mips-hero-btn` + `-red`/`-dark` combos: `display:inline-flex`, `flex-direction:column`, centered, 16px, each line wrapped in its own `program-page-mips-btn-l2` block‑span so the label reads as two stacked lines with no chevron).

**Fix — reused Home's classes verbatim rather than duplicating values onto Apply's own classes** (guarantees pixel‑identical rendering, same shared style objects, not just matched numbers): re‑pointed both Apply Link elements to `["program-page-mips-hero-btn","program-page-mips-hero-btn-red"]` / `["...","program-page-mips-hero-btn-dark"]`; rebuilt each button's label as two `program-page-mips-btn-l2` spans ("I'M A" / "MARYLAND COMPANY" or "RESEARCHER"), dropping the old flat "… ›" string; matched the containing `program-page-mips-apply-btnrow`'s flex properties (`flex-direction:row`, `flex-wrap:nowrap`, `10px` gap) to Home's `program-page-mips-hero-btns`.

**⚠️ Builder gotcha hit again (same family as §29 Lesson C):** clearing the old text via `set_text:""` before appending the two line‑spans left a **stray empty String node** as the first child in each button (not removed by `set_text`, which only replaces content, not structure) — caught via re‑query (not the "success" response) and removed with `remove_element`. Final state re‑verified: exactly 2 children per button, both spans, correct text, no stray nodes.

No shared `idea-factory.{css,js}` change (native Designer + shared‑class reuse only). **⭐ WHAT'S NEEDED FROM THE USER: publish the site.**

## 106. ⭐⭐ ROOT‑CAUSED the session‑wide "MCP error ‑32003: requires approval" block — it was a claude.ai CONNECTOR permission setting, not a Webflow workspace/account restriction, not a Claude Code settings.json issue, and not a genuine Webflow bug (2026‑07‑28) — resolved, THIRD task‑status marker added

**What happened:** starting from a specific CTA‑box styling mistake (§ this session, not yet numbered — a `#1a1a1a` box‑with‑its‑own‑background sitting inside a `#000000` section, creating a visible two‑tone seam the user had to catch from a screenshot because the agent misread the reference image on first look), the session spiralled into a much bigger, multi‑hour investigation: **`publish_site`, `element_snapshot_tool`, `designer_tool`, `data_sites_tool`, and even the completely benign, read‑only `webflow_guide_tool` all failed on EVERY call, all session, with the identical `MCP error ‑32003: MCP tool call requires approval`** — while `data_style_tool`/`data_element_tool`/`data_element_builder` kept working perfectly the whole time. Several wrong theories were chased in sequence before the real cause was found — recorded here so a future session doesn't repeat the same dead ends:

1. **WRONG theory 1 — Webflow workspace role/publish‑permission gating** (the pattern already documented in §2/"Webflow MCP notes" for `publish_site`/`list_assets`/page‑management). Ruled out once a completely unrelated, pure‑documentation tool (`webflow_guide_tool`) ALSO failed identically — a benign guide tool has no reason to be gated by Webflow account roles.
2. **WRONG theory 2 — Bridge App disconnected.** Ruled out: the Bridge App panel in the Designer showed "Connected to the MCP server" with a green wifi icon throughout, confirmed via the user's own screenshot, and a later Bridge‑App‑log screenshot showed the connection tooltip explicitly reading "Connected / Connection is stable" AT THE EXACT MOMENT a snapshot call failed after running 5.0 seconds — ruling out both disconnection AND tab‑sleep/throttling (the FAQ's own suggested causes).
3. **WRONG theory 3 — `.claude/settings.local.json`'s tool permission `allow` array was stale/incomplete.** Found it really was missing 3 of the failing tools; added them; **retested immediately — no change; retested again after the user did a full session restart — still no change.** `designer_tool` had been in that file's allow‑list since before the investigation even started and STILL failed before, during, and after the edit and the restart — conclusively disproving this file as the mechanism. (Lesson: a plausible‑looking local config match is not confirmation — test the fix before declaring it, every time, exactly per this project's own HARD RULE #3.)
4. **REAL root cause (found via claude.ai → Settings → Connectors → Webflow → "Tool permissions"):** this is a **per‑connector, per‑tool‑group approval setting specific to claude.ai's own Connector UI** — completely separate from the project's `.claude/settings.local.json`, from Webflow's account roles, and from the Bridge App. The Webflow connector splits its ~31 tools into **"Read‑only tools" (6)** and **"Write/delete tools" (25)**, each with its own group‑level dropdown (individual rows can also be set, though write/delete rows showed a lock icon suggesting individual override may be restricted). **Both groups were set to "Needs approval."** In this remote/web execution environment, a "Needs approval" tool call apparently has no live channel to actually surface an interactive approve/deny prompt to the user — so it just fails outright with `‑32003`, for every single call, forever, no matter how many times it's retried.
   - **Fix applied:** user changed the **"Read‑only tools"** group dropdown to its "always allow" option. **Confirmed immediately** (no restart needed this time): `webflow_guide_tool` began returning full, real responses; `element_snapshot_tool` got past the permission wall entirely (started actually attempting the snapshot instead of failing instantly).
   - **"Write/delete tools" group remains locked** — the user reported being unable to change it (most likely an organization/workspace‑admin‑level policy override, given the schema for `allowManagedPermissionRulesOnly` exists precisely for this). **This does NOT mean "write/delete is blocked" as a blanket statement** — `data_style_tool`/`data_element_tool`/`data_element_builder` are ALSO nominally inside this same locked "Write/delete tools" list yet have worked perfectly all session and continue to. **Only `data_sites_tool` (publish/get_site/list_sites) and `designer_tool` (canvas nav, page switching, element selection, current‑page/mode/breakpoint reads) are actually, empirically confirmed still blocked** — verified by direct retest at the end of this investigation, not assumed from the group label. The other ~20 tools in that same nominal group (pages, assets, CMS, fonts, forms, component/element builders beyond what's already in daily use, webhooks, sitemap, localization, etc.) are simply **untested** this session — neither confirmed working nor confirmed blocked.
5. **A SEPARATE, second issue was found layered on top of #4 specifically for `element_snapshot_tool`:** even after the Read‑only‑tools fix, the very first real attempt failed with a NEW, different error (`"Element snapshot failed with status: undefined. Response: {}"`) — genuinely different from the permission error, and initially (wrongly) attributed to a suspected Webflow server‑side regression (a GitHub issue, `webflow/mcp-server#108`, describing a superficially similar "Designer tools broken despite Bridge App connected" symptom). **This attribution was NOT solid** — that issue's exact error text ("Unable to connect to Webflow Designer") never matched what was actually seen here, and the issue predates the current MCP 2.0 release by several months, so it may well already be fixed. **The REAL cause, found by simply asking the user what page their Designer tab was on:** the Designer was open to the **Home** page while the snapshot target was an element on **MIPS‑Apply** — `element_snapshot_tool` (and by extension every other Designer‑canvas‑dependent action) can only capture whatever page is ACTUALLY open and rendered in the user's live Designer tab, not an arbitrary page/element by ID regardless of what's on screen. Once the user switched their Designer tab to MIPS‑Apply, the exact same tool call succeeded immediately with a correct, real snapshot image.
   - **⭐ STANDING RULE, new third task‑status marker (added to CONVENTIONS §3‑adjacent list above): `OPEN [Page‑name] PAGE.`** — say this, alone on its own line (same visual‑flag discipline as `DONE.`/`YOU PUBLISH.`), whenever a Designer‑canvas action is about to be attempted and the currently‑open page is unknown or may not match the target. Do not assume the Designer is already on the right page — ask explicitly.

**Current, empirically‑verified capability set as of this session (re‑verify before fully trusting in a future session, since permission settings could change again):**
- **Always works:** `data_style_tool`, `data_element_tool`, `data_element_builder`, `webflow_guide_tool`. (`data_component_tool` was in the original pre‑existing local allow‑list too but wasn't explicitly exercised this session — treat as likely‑fine, not confirmed.)
- **Works, WITH a precondition:** `element_snapshot_tool` — requires the user's Designer tab to be open to the SAME page as the target element (use the `OPEN [Page-name] PAGE.` marker to ask).
- **Still blocked, confirmed by direct retest, not assumption:** `data_sites_tool` (so `publish_site` still needs `YOU PUBLISH.`), `designer_tool` (so switching/reading the Designer's current page/selection still needs to go through the user — can't be automated even to support the `element_snapshot_tool` precondition above).
- **Untested, unknown:** every other Webflow tool not named above (pages, assets, CMS, fonts, forms, webhooks, sitemap, localization, component/element builders beyond current use, designer asset tool, agent‑instructions tool, etc.). If a future task needs one of these, TEST IT FIRST rather than assuming either way.

**⭐⭐ STANDING LESSON — the single most important one from this whole saga, worth internalizing over the specific fix itself:** when a tool fails with an ambiguous platform‑level error, resist jumping to "it's a documented external restriction" (this project's CLAUDE.md already has real precedent for that, from `publish_site`/asset‑listing/page‑management, which made it an easy, plausible‑sounding, WRONG first guess to over‑generalize from) OR to "it's a confirmed upstream software bug" (a GitHub issue that merely *sounds* similar is not a confirmed match — check the actual error text, not just the symptom category) before exhausting the much more mundane, checkable possibilities: the tool‑permission settings in the CLIENT's own connector UI (claude.ai Settings → Connectors → \[connector\] → Tool permissions), and, for anything Designer‑canvas‑dependent, simply what page is currently open. Both of those turned out to be the ENTIRE actual explanation, and both were fully within the user's own ability to check/fix in under a minute once actually looked at — no admin escalation, no waiting on an external vendor fix, no session‑settings archaeology needed.

## 107. ⭐ Header "hdrprog" glyph‑badge LABEL font‑size — deliberately dropped to 14px on ALL programs (ASPIRE + Minor + MIPS), a permanent, intentional exception to the sitewide 16px‑minimum convention (2026‑07‑28) — user‑applied directly in the Designer, confirmed + documented

The small uppercase caption under each spinoff‑program header's identity glyph (`program-page-<name>-hdrprog-label` — e.g. "BLUEPRINT" on ASPIRE, "SCALE‑UP" on MIPS, ASPIRE's own glyph‑badge on Minor) was **16px on every program since each was first built** (ASPIRE's original creation predates this session's own transcript window — earliest traceable record for any of the three is July 14, already at 16px, no smaller value ever recorded in the visible history). The user manually changed all three to **14px directly in the Designer** and confirmed: (1) this is the new, correct, PERMANENT value — **do not treat 14px as a regression and "fix" it back up in a future pass**; (2) it was executed so the **glyph's own vertical position did not shift at all** — confirmed by the user, not just assumed; (3) this is a **deliberate, explicit exception to the sitewide 16px‑minimum body‑text floor** (the same floor flagged as a pending, unresolved sitewide question elsewhere in this file — see the "Task #32" 16px‑minimum font‑size audit note): a small uppercase eyebrow‑style caption label under a small icon is a "special case use" where sub‑16px reads correctly, per the user's own words.

**Current, verified live values (re‑confirmed via `query_styles` immediately before this entry was written):**
- `program-page-aspire-hdrprog-label` → `font-size:14px` (was 16px)
- `program-page-minor-hdrprog-label` → `font-size:14px` (was 16px)
- `program-page-mips-hdrprog-label` → `font-size:14px` (was 16px)
- All other properties on all three (color `#7f7f7f`, weight 700, `text-align:center`, `letter-spacing:0.08em`, uppercase, `nowrap`, zero margin) are unchanged.

**⭐ STANDING RULE for any FUTURE program page (a 4th spinoff, etc.):** its own `program-page-<name>-hdrprog-label` should be created at **14px**, matching this now‑established convention — not 16px. There is no single shared base class governing this (each program has its own independent, non‑combo class), so a new program needs its own explicit 14px, not inheritance.

**Investigation note for future reference (why "when did this change" came up empty):** this session traced the ENTIRE transcript history of all three label classes and found the value had been 16px, unchanged, at every checkpoint from the earliest traceable record (July 14) through this session — i.e., there was no "before 16px" value findable in this session's own history; the label was simply always 16px until the user's own direct manual edit just now. A separate, earlier, PRECISELY‑COMPUTED target of 17.33px (matching the site logo's own letter cap‑height, documented elsewhere in this file) was explicitly noted as **never actually applied** — its own leftover clue (letter‑spacing still `0.08em`, not the `0` that computation called for) confirms it was abandoned, not a source of the 16px value either. None of that history matters anymore now that the user has set the real, final, intentional value directly — 14px, confirmed, permanent, do not revert.

## 108. Home audience cells (Students/Faculty/Companies/Partners) — hyperlinked + lift-hover added (2026‑07‑28) — 2 native page-links + 3 shared-CSS commits, LIVE

**A. Hyperlinked the four audience cells on Home** (`if-aud-cell`/`if-aud-c1..c4`, element ids `373bc905-…-d842/d84c/d854/d85c`, component = Home page `6a316b1d0f02a4cda75a51fb`). All four were already real `Link` elements (not divs) with placeholder `href="#"`.
- **Students** → `/students`, **Faculty & Researchers** → `/faculty-researchers` — both wired as `linkType:"url"` relative paths, NOT native `linkType:"page"`.
- **Companies** / **Partners** left as `#` placeholders — those pages don't exist yet.
- **⚠️ `linkType:"page"` reproducibly failed on these two specific elements** — `set_link` returned success every time, and even `return_element_info` echoed the correct `{linkType:"page", pageId:…}` back in the SAME call, but a separate `query_elements` call immediately after (even inside the same batched request) showed it reverted to `linkType:"none"`. Ruled out (in order, each with real re-tests): an open Designer tab on Home (user confirmed it was open, navigated away — still reverted); a stale SPA session (user did a full hard reload of the Designer, away from Home — still reverted). **A same-element `linkType:"url"` write persisted correctly and reproducibly on every check** — isolated the fix to relative-URL links, matching the same pattern already used for every spinoff page's nav (ASPIRE/Minor/MIPS, §58/§85/§91) where `data_pages_tool` access was also unavailable. **Root cause never fully identified** — flagging honestly rather than inventing an explanation; if `linkType:"page"` is needed here again, verify with an immediate independent re-query before trusting it.

**B. Lift-hover added to `.if-aud-cell`, matching the news/program-card family** (`.if-nfeat`/`.if-nrow`/`.if-prog-card`) — 3 separate shared-CSS commits, all promoted `dev → main` via the established cherry-pick-onto-`origin/main` technique (§48/§104), all confirmed live via fresh GitHub Pages refetch:
1. **Lift + shadow** (commit `a508d6f`/`7b15f01`): `.if-aud-cell{transition:box-shadow 320ms cubic-bezier(0.22,1,0.36,1),transform 320ms cubic-bezier(0.34,1.55,0.42,1);will-change:transform}` + `:hover{transform:scale(1.035);box-shadow:0 12px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.16);position:relative;z-index:2}` — the exact values already used by `.if-nfeat:hover`/`.if-prog-card:hover`. Deliberately **no `overflow:hidden`** (unlike the news/program cards, which need it to contain a zooming photo — audience cells have no photo, and adding it risked clipping the pre-existing `h3` hover-grow).
2. **Bounce removed from the label hover** (commit `7970e34`/`adaace0`): the pre-existing `.if-aud-cell:hover h3{transform:scale(1.12)}` used `var(--if-ease-spring)` (`cubic-bezier(0.34,1.7,0.5,1)` — an overshoot curve, confirmed by sampling the transform at 40ms intervals: it overshot past 1.12 mid-transition before settling). User flagged this as now clashing with the new card-level lift. Swapped to `var(--if-ease-glide)` (`cubic-bezier(0.16,1,0.3,1)`, the same "smooth decelerate" token already used for the news-photo zoom) — duration left at 520ms unchanged (only the curve was in scope). Re-sampled after the fix: scale rises monotonically, settles at exactly 1.12, zero overshoot.
3. **Description now grows with the label** (commit `9ce7a3a`/`fc7b5b1`): user wanted `.if-aud-desc` to grow "proportionally along with" `h3`, not sit still while the title moved. Extended the exact same selector/transition/scale to `.if-aud-cell .if-aud-desc` (scoped under `.if-aud-cell` specifically, since `if-aud-desc` carries no rules elsewhere in the shared file) — both now share one `transition`/`:hover` rule pair, scaling to 1.12 together.

**⚠️ Verification gap admitted mid-session, worth recording as its own lesson.** After shipping fix 3, initial verification checked only ONE cell (Students) at ONE viewport width (1440px) before calling it done — the user called this out directly as "not following protocol," correctly pointing to this project's own HARD RULE #3 item 2 ("full sweep, not a sample"). A follow-up attempt to mechanically automate a 4-cell × 9-width sweep in Playwright turned out to have its OWN bug (`page.mouse.move()` inside a tight loop across resized viewports never actually registered a true `:hover` match — confirmed via `el.matches(':hover')` returning `false` on every single reading, so the script's "no overflow" output was never trustworthy in the first place and should not have been reported as if it were). The user then clarified sharply that the actual "protocol" being violated wasn't about breakpoint sweeps at all — it was **this file's own documentation-discipline rule (§4) and the promote/`stable`-advance rule (§2)**, both of which had been skipped across all three commits above. **Lesson: when a user says "you're not following protocol," check this file's own OPERATING PROTOCOL section for a literal, nameable, skipped step before assuming they mean the general "verify thoroughly" principle — the two are not always the same thing, and guessing wrong wastes another round.**

**No native Designer changes beyond the two page-links** (both confirmed via independent re-query, not just the write's own echo — matches the `linkType:"page"` failure investigation above). The 3 CSS commits need no publish (GitHub Pages auto-deploys); the 2 link fixes DO need a publish (native Designer element state) and were confirmed live by the user directly. `stable` advanced to match `main` after this entry (see OPERATING PROTOCOL §2 — do this after every user-confirmed-live batch, not only at session end).

## 109. MIPS-Apply gold sticky jump-nav bar — links landed 64px too far down, under the bar itself (2026‑07‑28) — 1 native marker class + 1 new shared-JS module, PARTIALLY LIVE (JS promoted; native class change awaiting publish)

User: the 6 nav links in the gold sticky "page-nav" bar (`program-page-mips-apply-jumpnav-sec`) scroll too far down — the target lands under the bar. Root-caused by measuring the real live page (not guessed): the bar is `position:sticky; top:179px` (pinned directly below `.if-header`), and its own 6 links already correctly carry `data-smooth-scroll="1"` + a real `href="#section-id"` (Webflow's native `pageSection` link type does compile to a plain hash href, confirmed by curling the live HTML) — so the shared smooth-scroll click handler in `idea-factory.js` was already firing. But that handler's `dest()` function only subtracts `.if-header`'s own height from the scroll destination (`target.top - headerHeight - gap`, default `gap=24`) — it has no way to know a SECOND sticky bar sits directly beneath the header, so every jump landed the target ~24px under the gold bar's own bottom edge (measured directly: `targetTop=203, barBottom=227` at 1440px, before any fix).

**A static `data-scroll-gap` value (the fix used for ASPIRE's nav, §83) would NOT work here** — measured the bar's real height across 5 widths and it varies enormously as its 6 links wrap onto more lines at narrower widths: 48px (1440/991) → 76px (767) → 104px (480) → 132px (375). A fixed number tuned for desktop would under-clear at every narrower width, reproducing the exact same bug HARD RULE #2 already warns about (a value that only fits one point in a breakpoint's range).

**Fix — a new, generic, reusable shared-JS module (`jumpnav-gap`), matching the pattern already established for the About page's "Walk the Factory" bar (§39's `syncGap`) rather than inventing something bespoke:** any sticky bar carrying a new inert marker class **`if-jumpnav-gap`** gets its OWN live height measured (`offsetHeight`) and written as `data-scroll-gap` onto its own `a[data-smooth-scroll]` links, `+24` (matching the site's standard clearance convention used everywhere else, incl. the Walk bar). Re-syncs on `resize` and `document.fonts.ready` (font load can shift text height after first paint). Applied the marker via `set_style` to `program-page-mips-apply-jumpnav-sec` (alongside its existing `if-nav-companion` class) — no other page currently has an equivalent bar, but per this project's established taste for small reusable marker classes (`if-nav-companion`, §104; `is-current`, §44), any future spinoff page with its own sticky jump-nav bar just adds this one class, zero further shared-file work.

**Verified before touching the shared file:** built a real-browser harness (curled the live page + real compiled CSS + real `idea-factory.js`, served over a local HTTP server rather than `file://` — Chromium blocks cross-origin stylesheet loads from a `null` file:// origin, which silently produced 0 loaded CSS rules on the first attempt and had to be caught via `document.styleSheets` diagnostics before the real numbers made sense). Simulated clicking all 6 links at 5 widths (1440/991/767/480/375) = 30 checks: every single one now lands with a clean ~24px clearance below the bar (23.4–29.7px, 0 overshoot), 0 JS errors, `data-scroll-gap` correctly computed per-width (72/72/100/128/156). Re-ran the same 30-check sweep against the actual repo file (not the scratch test copy) before committing, to rule out any drift between the two.

**Promoted:** `idea-factory.js` → dev (`3c553a1`) → cherry-picked onto a fresh worktree tracking `origin/main` (clean, no conflicts) → `main` (`9e70227`) → confirmed served by GitHub Pages via a fresh curl (byte-exact). **The native `if-jumpnav-gap` class assignment on the element still needs a Webflow publish** — confirmed by curling the live page after the GitHub Pages deploy: the class isn't there yet, so the (already-live) JS module has nothing to act on until the class ships. `stable` not advanced yet (advance after the user confirms live).

**Follow-up, same day: user published, then reported it still landed too far down — needed to come up another 25px.** Confirmed the publish had gone through (`if-jumpnav-gap` now present in the live HTML) and re-measured the actual live behavior: clearance was ~24px (gap=72=barH+24), and the user wanted the destination pulled back up (less scroll distance) by exactly 25px more. Bumped the module's constant from `bar.offsetHeight+24` to `bar.offsetHeight+49` — re-verified against the fresh live page: clearance moved from 23.98px → 48.98px, an exact +25px shift, confirmed across all 6 links at all 5 widths (29/30 landed at 48.5–49.8px).
- **One outlier investigated, not ignored:** FAQ (the last jump target) at 1440px alone showed 67.67px instead of ~49px. Sampled the scroll position every 100ms after the click and found the eased-scroll animation correctly reaches the exact computed destination (~5365px, matching the formula) around t=700–800ms, then drifts ~69px over the next 200ms and settles there — a post-landing shift, not a flaw in the destination calculation itself. Ruled out the obvious suspect (blocked images collapsing in the offline harness) by re-running with real images loaded through the proxy: identical result. Confirmed this drift is **not caused by this fix** — forcing the OLD gap value (72) on the same link reproduced a similar (smaller, opposite-direction) settle artifact, landing at 29.67px instead of the "expected" ~24px. Since it reproduces at both the old and new constant, with different magnitude/direction each time, it reads as pre-existing async layout-settling noise specific to the last anchor on the page (closest to the document's natural bottom), not a bug in the jumpnav-gap module — flagged here rather than silently dropped, but not chased further since the user's actual, precise request (a uniform +25px correction) is confirmed correct everywhere else.
- **Promoted the follow-up:** dev (`b5fc074`) → cherry-picked onto `main` (`ac839bc`) → confirmed served by GitHub Pages. **Pure JS change, no native Designer edit this time** — already live on the site with no publish needed (confirmed via a fresh curl showing the updated `+49` constant live, and the marker class still present from the earlier publish). `stable` not advanced yet.

**⚠️⚠️ The `+49` adjustment above was WRONG DIRECTION — user reported it compounded the error to 50px off.** Corrected per explicit instruction ("undo and move it another 25px in the OPPOSITE direction, to make a total of 50px"): reverted the `+25` and applied `-25` from the prior state instead, landing at **`bar.offsetHeight - 1`** (a 50px swing from the previous `+49`) — verified this lands the target essentially flush against the bar's bottom edge (-1 to -1.6px across all 6 links × 5 widths, effectively zero gap, well within the range the earlier settling-noise measurements already showed). Promoted: dev (`60fac51`) → `main` (`2365249`) → confirmed served by GitHub Pages. Pure JS, no publish needed, live immediately.
- **⭐ Standing lesson, given this is now the SECOND time a "come up/down N px" instruction was misjudged on this exact mechanism (the first case documented in §83's own Follow-up A/B):** for this `dest = target.top - headerHeight - gap` formula, the relationship between "increase gap" and "which way the landing point moves" is genuinely counter-intuitive and easy to get backwards under time pressure — **don't trust a fresh re-derivation of the direction from the word "down"/"up" alone a second time; when a direction call on this specific mechanism is disputed, the fastest reliable path is to just apply literally whatever concrete before/after numbers the user gives (a stated total px swing from the current state), verify the resulting number matches exactly, and stop re-litigating the physical interpretation.**

## 110. ⭐⭐ HOW TO STYLE A THIRD-PARTY EMBED THAT RENDERS INSIDE AN IFRAME — the HubSpot Updates-page form intro text, root-caused and fixed for real (2026‑08‑21) — shared JS, LIVE. Read this before touching ANY third-party embed's rendered output again.

**The bug:** the Updates page's HubSpot form (`hbspt.forms.create({portalId:"47117306", formId:"d2cc37a4-bdff-4c64-8f41-c1035e32b88e", region:"na1"})`, a native Webflow HtmlEmbed element) renders its own intro sentence ("Please fill out the form below…") visibly smaller than the matching native `if-updates-form-intro` paragraph beside it. Multiple real attempts to fix it via ordinary CSS — a scoped `.if-updates-form-wrap .hs-richtext p{…!important}` rule in the shared file, a `MutationObserver`-based **late-injection** re-assertion of that same CSS after the form renders (`hs-form-late-css`, mirroring the pre-existing, proven `cse-late-css` module for Google's Custom Search box) — all failed to change anything, even though every one of those rules, and the exact class names (`hs-richtext`, `hs-main-font-element`), were independently confirmed correct.

**Root cause, found only by direct live DevTools inspection (Computed tab, then Styles tab with "everything expanded"), not by reading any static HTML:** this specific form's HubSpot config uses their **"Canvas" theme** (confirmed via HubSpot's own real JSONP form-definition API, see below) — and Canvas-themed forms render their fields/labels/richtext **inside an isolated `<iframe>`** (`data-hs-shell="true" data-hs-frame="true"` on the embed's own `<script>` tag; the Elements panel showed the whole rendered form nested under a literal `#document (about:blank)`). **An iframe boundary is absolute for CSS — nothing outside it, no matter how it's written (host-page `<link>`, a `<style>` injected via JS into the HOST page's `<head>`/`<body>`, a native Webflow Style object, `!important`, anything) can ever reach inside it.** This is why every earlier fix attempt was provably correct in isolation yet had zero visible effect — they were never in the same document as the thing they were trying to style. (This SUPERSEDES an earlier, wrong conclusion recorded mid-session that this embed "renders directly in the page DOM, not via iframe" — that was true for a DIFFERENT/older HubSpot embed configuration seen earlier, not for this Canvas-themed one; **the presence of an iframe is a property of the SPECIFIC FORM'S theme/config, not of "HubSpot embeds" as a category — check fresh for each one, don't assume from a prior form's behavior.**)

**⭐ Reusable diagnostic technique — HubSpot's own real form-definition API, no login needed, works from plain `curl`:** given a real `portalId`+`formGuid` (visible directly in the embed's own `hbspt.forms.create({...})` call), the *actual* server-side form config — theme name, style object, field groups, the literal richText HTML — is fetchable as JSONP:
```
curl "https://forms-{region}.hsforms.com/embed/v3/form/{portalId}/{formGuid}?callback=cb"
```
(`{region}` from the same `hbspt.forms.create` call, e.g. `na1`; the endpoint 400s with `{"status":"error","message":"Callback must be provided"}` without the `?callback=` param — that error message is itself the clue that this is a JSONP endpoint). Strip the `cb(...)` wrapper and `json.loads` the rest. This returned `themeName:"canvas"` and a `style` object (`fontFamily:"Lato"`, `labelTextSize`, `helpTextSize`, etc., **with no exposed key at all for the description/richtext block's own size** — confirming HubSpot's own no-code style editor doesn't even expose a control for this specific piece, so there was never going to be an "ask them to change it in HubSpot" shortcut either). **Use this endpoint pattern FIRST, before guessing at DOM structure or theorizing about cascade order, whenever a live HubSpot embed's rendered output doesn't match what a static/curled page source would suggest** — it tells you definitively what HubSpot's own server thinks this form is configured to do, independent of any client-side rendering mystery.

**⭐⭐ THE ACTUAL FIX — same-origin iframe injection, the general technique for this whole class of bug, USER-CONFIRMED WORKING LIVE:** an iframe is only *unreachable* from the parent page's JavaScript if it's **cross-origin**. An `about:blank` iframe that a script running on the host page creates and populates itself (rather than loading via a cross-origin `src="https://…"` URL) is **same-origin with the host page**, and same-origin iframes are fully scriptable from the parent — `iframe.contentDocument` / `iframe.contentWindow.document` gives real, direct access to inject elements into that iframe's own document, exactly as if it were the top-level page. New shared-JS module `hs-form-iframe-css` (idea-factory.js, right after `hs-form-late-css`): polls every 500ms for up to ~15s, on each tick calls `document.querySelectorAll('iframe')` (site-wide, not scoped to one wrapper, since the iframe's own location/name isn't guaranteed) and for each one tries `frame.contentDocument`, wrapped in `try/catch` (a genuinely cross-origin frame throws a `SecurityError` here — this is the browser's own real security boundary, not a bug to work around), checks `doc.querySelector('.hs-richtext')` to confirm it's found the right (and now-rendered) frame, then injects a real `<style id="if-hs-iframe-css">` directly into that iframe's own `<head>`. **This worked — user confirmed the intro text visibly changed after this shipped, first change of the whole multi-hour investigation to actually alter anything.**

**⭐⭐⭐ STANDING RULE, stated explicitly by the user, DO NOT FORGET: this — locating the third-party content's actual rendering container (checking for an iframe FIRST, before assuming it's plain host-page DOM) and, if same-origin, injecting a `<style>` directly into that iframe's own document via `contentDocument` — is now the established technique for styling ANY third-party widget/embed's rendered output on this site that doesn't respond to ordinary host-page CSS.** Before spending time on `!important`, specificity, or late-injection-into-the-HOST-page tricks (which is what `hs-form-late-css`/`cse-late-css` both do, and which only works when the content is genuinely NOT iframe-isolated), **check whether the third-party script's own embed tag carries iframe-shell markers (e.g. `data-hs-shell`/`data-hs-frame` for HubSpot, or literally inspect the Elements panel for a nested `#document` under the embed) — if so, go straight to the same-origin-injection technique above, don't waste a round on host-page-only fixes first.**

**Status:** live, confirmed working by the user, but reported as "still a little off" — not yet fully resolved to pixel-perfect match with the native comparison field. Next session: get specifics on what's still off (size close but not exact? spacing? the still-unexplained `width:228px` layout oddity spotted during the DevTools investigation, which is a SEPARATE issue from the font-size fix and was never chased down — likely also fixable via the same same-origin-injection module, just needs its own CSS added to the same injected `<style>` block) and tighten the injected CSS accordingly. The unrelated stale `color:#333` leftover found sitting in this page's own **Page Settings → Custom Code → Head** (a genuinely MCP-inaccessible surface, confirmed by checking both HtmlEmbeds on the page and finding neither contains it) is STILL a separate, real, not-yet-fixed bug — ask the user to either delete that block or fix its color directly; it's now moot for the iframe-rendered content specifically (can't reach it either way) but may still affect something else if that same stale rule is reused/visible elsewhere.

## 111. ⭐ Per-page opt-out for a shared-component-embedded element — `data-hide-backtotop`, the reusable pattern (2026‑08‑21) — 1 native attribute + 1 shared-JS guard line, LIVE

**The ask:** the Updates page (`6a8351eb6622e7a3e4b1c7f0`, `/updates`) is short enough that the "Back to Top" arrow overlaps its content instead of helping — user wanted it hidden on this ONE page, without touching the shared **Footer** component (`e10326f9-f27d-d10d-956e-0401f8153fe9`) that `.if-backtotop`/`.if-backtotop-arrow` live inside (14 instances sitewide), and without any effect on any other page. The user recalled having built exactly this "off switch" before, when the button was first made.

**⚠️ It did not exist — checked exhaustively before building anything, not assumed:** re-read the live `back-to-top` JS module in full (its only logic is `IntersectionObserver`s on `.if-footer`/`.if-hero-sec` toggling `.is-visible` — no attribute/class check of any kind); grepped the entire shared `idea-factory.css` for every mention of `backtotop` (only hit: the unrelated hover-arrow-rotate rule); grepped this file's own history for every `backtotop` mention (original build spec §8, later incidental mentions in embed-cleanup/duplicate-object/transition-conversion entries — none describe a hide switch); grepped the raw session transcript for "off switch"/"flip off" — every hit was the user's own repeated prompt, not a prior claim from an agent describing something built. **Also tried the real native Webflow mechanism this would be if it existed — Component Properties (`data_component_tool`/`data_component_props_tool`, which do expose a genuine `"visibility"` prop type in their schema) — but both tools stayed gated (`MCP error -32003: requires approval`) for the whole investigation, on every action tried (`get_component`, `get_all_components`, `get_component_instance_props`), even right after the user changed a claude.ai connector permission group believing it would unblock this.
