Never got a conclusive read on whether the Footer component itself has such a prop.** Given no existing switch and no way to inspect/use Component Properties, built a new one from scratch using tools that DO work reliably (`data_element_tool`, `data_style_tool`) rather than keep stalling on a blocked path.

**The pattern (native + one tiny shared-JS guard, same split this project uses everywhere for "genuinely non-native cross-page behavior," HARD RULE #0's own carve-out):**
1. **Native, per-page, zero risk to the shared component:** a plain custom attribute **`data-hide-backtotop="1"`**, set via `data_element_tool.set_attributes` on the page's OWN `.if-header` Block (a plain page-level element, confirmed via `query_elements` to be `type:"Block"`, NOT a `ComponentInstance` — i.e. never shared with any other page). Visible/editable natively in the Designer's Settings tab for that element. Independently re-queried via `get_attributes` after writing to confirm it persisted (HARD RULE #3 — never trust the write's own echo).
2. **One added line in the shared `back-to-top` module** (`idea-factory.js`): `if(document.querySelector('[data-hide-backtotop]'))return;` right after the existing `if(!btn||!footer)return;` guard — if any element on the page carries that attribute, the module never wires up its observers at all, so the button never gets `.is-visible` and stays in its native default-hidden state.
- **Why the attribute lives on `.if-header` and not, say, `<body>`:** `.if-header` is confirmed page-scoped (built fresh per page, unlike the Footer/Nav which ARE shared components) and guaranteed present on every real page — a safe, always-available, definitely-not-inside-the-shared-component anchor. Any other genuinely page-level element would work equally well; this is just the convention picked.
- **Verified in a real headless browser (not asserted) before shipping:** two full page renders of the real module — one with a `.if-header[data-hide-backtotop]`, one without — each with a hero+footer tall enough that scrolling to the bottom fully clears the hero from the viewport (a first test attempt used too-short elements and produced a false negative on BOTH cases; fixed by giving hero/footer real heights so the intersection geometry wasn't ambiguous, then re-ran). Result: **WITH** the attribute, `.if-backtotop` never gets `is-visible` even scrolled to the very bottom; **WITHOUT** it, `is-visible` gets added normally, exactly matching pre-existing behavor. 0 JS errors either case.
- **Promoted:** committed on dev (`d23d38e`) → cherry-picked onto a worktree tracking `origin/main` (clean, no conflicts) → pushed to `main` (`39b9bd6`) → polled GitHub Pages until the new JS was actually being served (confirmed live ~15s later via a fresh fetch). The native attribute itself needed a Webflow **publish** to go live (confirmed by curling the live Updates page immediately after setting it — plain `<header class="if-header">`, no attribute yet — same "native edits sit staged until Publish" behavior as everything else on this site); user published and confirmed the button is gone on Updates only.
- **⭐ Reusable going forward:** this is now the standing pattern for "one nested element inside a shared Component needs different behavior on one specific page, without touching the shared definition or any other instance" — ANY future case of this shape (not just back-to-top) can reuse the identical two-part recipe: a plain `data-*` attribute on a page-level (non-component) element, checked by a short guard in whichever shared-JS module drives that element's behavior. No native Component Properties access required. `stable` not advanced yet (advance after next full-site confirmation pass).

## 112. MIPS/Minor batch — Formstack scroll-jump fix, contact-card hover, Round-table gold hatch, hamburger-menu fixes, header-block display:none removal (2026‑08‑21/22) — 3 shared-JS/CSS promotions + native Designer changes, MOSTLY LIVE, one native change PUBLISH-PENDING

Six separate MIPS/Minor fixes this session, in the order they shipped. All promoted shared-file changes went through the established dev→worktree-cherry-pick-onto-`origin/main`→push workflow; all native Designer changes were independently re-queried after writing (never trusted the write's own "success" echo), per HARD RULE #3.

**A. Formstack (MIPS Statement-of-Interest) iframe — scroll-jump fixed, promoted `ff-form-iframe-css` module.** The prior height-sync approach (`height:auto` then remeasure) transiently shrank the host page, permanently clamping scroll position if the user was scrolled near the bottom. Rewrote `syncFrameHeight(doc)` to measure real content bounds directly — walks `doc.body.children`, takes each child's `getBoundingClientRect().bottom` **plus its own `margin-bottom`** (the rect alone under-measured by ~14-16px, since a trailing margin isn't part of the rect) — and only ever writes a new iframe height (via `!important`) when it differs from the current one by >2px. Never touches the iframe's height speculatively, so nothing shrinks out from under the user's scroll position. Also added a `ResizeObserver` on the iframe (guarded to width-only changes) plus a debounced (150ms) `window.resize` listener, so the form re-measures correctly on any responsive/width change, not just its initial render. User-confirmed: "OK this looks right!" Promoted: dev `9df7558` → main `59b5edc`.

**B. MIPS Contact page — 4 staff panels get the sitewide "lift off page" hover, reusing the established News-card mechanism exactly (not a new invention).** Self-hover (box grows + shadow deepens + lifts, native `:hover` pseudo — 100% Designer-representable, HARD RULE #0's own carve-out) applied directly to `program-page-mips-contact-staff-card`: `transition-property:"box-shadow, transform"`, `transition-duration:"320ms, 320ms"`, `transition-timing-function:"cubic-bezier(0.22,1,0.36,1), cubic-bezier(0.34,1.55,0.42,1)"` (both HARD RULE #4-compliant separate longhand fields, never a bundled `transition`), hover: `transform:scale(1.035)`, `box-shadow:0 12px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.16)`, `position:relative`, `z-index:2` — byte-for-byte the same values as `.if-nfeat:hover`/`.if-prog-card:hover`. The SECOND layer — the panel's own content growing *more* than the box on the same hover — is necessarily parent-hover-child (Webflow genuinely cannot express this natively), so it's the one line that had to go in the shared file: a new native wrapper style `program-page-mips-contact-staff-inner` (`display:flex;flex-direction:column`, own transition fields) applied around each panel's content, plus `.program-page-mips-contact-staff-card:hover .program-page-mips-contact-staff-inner{transform:scale(1.05);}` inserted into the shared minified runtime-CSS line, right after the identical existing `.if-nrow:hover .if-nrowtext{transform:scale(1.05);}` rule it was modeled on. Promoted: dev `706752f` → main `43ff5b3`.

**C. MIPS "Round" chart-table header recoloring — a DURABLE, portable fix, not a per-embed patch (user's explicit, forceful requirement).** The Round pages' chart tables are raw HTML/CSS pasted in from an external table-generator tool, one per Round page, each with its own inserted `<style>` block. User's header row used a pale blue fill (`.tg-sqty{background-color:#d6e2eb;...}` — a **randomized per-cell class name**, different on every export) and demanded it become the site's established gold "hatch" fill (`background-color:rgba(255,210,0,0.7)` + `background-image:repeating-linear-gradient(170deg,rgba(255,255,255,.55) 0,rgba(255,255,255,.55) .5px,transparent .5px,transparent 2.5px)` — the exact recipe already used elsewhere on the site, confirmed by live query, not misremembered as a flat color) — explicitly refusing any solution requiring per-embed manual edits, since "I'm going to have several pages of these." **`data_element_settings_tool` (the only tool that can read/write an HtmlEmbed's raw `code`) is blocked by a persistent connector-level permission gate** (confirmed via two distinct failure modes this session: a live, decline-able "Denied by user" prompt, and — after the user explicitly re-allowed it — a deeper "MCP error: requires approval" that survives even explicit live approval; `data_pages_tool.list_pages` hit the identical gate). Since editing embed code directly was both undesired AND blocked, the fix instead targets the table-generator tool's own **fixed, unchanging wrapper class** (`.tg` — confirmed the tool always emits this exact class name; only the per-cell suffix classes like `tg-sqty` are randomized per export) with a single shared-CSS override, `.tg th{...}`, appended near the end of `idea-factory.css` with an explanatory comment. This one rule now overrides the header fill on the CURRENT Round-71 table AND will automatically cover every FUTURE Round page's table too, with zero embed edits ever needed — verified with a portability test using a deliberately different hypothetical future export (different randomized per-cell classes, different blue shade, different row count) confirming the override still lands correctly via cascade alone. (An early self-caught test mistake: my first "future embed" test simulation used a DIFFERENT wrapper class `.tg2` instead of `.tg`, which correctly failed to match — that was a bug in my own test, not the fix, since the real tool never varies its own wrapper class; corrected and re-ran clean.) Promoted: dev `711df95` → main `3b83448`. User confirmed: "That actually appears to work" / "OK that bit's done."

**D. MIPS + Minor mobile hamburger menus showing no visible items — root cause: an orphaned duplicate style class silently applied instead of the correct one.** Both spinoffs' regular (non-hamburger) nav worked fine; only the "small"-breakpoint hamburger dropdown was affected. 6 nav-link elements across the two (Minor's "Courses"/"Contact"; MIPS's "Apply"/"Impact"/"Projects"/"Contact") were applying `if-nav-link-ORPHANED-do-not-use` instead of the real `if-nav-link` — a leftover from an earlier duplicate-style-object cleanup pass elsewhere in this project's history (the §11/§76 "duplicate style objects" pattern this file has documented repeatedly) — so they were missing the native "medium"-breakpoint mobile-menu color override (`color:#000000`), rendering as invisible white-on-white text against the dropdown's light panel. Fixed by reassigning all 6 elements to `if-nav-link` via `set_style`. **Verification gotcha hit twice this pass:** `query_elements`/`query_styles` calls addressing elements INSIDE a component definition (both spinoffs' Main Nav components) silently return 0 matches if `scope_component_id` is omitted, even though the element genuinely exists — caught and corrected both times by re-running with the correct `scope_component_id`. Confirmed via a real-browser reconstruction test (real live compiled CSS + real HTML with only the class-name fix simulated) that "Courses"/"Contact" go from white (invisible) to black (visible), with the never-broken "About" link's color unchanged before/after as a control. Native fix, needs publish; user confirmed: "OK that's fixed."

**E. MIPS mobile-only "Apply" CTA button hidden in the hamburger dropdown, desktop + other spinoffs' identical buttons untouched.** The gold Apply button shares its class (`if-give-btn-solid`) with ASPIRE's/Minor's/the main site's own equivalent CTA buttons — editing that shared class directly would have hidden all of them. Instead created a new scoped combo, `program-page-mips-navbtn-hidemobile` (parent `if-give-btn-solid`, empty base, `display:none` at the "medium" breakpoint only), and applied it ALONGSIDE the existing class on just the one MIPS mobile-menu button element. Verified via a reconstructed-CSS Playwright test (the real, just-confirmed stored property values, since native changes need a publish before they're live) across both breakpoints: MIPS's button visible on desktop / hidden in the hamburger; ASPIRE's and Minor's identical buttons visible at both widths, completely unaffected. User confirmed: "OK that's fixed."

**F. ⭐⭐ MIPS header identity block (program name + gold bar + glyph badge) disappearing ENTIRELY at every width below 767px — root cause found, but user explicitly required understanding WHY the culprit override existed before removing it, not just deleting it.** User's exact requirement (verbatim, and worth preserving as a standing habit for this whole project): *"What was that 'special case' set up FOR? It had to have been put there for a reason. What was the reason?"* — i.e. never delete something without first understanding its original purpose, especially on a project with this much documented, deliberate design history.
- **Investigation, not assumption:** grepped this entire file for any documented reason — found none (only an unrelated coincidental name match, the About page's `if-glyph-tall` stage glyphs). Queried the live style `program-page-mips-hdrprog` across ALL breakpoints (`main`/`medium`/`small`/`tiny`) and found the "small: `display:none`" override is the **only** breakpoint override that exists on this class anywhere — no partial/staged fallback logic, no companion overrides at other breakpoints. This is a single, blunt, all-or-nothing hide, not part of any considered responsive system.
- **⚠️⚠️ First empirical test produced an alarming, but entirely FABRICATED result — caught before it was trusted.** Built a real-browser reconstruction (curled live HTML + live compiled CSS, removed just the one `display:none` property to simulate the fix, rendered via Playwright) and got what looked like a genuine bug: the logo ballooning to near-viewport-width, causing overlap and overflow. **Root cause of THIS result: Subresource Integrity (SRI).** The curled HTML's `<link>` tags still declared the ORIGINAL file's `integrity="sha384-..."` hash; serving back a locally-MODIFIED copy of that CSS made the browser recompute the hash, find a mismatch, and **silently block the entire stylesheet** — so the "bug" was actually a completely unstyled page, not my proposed fix. Confirmed via an explicit console message (`"Failed to find a valid digest in the 'integrity' attribute... blocked"`). **Fix (the same one already documented elsewhere in this file for testing modified CSS on this site): strip every `integrity="..."` attribute from the HTML via regex before using it in any test that modifies the referenced CSS.** Reran with the corrected, SRI-stripped HTML and got genuinely valid data.
- **Real result (5 widths: 767/600/480/400/320px, real compiled CSS, real idea-factory.css/js):** with the override IN PLACE (current live state), the whole block is invisible at **every single tested width** — exactly the user's bug report. With it REMOVED: the name+gold-bar (`.if-hdrprog-name`) stays **visible at all 5 widths, never disappears**; the glyph badge (`program-page-mips-hdrprog-glyph-tall`, an unusually large 72.48×72.48px vs. the standard 30px glyph) correctly drops first at ≤480px; logo+title then shrink together proportionally below that (153px→131.6px→94.9px logo; 19px→16.3px→11.8px font) — matching ASPIRE's/Minor's already-working pattern exactly, with **zero overlap with the hamburger button at any width**. A separate horizontal-overflow figure at 480/400/320px was found to be **byte-identical whether the override is present or removed** — i.e. caused by something else on the page entirely, unrelated to this header block (flagged to the user as a distinct, pre-existing issue, not fixed as part of this task).
- **Conclusion given to the user, honestly, including the limit of what could be determined:** no documented reason was found, and the override is a single unconditional rule with no supporting responsive logic — consistent with a stopgap applied before the shared `header-name-fit` JS module (which already explicitly documents MIPS's own program-name text in its own header comment, e.g. "Maryland Industrial / Partnerships (MIPS)") was correctly handling this page, never cleaned up once real content landed. The module handles MIPS's oversized glyph and longer name correctly on its own; the override isn't preventing any real problem, it's actively causing the one being reported.
- **Fix, applied only after reporting this and getting explicit go-ahead** ("OK fix it. Be prepared to revert if something breaks."): removed the `display:none` property at the "small" breakpoint from `program-page-mips-hdrprog` via `update_style`/`remove_properties`. Independently re-queried immediately after (not the write's own echo) — confirmed the `small` breakpoint entry is now completely absent from the style's `breakpoints` object. Attempted an `element_snapshot_tool` Designer-canvas check too (HARD RULE #0) — it errored (`status:false`), most likely because the user's Designer tab wasn't open to MIPS-Home at the time (the tool's documented precondition); flagged this gap honestly rather than silently skipping it, and offered to retry once the right page is open.
- **✅ PUBLISHED, user-confirmed live and correct** ("The fix on the logo is still right"). Revert, if ever needed: re-add `display:none` at the `small` breakpoint on `program-page-mips-hdrprog` — one call, the exact original value is known and documented here.

**G. ⚠️⚠️ The mobile Apply-button hide (§112‑E) reappeared TWICE after being reported fixed — the real root cause was a competing `!important` rule the original verification never actually tested against.** After publishing the §112‑F header fix, the user reported the Apply button was back in the hamburger menu.
- **First pass:** re-queried the button's live class list and found `program-page-mips-navbtn-hidemobile` was simply missing from it (down to `["if-give-btn-solid"]` alone) — the style OBJECT was still correctly defined (`display:none` at "medium", no duplicate), only the element's own class assignment had been lost. Re-applied it via `set_style` (had to add `scope_component_id` — first attempt without it returned "Element not found"), independently re-verified via a fresh query, reported fixed, asked the user to publish. **Root cause of THIS disappearance was never established** — my own recent edits never touched this element, and no other explanation was found; flagged honestly rather than invented.
- **After that publish, the button was STILL showing.** This time the class WAS correctly present in the live HTML (confirmed via curl) — so the real bug was in the CSS itself, not the element's class list. Root-caused by directly reconstructing the REAL live page (real HTML + real compiled CSS + real jQuery + Webflow's own native nav-toggle JS bundles, fetched fresh — not a hand-written CSS snippet) and actually **clicking the real hamburger toggle**: the button's computed `display` stayed `block` even inside the genuinely-open mobile menu. Diffing the page's inline `<style>` blocks turned up the culprit — the in-site `st0` layout embed (the sitewide "DO NOT DELETE" custom-code embed, §0/§3/§12/§54/§55) carries:
  ```css
  .if-navmenu .if-give-btn-solid { display: block !important; order: 91 !important; ... }
  ```
  Same numeric specificity as the combo rule (`(0,2,0)` either way — a descendant combinator and a compound class chain count identically), but **`!important` always wins over a non-important declaration regardless of specificity** — and Webflow's native Designer style panel has no way to mark a property `!important`, so no purely-native fix could ever have beaten this embed rule. This is the exact same defeat-the-embed problem CLAUDE.md's §13 already solved once (the old nav current-marker vs. `st0`'s hover rule) — same fix shape applies: **one class higher specificity + `!important` of our own**, in the shared file. Added, scoped ONLY to the MIPS combo class so no other spinoff's identical button is touched:
  ```css
  @media screen and (max-width:991px){
    .if-navmenu .if-give-btn-solid.program-page-mips-navbtn-hidemobile{display:none!important;}
  }
  ```
- **⚠️ Self-correction on verification methodology, worth internalizing:** the ORIGINAL §112‑E "verified via a reconstructed-CSS Playwright test" used a small, hand-written CSS snippet containing only the base rule and the new combo rule — it never included the `st0` embed's competing `!important` rule at all, so of course it "passed." **A fix tested in isolation from the full real page's actual competing CSS can produce a false pass — when a fix depends on winning a cascade conflict, the verification MUST include every real rule that could compete, not a hand-assembled excerpt.** The only fully trustworthy test here was the one built from the genuine, complete, freshly-curled live page plus its real embed content and real toggle JS.
- **Verified (this time, against the real thing):** real live HTML + real compiled CSS + real jQuery + real Webflow nav-toggle JS bundles + the fixed `idea-factory.css`, hamburger toggle actually clicked — button computes `display:none` and a 0×0 rect both before AND after the menu opens (menu itself confirmed genuinely `display:block`); desktop (1400px) unaffected (`display:flex`, 84.75px visible) — the media-query scoping holds. Re-ran the identical test a final time against the **actual GitHub-Pages-served** file (not the local copy) after promotion — identical clean result, 0 JS errors.
- **Promoted:** dev `1f14c49` → cherry-picked cleanly onto a worktree tracking `origin/main` → `main` `ac0ad5a` → confirmed live via GitHub Pages within ~2 minutes (a first polling attempt showed "not live" for 20 tries, but that was the poll script's own grep pattern missing a trailing semicolon in the target string — the file was already correctly served; direct content inspection confirmed it). No native Designer change needed for this piece — the combo class itself (from §112‑E) was already correctly re-applied and published; this was purely a shared-CSS specificity fix.

## 113. ⚠️⚠️ Cleanup audit, prompted by a direct user question ("do you clean up failed attempts?") — 9 dead style objects deleted + a real live naming-inversion bug found and fixed (2026‑08‑22) — native Designer, no git-tracked files touched

Checked, rather than just reassured, per the user asking directly whether failed-attempt cruft gets cleaned up. Two things were genuinely worth cleaning; the second is the important one.

**A. `if-nav-link-ORPHANED-do-not-use` (from §112‑D, this session) — the one piece of debt I'd actually caused.** I'd moved 6 elements (Minor/MIPS nav links) OFF this dead duplicate class but never deleted the object itself. Confirmed zero elements reference it anywhere — checked all 4 nav components (master `Main Nav`, `Main Nav — ASPIRE`, `Main Nav — Minor`, `Main Nav — MIPS`), not just the two I'd fixed. Deleted; independently re-verified gone.

**B. ⚠️⚠️ The older, pre-existing "zzz-orphan" batch from §76's sitewide clamp-flattening pass had a genuine live bug hiding in it — a naming inversion, not just leftover dead weight.** §76 documented isolating 10 duplicate style objects by rename (`*-zzz-orphan` suffix) so they could never again collide with a live class name; 3 were deleted at the time, 7 were left (blocked by a `remove_style` tool bug). Found 9 still present this session (`if-foot-logos`, `if-events-wrap`, `if-event-feat`, `if-id-tagline`, `if-event-feat-title`, `if-event-row-meta`, `if-aud-wrap`, `if-news-wrap`, `if-foot-grid` — all `-zzz-orphan`). Before deleting anything, checked usage across every page that could plausibly reference each one (Home, Students, About, Faculty, plus the Footer and Main Nav components directly, since component-internal classes don't show up in a page-level element query without `scope_component_id`) — HARD RULE #3's "full sweep, not a sample," not a spot-check.
- **8 of the 9 were genuinely, confirmedly dead** (zero matches everywhere checked) — deleted, independently re-verified gone.
- **The 9th, `if-id-tagline-zzz-orphan`, was NOT dead — it had exactly ONE real element still applying it, inside the master `Main Nav` component.** This is the master site's own header tagline, live on Home/Students/About/Faculty. Investigated before touching anything: `if-id-tagline-zzz-orphan` (id `dea6a492…d10`) and a separate, identically-propertied object plainly named `if-id-tagline` (id `b6a33b02…d20147`, the SAME `dea6a492…`/`b6a33b02…` duplicate family documented in §11/§23) both existed. Curled the real live Home page and confirmed via the actual rendered HTML: `<div class="if-id-tagline-zzz-orphan">` — **the "orphan"-suffixed object was the genuinely live one; the plain, correctly-named `if-id-tagline` was the true dead orphan.** Some earlier session's isolate-by-rename pass had swapped the labels — exactly the risk HARD RULE #3 item 1 warns about ("a `success` response can land on a dead orphan while the live, compiling object stays untouched"), except here it was the ORIGINAL isolation step itself that got the two backwards, not a later edit.
  - **Fixed via the standard isolate-then-restore procedure:** renamed the confirmed-dead `if-id-tagline` → `if-id-tagline-ZZZ-TRUE-ORPHAN-confirmed-dead` (frees the clean name) → independently re-verified → renamed the confirmed-live `if-id-tagline-zzz-orphan` → `if-id-tagline` (restores the correct name to the object that's actually rendering) → independently re-verified `if-id-tagline` now resolves to exactly ONE object (the correct, live `dea6a492…`, matching properties) → deleted the now-clearly-isolated, confirmed-dead orphan → re-verified gone.
  - **Nothing changed functionally** (both objects had identical properties throughout, so the live tagline never looked different) — this was purely a correctness/clarity fix. A **publish** would be needed for the live HTML's literal class attribute to actually read `if-id-tagline` again instead of `if-id-tagline-zzz-orphan` (cosmetic only, zero visual effect either way, since both compile identically) — not urgent, but worth doing on the next publish so the site's HTML matches its own Designer style names again.
- **⭐ Standing lesson, sharper than anything §76/§78/§100/§101 already say: an "orphan"-suffixed name is a LABEL from a PAST cleanup attempt, not a guarantee — that label can itself be wrong if the original isolation step swapped which object was live.** Before deleting ANY object with a "-zzz-orphan"/"-ORPHANED"/similar marker name, still check real usage from scratch (page + relevant component scopes) rather than trusting the name at face value — this project's own historical cleanup passes are not infallible, and this session almost repeated the mistake in the other direction (nearly deleting the live tagline object because its name said "orphan").

**Also checked and confirmed clean (answering the user's actual question directly): no stray/dead code in the git-tracked shared files from this session's own iterations** — the Formstack `syncFrameHeight` rewrite is a single clean function with no leftover old version; the contact-card-hover and Round-table-hatch CSS additions each appear exactly once, correctly placed; no debug `console.log` statements beyond the established guarded try/catch error-warning pattern used by every module; `git status`/`git clean -ndx` show nothing stray in the actual repo (scratchpad test scripts live outside the repo entirely, in the session's ephemeral scratchpad, never committed). The commit history itself is granular (many small, focused commits) but each one left the code in a working state — normal iterative refinement, not abandoned/broken intermediate states.

**NEXT SESSION (superseded by §115 below — kept for the specific pointers): both the MIPS header-block fix (§112‑F) and the mobile Apply-button hide (§112‑E/G) are confirmed live and correct. The §113 tagline naming-fix is correct in Webflow's stored data now but cosmetic-only until the next publish (zero visual risk either way). If any other "-zzz-orphan"/"-ORPHANED" style is found in the future, re-verify its usage from scratch before trusting the name — see §113's tagline finding.**

## 114. MIPS bug-fix batch — hero info-list wrap groups, Eligibility table native responsive collapse, "Before You Apply" photo aspect ratio (2026‑08‑25) — native Designer, PUBLISHED

Three user-escalated fixes on live MIPS pages, in the order shipped.

**A. MIPS-Home hero info-list — items were overflowing their bordered box instead of wrapping (user, hostile).** Root cause: each info-list row paired a title/label with a value/date as SEPARATE flex children with `white-space:nowrap` baked into 3 shared classes, and the row itself had no `flex-wrap`. At any width narrower than the full unwrapped content, the label and its value/date rendered as one unbroken line that ran straight past the box's own border. **Fix:** removed the baked-in `white-space:nowrap` from all 3 classes; added `flex-wrap:wrap` to the row. **Follow-up correction (user, more precisely): the title is ONE group, the date/value is ANOTHER group — when the row can't fit both, the SECOND group must wrap as a whole beneath the first, not have its own internal words break first.** Fixed by wrapping each label and its value/date pair in its own flex sub-group div (`flex-shrink:0`, internally `flex-wrap:nowrap` so the pair's own words never split), with the wrapping behavior living on the OUTER row (`flex-wrap:wrap`) so a whole group drops to its own line as a unit once the row runs out of width — no group's internal text ever breaks mid-phrase, and no group extends past the box border. Desktop keeps both groups on one line whenever there's room; only encroachment triggers the drop.

**B. MIPS-Apply Eligibility table — cut off past desktop width, then (after a naive first fix) unreadable — real cell-by-cell responsive collapse (user, hostile, twice).** The table (raw HTML pasted from an external chart-generator, §112‑C) had no responsive handling at all — it simply ran off the right edge of the viewport below desktop width. **First fix (WRONG, caused the worse bug):** collapsed the table to a naive single column by hiding all cells except the first, which visually merged every row's HEADER cell into one undifferentiated block at the top, with the corresponding VALUES rendered below as an unlabeled dump — no way to tell which value belonged to which header. **Real fix:** collapsed via CSS `::before` pseudo-element labels injected per data cell (each cell's `::before` reads its own column header text, so at the collapsed breakpoint every value carries its own inline label directly above/beside it) plus divider borders between each now-stacked row-group, and the existing gold-crosshatch highlight combo (§65‑A/§103's recipe) preserved on the highlighted row's cells throughout the collapse. Verified via a real-browser reconstruction (live HTML + live compiled CSS, all widths desktop→375px): the table now stays genuinely readable — every value self-labeled — at every width, with 0 page-level horizontal overflow, replacing the earlier version that either cut off (unfixed) or dumped unlabeled values (first, wrong fix).

**C. MIPS-Apply "Before You Apply" photo — aspect ratio iterated per direct user spec, twice.** Was stretched to fill the full section height (§102), which the user found "too thin and narrow." Reverted to a fixed-ratio box; iterated: (1) portrait, ratio 1.618:1 (golden ratio, tall) at desktop; (2) "from tablet view on, flip it" — landscape 1.618:1 (wide) from the `medium` breakpoint down through mobile. Implemented as two `aspect-ratio` values on the existing `if-stg-photo`-family combo: portrait at `main`, landscape at `medium`/`small`/`tiny`. Verified via a real-viewport `page.screenshot()` after scrolling to the element (an `elementHandle.screenshot()` on this sticky-header page produced a misleading composite bleeding the sticky header into the crop — worked around by scrolling first and screenshotting the real viewport instead).

All three native Designer / raw-embed-CSS fixes, published, confirmed live. No shared `idea-factory.{css,js}` change. `stable` not advanced yet this session.

## 115. ⭐ VENTURES spinoff — first page built end-to-end from a layout guide, on a MIPS-Apply duplicate, with a full class-rename safety sweep (2026‑08‑25) — native Designer, PUBLISHED, verified 0-overflow at 11 breakpoints + Designer-canvas snapshot

New spinoff, **"Ventures" (Mtech Ventures)**, page **Ventures-Home** (page id `6a8d3bf5c2d5cb1b031d661a`, slug `/ventures-home`, site id `6a316b1a0f02a4cda75a50e7` — same site as every other spinoff staged here, §58/§85/§91). User duplicated **MIPS-Apply** themselves and handed off the resulting page to build from a layout-guide image, with two binding constraints stated up front: **(1) rename every inherited class so MIPS-Apply/MIPS-Home are never affected**, **(2) the hero banner is a VIDEO, not a still image** (a first for this project — every other site so far uses a static hero photo), plus the standing "verify responsively at all breakpoints" mandate restated twice in the same message. User explicitly instructed not to stop for questions until finished.

**Class-rename sweep (the binding safety constraint) — verified complete via `element_filter:{style:"program-page-mips"}` returning 0 matches sitewide on this page**, after two passes (the second pass caught 2 leftover section wrappers a first pass had missed — exactly the "full sweep, not a sample" discipline HARD RULE #3 demands). Every visual/content class rebuilt under the **`program-page-ventures-`** prefix per CONVENTIONS §3: header identity block (`-hdrprog*`), hero (`-hero-left/-right/-btn*/-subhead/-videoframe/-videoplay/-videolabel`), facts strip (`-facts/-fact-cell/-fact-label/-fact-desc`), about section (`-about-cols/-copy/-media/-photo`, `-sec-h`, `-next-box*`), stats band (`-statsband*`), plus a generic `-sec` section-padding wrapper replicating MIPS's shared `program-page-mips-sec` values. **MIPS-Apply/MIPS-Home confirmed untouched throughout** (every write scoped to the Ventures-Home component id; spot-checked MIPS-Home's own header element tree mid-build and found it unchanged).

**Content built, section by section, from the layout guide:** header nav (plain elements — see gap below) with a "Mtech Ventures" program-identity block (reused the "Assembly Line" stage glyph asset as a placeholder badge icon) → hero (H1 "Mtech Ventures", gold subhead, white lead paragraph, stacked gold "Apply for Space »" + outline "Book Office Hours »" buttons, a placeholder video-frame graphic on the right — **explicitly a graphic placeholder, not a real `Video`/`YouTubeVideo` element, since no actual file/URL was ever supplied**) → a 6-cell gold-left-border facts strip (Where/Space/Equity Taken/Residency/Cost/Who Can Apply) → an About section (2 paragraphs + a black "NEXT" callout box + a placeholder photo box explicitly labeled "Placeholder photo — Herbert Rabin Technology Advancement Building," deliberately NOT reusing MIPS's visually-wrong Medcura photo) → a red "Since 1985" stats band (3 stat cells: $3.6B / 146 / 1,000+, each with real descriptive copy). The pre-existing shared Footer component instance (CTA band + footer grid) was left completely untouched, same as every other page.

**Responsive verification — the actual, checkable half of the mandate — done properly, not asserted.** The page had been published at an earlier, incomplete point mid-build (stale HTML missing the facts/about/stats sections and 1 leftover MIPS class); ran `publish_site` myself (worked this session — not gated, unlike some earlier sessions' findings, §94/§106) and re-confirmed via a fresh curl that the live HTML now matches the full, final build exactly (0 leftover MIPS classes, all new sections present). Built a real-browser Playwright harness from the genuinely-live HTML + live compiled CSS + real shared `idea-factory.css`/`.js`, and swept **11 widths from 320px to 1440px**: `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` matched the viewport width at every single one (max 1px subpixel drift at 320), **0 elements poking past the viewport edge at any width**, 0 genuine JS errors. Confirmed each responsive mechanism engages at the correct native breakpoint: hero grid 2-col (4fr:3fr "Home page" ratio, §90) down through 992px → 1-col at ≤991; facts strip 3→2→1 columns at the `main`/`small`/`tiny` boundaries; About stacks to 1-col at ≤767; stats band stacks to 1-col at ≤767; the video-frame placeholder resizes cleanly with its column, no overflow at any width. Took real screenshots at 1440/820/390px and looked at them directly — layout reads correctly at all three. **Also confirmed in the Designer canvas** via `element_snapshot_tool` on the hero section (HARD RULE #0's own requirement — not just a published-only check): renders correctly natively, real Interstate font, real brand colors, no divergence from the published render.

**⭐ Reusable finding this build surfaced: the header identity-block visual classes are DELIBERATELY dual-classed.** Each spinoff (ASPIRE/MIPS/Minor) keeps its own page-specific visual class (e.g. `program-page-mips-hdrprog-title`) for its real styling, but ALSO carries a second, completely EMPTY, shared marker class (`if-hdrprog`, `if-hdrprog-name`, `if-hdrprog-title`, `if-hdrprog-badge` — confirmed via `query_styles` to be pure `{"base":{}}` inert hooks) purely so the shared JS module **`header-name-fit`** (idea-factory.js) can find and auto-shrink the glyph badge / logo+title as a unit when a program name is too long for its box, uniformly across every spinoff regardless of each one's own class names. **Attempting to add these marker classes to Ventures via `set_style` failed** with the same "multi-class combo-resolution" error documented earlier this session (passing 2 independent global classes with no registered combo relationship) — root cause of how MIPS/ASPIRE/Minor actually got this dual-class state onto their elements was NOT determined (their combo pair returns 0 matches as a registered chain via `query_styles`, so it's not a formal combo either). **Deliberately left unfixed rather than risk creating a duplicate-named style object** (the exact, repeatedly-documented anti-pattern from §11/§76/§78/§100/§101/§113) — verified instead that "Mtech Ventures" (the shortest program name of any spinoff built so far) never trips the shrink module's overflow check at any tested width even without the marker classes wired up (`titleOverflow: false` at all 11 widths), so the practical risk is negligible. **If a future Ventures page ever needs a longer header title, this is the first thing to revisit** — find how the Designer itself achieves the multi-class assignment (likely only reachable by a human dragging a second class onto the element in the Designer UI, not via any MCP tool tried this session) or ask the user to add the 4 marker classes manually.

**⚠️ Known, deliberately-scoped gaps — reported plainly, not glossed over, per the user's own "refine later, just get it done" instruction:**
- **No working mobile hamburger toggle.** The nav was built from plain elements (`Block`/`Link`/`DivBlock`), not a true Webflow `Navbar` component — `data_element_builder`'s type enum has no `NavbarWrapper`/`NavbarMenu`/`NavbarButton` etc., and `data_component_tool` (which could have duplicated MIPS's real working "Main Nav — MIPS" component) returned `MCP error: MCP tool call requires approval` on every attempt (`duplicate_component`, `get_component`) — a currently-unresolved tool-access block, distinct from the `data_sites_tool`/`data_pages_tool` gates noted elsewhere this session (`data_sites_tool.publish_site` worked fine; `data_pages_tool.get_page_metadata` was gated). **Consequence, confirmed visually via the tablet/mobile screenshots:** below the `medium` breakpoint (≤991) the full nav link list renders permanently expanded (Webflow's existing shared CSS for `.if-navmenu` at that breakpoint is styled for the menu's OPEN state, and with no JS toggle it's simply always in that state) — every link IS still reachable and clickable, nothing overflows or breaks, it just isn't space-saving/collapsible like every other page's working hamburger nav. **This is the single most significant remaining gap** — the real fix is either a human duplicating the working nav component in the Designer UI (the exact §58/§85/§91 recipe used for every other spinoff, which itself depends on `duplicate_component` access this session never got), or a future session with working `data_component_tool` access.
- Incubator/Programs top-nav items have no dropdown submenus (the guide showed carets on them; no submenu content was ever supplied, so none was fabricated — flat links to `#` instead).
- Header logo is a plain "Idea Factory" text placeholder, not the real inline-SVG UMD/Idea Factory logo mark used elsewhere on the site.
- Hero banner is a static placeholder graphic (dark frame + play icon + "Banner video — placeholder, awaiting final file" label), not a real `Video`/`YouTubeVideo` embed — no actual video file or URL was ever provided.
- About section's building photo is a plain gray placeholder box with an honest label, not a real photo.
- Several fact/stat figures (Cost, Who Can Apply, all 3 "Since 1985" stat descriptions) are reasonable, clearly-labeled placeholder copy pending real figures from the user — not fabricated to look authoritative.

No shared `idea-factory.{css,js}` change this session (all native Designer). `stable` not advanced (advance after the user confirms live/happy with this first pass, per the standing "refine later" framing).

### 115a. Ventures-Home — mobile search box was missing from the hamburger menu, fixed by replicating MIPS's exact shared-class structure (2026‑08‑25) — native Designer, PUBLISHED, verified live

User caught a real gap: the header's desktop search box (`if-id-search`) correctly hides at ≤991px (per the pre-existing, shared `if-id-search` breakpoint rule — confirmed unmodified, unique to no other page), but its mobile replacement never got built into Ventures' expanded nav content at all, unlike every other spinoff.

**Root cause:** MIPS/ASPIRE/Minor's real nav Components all carry a dedicated **`if-mtoprow` → `if-msearch`** pair as the first structural child of `if-navbar-in` (confirmed by reading MIPS's actual "Main Nav — MIPS" component tree, scoped via `scope_component_id`) — a small bordered, magnifying-glass-icon search box that's `display:none` on desktop and `display:block` only at the `medium` breakpoint, i.e. it only exists to fill the gap the desktop search leaves once it hides. Ventures' plain-element nav never had this pair built at all — an oversight, not a deliberate omission.

**Fix — pure structural reuse, zero new CSS.** Confirmed `if-mtoprow`/`if-msearch`/`if-id-search` are all pre-existing SHARED classes (not page-specific), already fully responsive (hide/show at the correct breakpoints, `if-msearch` styled with border/icon/padding at `medium` via its own existing rule). Built `if-mtoprow` (DivBlock) as the FIRST child of Ventures' `if-navbar-in`, containing `if-msearch` with the placeholder text "Search the Idea Factory" — matching MIPS's exact structure/attributes (`tabindex="0"`).

**⚠️ Two more instances of the already-documented "`set_text` silently no-ops on non-text-capable element types" gotcha, hit back-to-back:** (1) `data_element_builder`'s nested `set_style`+`set_attributes`+`set_text` on a fresh `DivBlock` silently dropped the text (DivBlock isn't in `set_text`'s supported type list) — confirmed via `data_element_tool.set_text` on the same element returning a hard error ("This element doesn't support text"), not just a silent no-op. (2) Rebuilding as a `TextBlock` (a supported type) still didn't apply the SAME-CALL `set_text`, and instead seeded Webflow's own default placeholder ("This is some text inside of a div block.") — fixed, per the project's own established pattern, by re-querying to find the real String child node and calling `set_text` on THAT node directly, which succeeded and was independently re-verified via a follow-up query (not trusted from the response echo alone).

**Verified the FINAL VISUAL POSITION, not just DOM presence — this caught a real misunderstanding before it was reported as done.** A first measurement showed the new search box rendering well BELOW all the nav links (top≈445px vs. the first nav link's top≈95px at 820px width), which looked wrong against the assumption that "first child in the DOM" should mean "first item shown." Investigated rather than assumed broken: `if-mtoprow` carries `order:90` and the Apply button carries `order:91` (both in the `medium` breakpoint's flex-order scheme) — high explicit CSS `order` values that intentionally push both elements to the END of the mobile menu's visual flow, regardless of DOM position; the nav links have no explicit order (default 0) and render first. **Confirmed this is MIPS's own real, live, intentional behavior** by loading MIPS-Home's actual page with its real compiled CSS and reading the same elements' computed `order` values directly (`if-msearch` order `-2` within its own row, `if-mtoprow` order `90`, `if-give-btn-solid` order `91`) — Ventures' new search box lands in exactly the same relative position (after all nav links, before the Apply button) as every other spinoff's, which is correct, not a bug.

**Published and re-confirmed on the live page:** fresh curl shows `if-mtoprow`/`if-msearch` present exactly once each in the correct position, "Search the Idea Factory" appearing twice (desktop placeholder + mobile placeholder); real-browser screenshots at 820px and 390px show a clean, correctly-bordered, correctly-icon'd search box sitting right below "RESOURCES" (the last nav link) with no overflow at either width.

No shared `idea-factory.{css,js}` change (100% reuse of pre-existing shared classes). `stable` not advanced yet.

The §114 MIPS Eligibility-table and hero-wrap fixes are published and confirmed live; the older still-open item (publishing MIPS-Apply/MIPS-Contact, task #2) may already be moot since this session republished the whole site via `publish_site` — verify against a fresh live curl before assuming it's still pending.**

## 116. ⚠️⚠️ HARD RULE — a spinoff page reusing a SHARED `if-*` class for genuinely page-specific content (not a deliberate "look family") is a live cross-site breakage risk, not a style choice. Found and fixed the real instance: MIPS's animated impact-number styling was silently editing ASPIRE's already-shipped numbers. (2026‑08‑25) — native Designer, PUBLISHED, verified

**User's report (angry, and correct):** styling the responsive breakpoints on MIPS-Home's animated "impact numbers" broke the SAME numbers on ASPIRE-Home — a page already spun off and considered done. Demanded: keep every class used on "narrow" per-page content DISCRETE per site; at minimum give every such thing a page-specific combo/sub-class so it can be edited on one page without ever touching another.

**Root cause, found by direct inspection, not guessed:** the shared, sitewide `if-countup` (the big animated number) and `if-stat-label` (its caption) — originally built for Home's own "The proof" section — were still directly attached to BOTH ASPIRE-Home's "ASPIRE by the numbers" elements AND MIPS-Home's "Four decades of return" elements. ASPIRE had already done PART of the right thing (§65-E): it layered a genuine Webflow **combo** class (`.if-countup.program-page-aspire-numbers-countup`, `.if-stat-label.program-page-aspire-numbers-label`) on top — but that combo only overrode `font-size`. Every OTHER property (`color`, `font-weight`, `letter-spacing`, `white-space`, `margin`, `border-top`, `padding-top`, `text-transform`, `font-family`) still came straight from the shared base. MIPS's number blocks had their own class (`program-page-mips-impact-num`) that DID override most properties (safe on that front) — but its labels used **bare `if-stat-label` with zero page-specific class at all**. Editing anything on `if-stat-label` (or any of the uncovered `if-countup` properties) — exactly the kind of "make it responsive" edit the user described — writes to the ONE shared style object and instantly changes Home's, ASPIRE's, and MIPS's rendering simultaneously. **This is a structural bug in how those two pages were built, not a one-off mistake in a single edit** — a combo that only overrides SOME properties still leaves every other property live-wired across every page sharing the base class.

**A second, independent bug surfaced in the same audit:** `program-page-mips-impact-num`'s own breakpoint values were non-monotonic — `main 80.6px → medium 58px → small 69px → tiny 69.0px` — sizes going back UP at `small` instead of continuing to shrink. This is very likely the visible symptom of the interrupted "styling responsive on MIPS" session itself, independent of the ASPIRE leak.

**Fix — full decoupling, not a patch.** For both ASPIRE and MIPS, replaced the shared-class dependency with a genuinely standalone class carrying the COMPLETE property set (not just size), then removed `if-countup`/`if-stat-label` from the elements entirely — no base class attached at all, so nothing on Home can ever again reach these pages, and nothing on either spinoff can reach Home or each other:
- **ASPIRE:** `remove_style` on the two old partial combos failed with the same "internal error" this project has hit before (§76/§78) — worked around with the proven `rename_style`-to-free-the-name procedure, then `create_style`'d fresh **standalone globals** (not combos — a combo only applies when the element still carries the base class, which defeats the whole point) under the SAME names (`program-page-aspire-numbers-countup`, `program-page-aspire-numbers-label`), with every property from `if-countup`/`if-stat-label` merged in alongside the existing font-size breakpoint steps. Applied `set_style` to ASPIRE's 2 number + 2 label elements dropping `if-countup`/`if-stat-label` entirely. The two old, now fully-orphaned combo objects were then successfully deleted (`remove_style` worked on retry — the earlier failure wasn't permanent this time).
- **MIPS:** added the missing `font-family` to `program-page-mips-impact-num` (it had none — an element with ONLY a program-page class and no `if-` class at all gets NO sitewide font default, since the broad font rule matches on `[class^="if-"]`; this would have silently regressed to Arial the moment `if-countup` was removed if not added explicitly) and fixed the broken `small`/`tiny` values to a clean monotonic `46px`/`42px`. Created a brand-new **`program-page-mips-impact-label`** standalone class carrying `if-stat-label`'s full property set (padding-top, border-top, color, font-weight, letter-spacing, uppercase, font-family, 14px). Applied to MIPS's 3 number + 3 label elements, dropping `if-countup`/`if-stat-label`.
- **Home** was left completely untouched — `if-countup`/`if-stat-label` still exist, unchanged, and now serve ONLY Home's own "The proof" section. Confirmed via a fresh live fetch: Home's section still renders identically (70/55.5/43/40px numbers, unchanged), and it's now the ONLY page depending on these two classes.

**Verified, not asserted:** independently re-queried every element's class list after each `set_style` (not the write's own echo) — confirmed 0 elements on ASPIRE or MIPS still carry `if-countup`/`if-stat-label` (curl'd the live published HTML: `if-countup` and `if-stat-label` both return 0 occurrences on `/aspire-home` and `/mips-home`, still 3+3 on `/`); re-fetched the fresh compiled CSS and extracted every rule block for all 4 new/fixed classes across all breakpoints — full property sets present, `program-page-mips-impact-num`'s font-size now genuinely monotonic (measured directly via `getComputedStyle` in a real browser at 7 widths: 80.6→80.6→58→46→46→42→42, no more backwards jump). Took real screenshots of Home's, ASPIRE's, and MIPS's stat sections at desktop width — all three render correctly (white bold numbers, uppercase labels with a translucent white border-top divider, ASPIRE's "900 / $1.4M," MIPS's "723 / 14,000 / $52.7B"). Published; confirmed live.

**⭐⭐ STANDING RULE, elevated to this level because it just caused real, reported cross-site damage: before reusing ANY shared `if-*` class for something a spinoff page will treat as its OWN content (a number, a stat, a data value — anything likely to be tweaked per-page later), either (a) give the element ONLY a page-specific class with the complete property set and zero dependency on the shared base, or (b) if a combo is used, the combo must cover EVERY property of the base class, not just the one that happened to need a different value at build time.** A combo that overrides only `font-size` "protects" nothing else — every other property is still one shared edit away from changing every page that reuses the base class. This is a DIFFERENT, stricter standard than the sitewide "look family" reuse this project deliberately keeps (`if-hero-*`, `if-prog-card`, `if-mani-p`, `if-eyebrow-row`, `if-stage-wrap`, nav/footer chrome, etc. — those are INTENTIONALLY shared visual systems where a look changing everywhere at once is the desired behavior, per CLAUDE.md's own §61 reuse-first practice). The line: **a shared class is fine when the THING it styles is meant to look identical everywhere by design (a button shape, a page margin, a card hover); it is NOT fine the moment the class is carrying page-specific DATA or a value someone will want to tune independently per spinoff (a stat number, a count, a measurement).** Apply this test before reusing any `if-*` class on a new spinoff page going forward, not just for count-up numbers.

**Scope note, stated plainly rather than silently narrowed:** this session fixed the SPECIFIC, reported instance (the animated impact-number family) end-to-end and confirmed no other page currently shares `if-countup`. It did **not** perform a full sitewide audit of every other `if-*` class for the same "narrow content vs. shared look" risk — that's a larger undertaking (checking every class touched by more than one spinoff against the test above) and wasn't attempted here under time pressure. If the same symptom (an edit on one spinoff changing another) recurs on a DIFFERENT class, apply this exact diagnostic (find the shared base class, check whether the page-specific override is a genuine standalone/full-coverage class or just a partial-property combo, decouple fully) rather than re-deriving the method from scratch.

**NEXT SESSION: keep maintaining this file per the OPERATING PROTOCOL. If asked to continue hardening against this class of bug, the concrete next step is a sitewide sweep — for every `if-*` class applied on more than one of Home/ASPIRE/MIPS/Minor/Ventures, check whether it's a deliberate shared-look class (fine) or is carrying page-specific content/data (fix per §116's test) — starting with anything containing "stat," "num," "count," "figure," "glance," or "impact" in a nearby class name, since that's where this exact bug lived.**
